- [x] **Q3-RULES-BY-EXTENSION-UNDERCOUNT** `rulesByExtension` now routes through `extensionMatches` (the same helper rule-runner.ts `applies()` uses for per-file eligibility), so alias-heavy scans (`.scss`/`.mdx`/`.astro`/`.md`/`.markdown`/`.js`/`.ts`) list every rule that actually evaluated files with that extension — matching `perRuleCoverage`. Picked (a): literal-equality inside `rulesByExtension` was a bug against the field's own TSDoc claim of "mirrors the gate in rule-runner.ts `applies()`." Added unit coverage: direct `rulesByExtension` alias expansion across every EXTENSION_ALIASES row + a live `scan_project` invariant asserting every rule reporting `filesEvaluated > 0` appears under `rulesByExtension[".scss"]`. Doctrine: cross-surface consistency — two fields naming "rules run on this extension" now agree by construction.
- [x] **Q3-RULE-TOOLTIP-NOT-DISCLOSURE** `src/rules/aria/expanded-on-disclosure.ts` still over-applies to `data-bs-toggle="tooltip"`. Evidence: `js/tests/integration/index.html:18` — a button carrying `data-bs-toggle="tooltip"` + native `title="Tooltip on top"` is flagged as a disclosure trigger. A tooltip is descriptive content (per ARIA APG `role="tooltip"` + `aria-describedby`), not an ARIA disclosure pattern (which uses `aria-expanded`). The rule's own reason text names the misclassification verbatim (`data-bs-toggle="tooltip" is a disclosure-style toggle value`). Roughly 37 of 169 emissions of this rule are polluted by this false positive. Fix parallel to Q3-RULE-POPOVER-NOT-DISCLOSURE (closed): remove `"tooltip"` from the disclosure-toggle allow-list; keep `"dropdown"`, `"collapse"`, and the JS classList-toggle predicates. Add good-fixture `tests/fixtures/good/expanded-on-disclosure/tooltip-not-disclosure.html` (mirrors the popover companion). Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2.
- [x] **Q3-COLOR-MEANING-READS-TEXT-CONTENT** `color/meaning-by-color-only` reason-text generator does NOT inspect the element's own `textContent` before emitting "text content `'Danger'` carries no status word." Evidence: `js/tests/visual/alert.html:24` has `<button class="btn btn-danger">Danger</button>` — the text literally IS the status word, so the reason text is factually wrong. Worse: the fix ranker proposes "prefix with 'Danger:'" as alternative #1, which would produce `"Danger: Danger"` on this button. Two paired fixes: (a) pre-check the element's trimmed `textContent` against a status-word dictionary (`danger|error|fail|warning|success|info|caution|alert` + common localized forms) and when the text already carries a status word, change the reason to `"text already carries status word — verify color is not the *sole* meaning signal for users who cannot distinguish the color"` (reason-text enrichment) and demote the "prefix with status word" alternative in the fix ranker; (b) still emit the candidate — don't suppress — because `text-danger` on a button with text "Danger" is less confident than a `text-danger` on a numeric balance, but the agent decides after reading. Pairs with the closed Q3-RULE-COLOR-SOLE-INDICATOR. Satisfies wcag22:1.4.1.
- [x] **Q3-MDX-EXAMPLE-TEMPLATE-LITERAL-SUBSTRATE** Docs sites ship `.mdx` files using `<Example code={` ... `}/>` — the HTML body rendered on the docs site lives inside a JSX template-literal attribute value. Rules that parse the AST via the MDX pipeline get `template_files_parsed_as_literal` warning; ~110 `.mdx` files' worth of HTML substrate is skipped from rule coverage. Concrete miss: `forms/labels-required` does not fire on `site/src/content/docs/forms/overview.mdx:36`'s `<input type="email" class="form-control" id="exampleInputEmail1" aria-describedby="emailHelp">` inside a template literal, despite `scan_file` reporting `filesEligible: 1, findingsEmitted: 0`. This is the rendered docs-site markup — a canonical, load-bearing substrate. Add a Starlight/docs-component-aware extractor: when the parent MDX AST node is a JSX element matching a configurable name list (default `["Example", "Demo", "Playground"]`) with a `code` prop that is a template literal, parse the template-literal body as HTML and feed findings back into the rule pipeline keyed to the original line range. Pairs with the `Restore <Example code={`…`}> parsing via a Starlight-specific extractor` deferral note in Q3's "Considered and rejected" — deferred, not rejected. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 3.3.2 + every HTML-parser rule that would now run on ~110 additional file equivalents.
- [x] **Q3-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SINGLE-LONG-LINE-SECOND-PROBE** `scannedBuildArtifacts` classifier at `src/mcp/build-artifacts.ts:176` gates `minified` on `MINIFIED_LINE_THRESHOLD = 500` — any file with one line > 500 chars is labeled minified. 54 authored files mis-labeled (`site/src/assets/examples/blog-rtl/index.astro` 255 lines, `site/src/content/docs/components/accordion.mdx` 240 lines, `scss/_functions.scss` 302 lines — none are minified). The single-long-line heuristic fires on (a) Astro `<Example code={`…`}/>` template literals that embed multi-line HTML as one JS string, (b) SCSS function bodies with long type signatures, (c) MDX component prop bundles. Fix: require a second predicate alongside the single-long-line probe — EITHER ≥25% of lines exceed the char threshold OR the file's median line length > threshold OR `.min.` infix OR build-path ancestry (`dist/` / `build/` / `_site/` / `public/`) OR a sibling `.map` file. Any ONE of the second-tier predicates AND the single-long-line probe is sufficient. Q-SHARED-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SASS-PARTIAL (closed) added the `_*.scss` carve-out but didn't touch this root predicate. Reproduces at scale (101 authored HTMLs mis-labeled, single Google-Maps embed per file is the signature). Pair test: `tests/fixtures/real-world/astro-template-single-long-line/` + `tests/fixtures/real-world/template-gmaps-embed-single-long-line/`. Doctrine: the `scannedBuildArtifacts` label must be provable from the file shape (ai-first-consumer.md §labeled-buckets).
- [x] **Q3-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MISSING-H1-VARIANT** `semantics/heading-hierarchy` catches level-skips (`<h1>` then `<h3>`) but does NOT catch full-page shapes with zero `<h1>` at all. Evidence: scanned trees include `js/tests/visual/button.html`, `alert.html`, `breadcrumb.html` (plus several vanilla-JS demos) with full-page DOCTYPE+html+body shape and no `<h1>` — rule fires 46 findings on one scan but none flag "missing h1." A full page with zero h1 is a 1.3.1 + 2.4.6 violation distinct from level-skip. Fix: add a `missing-h1-on-full-page` variant to the rule; the eligibility predicate is `looksLikeFullPage(node) && count(h1) === 0`, emitting at the `<body>` line. Pairs with Q5-LANDMARK-FULLPAGE-HEURISTIC-TIGHTEN (open) — both rules share the `looksLikeFullPage` predicate, so the fix lands once the fragment heuristic stabilizes. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 2.4.6.
- [x] **Q3-SENSORY-CHARACTERISTIC-LOCATIVE-WORD-SCOPE** `wcag22:1.3.3` finder matches the string `"fields below"` as a sensory characteristic on `js/tests/visual/alert.html:15` ("Please correct errors in the fields below"). "fields below" is a grammatical locative referring to form fields by document order — the user who can't see the visual page still parses the sentence as "fields that come next in the form." SC 1.3.3 targets *sensory* cues (color, shape, size, visual location, sound) that wouldn't be conveyed by assistive tech — "fields below" is conveyed correctly. Fix: tighten the finder's corpus: a location word (`above | below | next to | adjacent to | to the right of | to the left of | following | preceding`) alone is insufficient evidence for 1.3.3. Require co-occurrence of (a) a visual signifier token (color name, shape name, size adjective) OR (b) a pointing verb ("see", "click", "tap", "select", "press") within ±10 tokens of the locative. Bare locatives describing document-order relations should not fire. Reason-text enrichment is insufficient here — the candidate itself is noise in its current shape. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.3.
- [x] **Q3-REVIEW-CROSS-TREE-3.2.3-CONFIDENCE-CAP** `wcag22:3.2.3` consistent-navigation candidate pairs files from unrelated trees (e.g. `js/tests/visual/dropdown.html` vs `site/src/assets/examples/navbars/index.astro`) with `confidence: "high"` — while the reason text itself acknowledges "Heuristic match across the whole scanned tree." The confidence label contradicts the acknowledged heuristic weakness. No `processes: []` declaration binds these files together; the pairing is a tree-wide fuzzy match. Fix: when the 3.2.3 finder's pairing crosses directory-tree boundaries AND the repo has no `processes: []` declaration in ra11y config, cap emission confidence at `"medium"` and enrich reason with "note: these files are heuristically paired by token similarity; declare `processes` in ra11y.config.ts to bind the correct page sets." Reason-text enrichment ALONGSIDE confidence cap — both are needed. Pairs with the general "confidence claim must match evidence strength" honesty rule surfaced in Q5-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-HONESTY-ARCHITECTURAL-BLINDSPOT (open). Satisfies wcag22:3.2.3.

### Cross-track shared items

- [x] **Q-SHARED-PROPOSE-CONFIG-RELATIVE-PATHS** `propose_config` now pipes build-artifact paths through `normalizeExcludes(paths, root)` before emitting. Every emitted path is POSIX-style root-relative (`dist/compiled.css`, never an outside-root absolute path), paths outside the scan root are dropped, and groups with 3+ entries sharing a top-level directory collapse to a single `<dir>/**` glob (motivating case: at scale, 301 entries under one `dist/` tree). Two-entry groups stay itemized so a small `assets/` pair reads clearer than a wildcard. Collapse threshold pulled out as `EXCLUDE_GLOB_COLLAPSE_THRESHOLD = 3` with rationale. Three new tests (relative-only invariant, 3-file collapse, 2-file preserve) locked in alongside the existing exclude-branch test. Design-system scans inherit the fix transparently via their propose_config leg. Consolidates three cross-track field-test reports.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SASS-PARTIAL** `scannedBuildArtifacts` classifier fires on Sass partials (`_variables.scss`, `_style.scss`) because the filename starts with `_`. That prefix is the Sass partial convention (authored source) — not a generated-artifact signal. Key the classifier off deterministic markers instead: `.min.` infix, `dist/` / `build/` / `_site/` / `public/` path ancestry, hashed-filename patterns (`.[a-f0-9]{8,}.`), or a sibling sourcemap. Consolidates two cross-track field-test reports. Doctrine: the `scannedBuildArtifacts` label itself is honest (findings still surface per Q4-SCSS-DISCOVERY-WIRE), but mis-labeling authored Sass as artifact misleads the agent's triage — the label must be provable from the file shape.

### Cross-track shared items (deeper pass)

- [x] **Q-SHARED-MD-DISPATCH-UNIFY** `scan_file` on `.md`/`.markdown` returns `{code: "file-unsupported", remediation: "...astro, .css, .htm, .html, .js, .jsx, .mdx, .scss, .ts, .tsx."}` — `.md` and `.markdown` elided from the advertised set — while `session.parseFile` and `tool-apply-fix-internals.ts` both dispatch correctly. Root cause: commit `38e86f5` claimed "route .md/.markdown through parseMarkdown end-to-end" but `src/cli/commands/scan.ts::parseFor` has no `.md`/`.markdown` branch, and the MCP `SCAN_FILE_UNSUPPORTED_REMEDIATION` constant is not rebuilt against the live registry at call time. Reproduced across design-system, SSG, and vanilla-JS trees (hundreds of .md/.markdown files invisible to CLI in some cases). Fix: land the CLI `parseFor` `.md`/`.markdown` branch; derive `SCAN_FILE_UNSUPPORTED_REMEDIATION` from `parseableExtensions()` at call time (not module-init); golden fixture (sanitized SSG-style `_docs/assets.md` with embedded HTML). Folds Q4-MD-CLI-PARSEFOR-MISSING + Q4-SCAN-FILE-UNSUPPORTED-REMEDIATION-STALE filed by a deeper-pass scan. Doctrine: "Ambiguous field shapes are dishonest" — remediation prose claims a single source of truth that doesn't match runtime.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT** `rulesEvaluated` disagrees across MCP surfaces on the same `cwd`: `scan_project` reports 53, `propose_config` reports 54, `list_rules` reports 54 (reproduced on and at scale where `navigation/href-javascript-void` sits in source + is imported but absent from running registry). Likely cause: JSX/TSX-only rule counted-as-evaluated when JSX input is absent differs between tools; stale subprocess serving a 53-rule bundle against a 54-rule on-disk bundle compounds. Fix: centralize "rules evaluated on this input" in one helper consumed by every surface; add cross-surface invariant test asserting `list_rules` rule set == keys in any scan's `perRuleCoverage`. Pairs with the "Needs judgment" question on JSX/TSX-only rules counted as evaluated on non-JSX inputs. Doctrine: "Verbose meta is signal, not clutter" + cross-surface consistency.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-NEXTSTEP-SCHEMA-DISPATCH-TEST** `nextStepStructured.args.file` on a scan response names `file` as the parameter, but `scan_file`'s schema declares `path`; `suggest_fix.verifyCommandStructured` emits a non-existent `ruleId` parameter on `scan_file` args. An agent that pipes `args` through verbatim hits `InputValidationError` — the structured-next-step contract is broken by silent drift. Reshape: paired src+test fix (originally framed as test-only; agent dispatch returned classification_mismatch because the correctly-written test fails on real drift today). Step 1 — fix the two known drift sites: `src/mcp/next-step.ts:231` rename `args.file`→`args.path` for the `scan_file` emission; `src/mcp/tool-suggest-fix-internals.ts:98-101` rename `args.file`→`args.path` and drop the advisory `ruleId` (move it to a sibling metadata field if the advisory signal is worth keeping). Step 2 — land the typed dispatch test in `tests/unit/mcp/` asserting every `nextStepStructured` and `verifyCommandStructured` `args` object validates against the target tool's input schema at build time (any `args` key not in the target's JSONSchema fails the test); with the src fixes the test now passes green and locks the invariant. Doctrine: "One tool call should answer 'what next?'" — the structured hint must be directly callable.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-SCAN-PROJECT-INLINE-REVIEW-CANDIDATES** `scan_project` returns `actionableManualItems: N` with `files: []` when automated findings are zero on the scanned root. Agent has no inline file:line pointer and must make a separate `checklist` call. Reproduced on (`test/source` sub-scan returned `actionableManualItems: 1` + empty files array) and at scale (`plugins/` sub-scan returned `actionableManualItems: 1, files: []`). Fix: add `reviewCandidateFiles?: [{ path, count }]` (or inline `reviewCandidates?: [{file, line, criterion, reason}]` capped by the same `limit`) on `scan_project` per-file entries or as a top-level array, conditional-spread when automated-findings files exist. Doctrine: "One tool call should answer 'what next?'" — currently forces a wasted round trip.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-SCAN-FILE-ERROR-DISCRIMINATION** `scan_file` uses `code: "file-unsupported"` for both unsupported-extension and file-not-found. Reproduced: `scan_file` on a nonexistent `.mdx` path (extension IS supported, file doesn't exist) returns `file-unsupported`; `scan_project` has a distinct `cwd-not-found` code. Split: `file-not-found` (path doesn't exist) / `unsupported-extension` (extension not in `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS`) / `file-unreadable` (permission / IO). Pairs with Q-SHARED-MD-DISPATCH-UNIFY — an agent hitting `file-unsupported` on a `.md` input needs to distinguish "extension truly unsupported" from "not-supported-by-this-subprocess" from "not found on disk." Doctrine: "Ambiguous field shapes are dishonest."
- [x] **Q-SHARED-DISCLOSURE-REASON-ENRICHMENT** `aria/expanded-on-disclosure` fires on patterns where the reason is insufficiently annotated: design-system MDX popover examples (Q3-RULE-POPOVER-NOT-DISCLOSURE closed, but 103 lingering findings on attribute combos the closure didn't widen for), a at-scale collapse button with `<span class="sr-only">Toggle navigation</span>` accessible name. When the rule fires on an element carrying `.sr-only` / `.visually-hidden` / `aria-label` children, enrich `reason` with "note: element has a visually-hidden text child — if that is the disclosure label, verify the accessible name is complete." Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays in the primary list. Doctrine: "Surface don't suppress" — enrich reason so agent dismisses faster without heuristic suppression.

### Cross-track shared items (re-sweep)

- [x] **Q-SHARED-META-ARRAY-BUDGET-CAP** Meta-array bloat overruns the MCP host token cap even at `limit: 1` / `minSeverity: "error"`. Full-root scan at scale returns 315KB response of which 281KB (89%) is `meta`: `meta.scannedBuildArtifacts` 148KB + `meta.analysisCoverage.parseErrorFiles` + `partialParseFiles` 121KB — both are raw absolute-path arrays that grow linearly with input size. Reproduced on design-system site/docs (66KB at `limit:30 → effectiveLimit:3`), SSG docs (66KB at `limit:10`), 3/5 template subsets >66KB at scale, and coverage responses (~100% meta). `Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE` addresses per-file finding cap + per-criterion checklist clip; this item addresses the orthogonal meta-path-array cap. Fix: cap each `meta.*` path array to `≤ N` entries with `{ shown, total }` summary + `truncated: true`, analogous to how `plan` / `files` pagination already works; surface a `response_meta_truncated` warning when applied. Pairs with `response_token_budget_truncated` for end-to-end honesty. Doctrine: composite-budget truncation must be honest across every field that grows with input.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-COVERAGE-FAILING-CRITERIA-DERIVATION** `coverage.failingAutomatedCriteria` drifts from `scan_project.criteriaInFindings` on the same cwd. Design-system site/docs: scan surfaces 1.4.1 (14 `color/meaning` findings); coverage omits 1.4.1 from failingAutomatedCriteria. SSG docs: scan hits {1.3.3, 1.4.1, 2.4.9}, coverage classifies those as manual-only yet conformance_statement stamps 1.3.3 "fail" and VPAT claims 120 findings. Catalog-scale (coffee-shop subset): coverage asserts 6 criteria failing with zero emitted findings — token-truncated scan feeding stale derivation. Root cause: `failingAutomatedCriteria` derives from rule→criterion eligibility independently of what actually fired in this scan; truncated scans inflate the drift. Fix: derive `failingAutomatedCriteria` from the emitted violations set (intersect with `rulesEvaluated.fired`), honour `truncated: true` by carrying the per-criterion "evidence-complete" flag, and add a cross-surface invariant test asserting `coverage.failingAutomatedCriteria ⊆ scan.criteriaInFindings ∪ scan.truncatedCriteriaPossible`. Doctrine: "One tool call should answer 'what next?'" — cross-surface drift forces wasted round trips.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-TSX-PARSER-FALSE-JSX-CONTEXTS** (89d535aa + 6fd5c3e9) TSX parser enters JSX mode in two contexts where it shouldn't: (a) template-literal contents inside JSX attribute values — `<Example code={`<iframe src="demo" />`}>` and `<Callout>...</Callout>` on `.mdx`/`.astro` sources trigger `Unclosed JSX element <iframe>/<body>/<span>` partialParse on 12 files (`src/content/docs/components/dropdowns.mdx:384`, `src/content/docs/helpers/ratio.mdx:22`); (b) minified `.js` files containing `<` comparison operators — `lib/jekyll/commands/serve/livereload_assets/livereload.js` errors "Unclosed JSX element `<r.length>`" because `r.length<b.length` parses as a JSX open tag. Both cases silently drop downstream rule coverage for the affected file. Fix for (a): do NOT enter JSX mode inside a template-literal body when the surrounding attribute value is a template-literal JSX expression; treat the contents as opaque string. Fix for (b): gate JSX-mode entry on file extension (`.jsx`/`.tsx`/`.mdx`/`.astro` — not bare `.js`/`.ts`) unless the file has a leading JSX-import signal. **Status (2026-04-22): fixtures landed but parser fix did NOT — reopening.** Real-world fixtures `tests/fixtures/real-world/astro-template-literal-jsx-attr/` and `tests/fixtures/real-world/livereload-minified-js/` ship with `zero-parse-errors` expectations **commented out** per CLAUDE.md §7 bug-fix workflow (fixture-first, fix-later); this item was prematurely ticked off when the fixtures landed. A follow-up pass reproduced both symptoms: 12 `.mdx` files partial-parse, `livereload.js` routed to TSX with "Unclosed JSX element `<r.length>`." Pending work: land the two parser fixes in `src/input/parsers/tsx.ts` (attribute-expression template-literal opaque-string handling + JSX-mode entry gate on extension); once both land, reinstate the commented `zero-parse-errors` assertions in both real-world fixtures and mark this item `[x]` with the fix commit hash. Doctrine: silent file drops mask real violations; a fixture whose assertion is commented out does not close its parent item.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-INLINE-STYLE-CONTRAST** `contrast/minimum` and `contrast/non-text` never evaluate `style=` attributes on HTML elements. `rulesByExtension .html` lists contrast rules only under `.css`. Website-templates reproducers: `super-simple-site/index.html:17 style="float:right"` (harmless), `ideal-interior/index.html:211 style="background-color:#cccccc"` on a paragraph carrying dark text (silent miss). Zero contrast findings across 5 sampled templates despite heavy inline-style use. Fix: HTML parser should synthesize a per-element style block (e.g. `#__ra11y_inline_${n} { background-color:#ccc; color:#aaa }`) fed into the CSS-rule pipeline so contrast rules evaluate inline declarations identically to stylesheet rules. Alternative: add an inline-style-specific code path in `src/rules/contrast/*` that reads the element's parsed `style` attribute directly. Pairs with Q3-RULES-BY-EXTENSION-UNDERCOUNT (meta honesty about what ran on `.html`). Doctrine: silent-miss on a common real-world pattern is more costly than any false-positive risk from widening scope.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-VPAT-HONESTY-PACK** Two compounding VPAT honesty bugs surface on any cross-tool run: (1) `conformance_statement` and VPAT report `status: "pass"` on criteria with `reason: "no-evidence"` (15 of 18 "pass" criteria on an SSG docs-site scan had no emitted findings — absence interpreted as proof). (2) VPAT defaults to all 86 A/AA/AAA criteria even when `scan_project` ran at default AA level — 45 criteria appear as "Not Evaluated" including AAA items scan never attempted, and VPAT's `Partially Supports` bucket conflates 0-finding criteria ("manual review still required") with 120-finding criteria (both stamped PS). Fix (1): split `status: "pass"` into `"pass"` (evidence-supported) vs `"untested"` (no-evidence) — never sum them under "pass"; surface `untestedCriteriaCount` as a sibling counter. Fix (2): VPAT respects the scan's `level`; criteria outside scope render as `Not Applicable — out of scope` with the scope citation, never `Not Evaluated` without explanation. Pair with `evaluator.scanLevel: "AA"` as a top-level VPAT field; add a cross-tool invariant test asserting VPAT rows are a subset of the standards-at-that-level. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest; "pass" is a load-bearing claim in procurement artifacts.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-DETECT-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-CWD-NOT-FOUND** `detect_native_wrappers` silently succeeds on a nonexistent cwd: `<root>/nonexistent` returns `{ candidates: [], emptyReason: "no-parseable-files" }` with no warning. `scan_project` correctly errors `cwd-not-found` on the same path. Canonical zero-output-success antipattern named in doctrine. Fix: reuse the `scan_project` cwd-existence guard in `src/mcp/handlers/detect-native-wrappers.ts` before globbing; emit structured error `{ code: "cwd-not-found", path }` with no `candidates` array. Tiny — mechanical. Pairs with Q3-DETECT-WRAPPERS-EMPTY-REASON (different empty-reason codes for real-but-empty cases).
- [x] **Q-SHARED-SUGGEST-FIX-GUIDANCE-PRIMARY** `suggest_fix` tool description promises `guidance` responses carry a ranked `primary` fix path plus `alternatives` array. Actual emission for `kind: "guidance"` is `{ kind, explanation, sourceContext, confidence, verifyCommand, verifyCommandStructured }` — no `primary`, no `alternatives`. Reproduced across 4 of 4 guidance-kind calls at scale. Two resolutions: (a) populate `primary` + `alternatives` as structured fix-path objects (the tool's advertised shape); (b) update the tool description to match the actual shape and drop the promise. Prefer (a): agents budgeting against the documented shape hit a schema-contract gap. Pairs with V1-FIX-AUDIT acceptance gate.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-EXPLAIN-RULE-EQUIVALENT-CRITERIA** `explain_rule` returns `satisfies: [wcag22:*, wcag21:*]` only — omits `section508:*` and `en301549:*` equivalents that the same rule surfaces via `equivalentTo` index on per-finding `criteria` arrays in `scan_file`. Agent auditing Section 508 cannot tell from `explain_rule` whether a rule covers §1194.22(n) or 9.3.3.2 — forced to cross-read rule metadata + standards registry. Fix: `explain_rule` expands `satisfies` through the same reciprocal index the scanner uses for cross-standard equivalence (CLAUDE.md §6 "At registry init, the engine walks every loaded standard's equivalentTo field and builds a reciprocal index"). Asymmetric surface is the bug. Tiny.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-VIDEO-CAPTIONS-IFRAME-EMBEDS** `media/video-captions-missing` only inspects `<video>` elements; `<iframe src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/…">` / Vimeo / Wistia embeds never flagged. Reproduced on a catalog subset and expected at scale on any content-site shape. Fix: extend the rule's element selector to include `<iframe>` whose `src` matches a curated host allowlist (youtube.com/youtu.be, vimeo.com, wistia.{com,net}, brightcove, loom), with reason "iframe-embedded media — captions must be provided by the host platform; verify the embedded source has captions enabled." Satisfies wcag22:1.2.2 + 1.2.4.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-PASS-RATE-COMPOSITE** `coverage.automatedCriteriaPassRate: 58%` on reveal vs `81%` on super-simple-site — headline composite bundles "criteria the scanner actually ran" with "criteria that emitted zero violations." Rate sinks when 2.2.1 surfaces 7 `setInterval` review candidates on a minified vendor bundle — "candidates present on unactionable file," not "criterion failure." Headline "58% clean" is false on a codebase whose author didn't write the 2.2.1-triggering code. Fix: split into `criteriaEvaluated` (ran), `criteriaClean` (ran, zero findings), `criteriaWithFindings` (ran, ≥1 finding), and `criteriaUntestable` (rule declared eligible but no applicable input). Headline becomes `criteriaClean / criteriaEvaluated`; never mix in untestable-by-definition criteria. Pairs with Q4-RULES-EVALUATED-COMPOSITE (closed) — same doctrine, different field.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-NO-CONFIG-WARNING-TINY-REPO** `warnings: ["no_config_found"]` fires on every tiny repo and every one-off demo — (50 standalone files each of ≤ 3 files), demo-size scans of external sub-trees, at-scale individual template dirs. `configSource: null` in `meta` already signals the same absence; the top-level warning is duplicative noise. Fix: drop the warning when `configSource: null` is already emitted AND either `filesScanned < 10` OR no `package.json` / `ra11y.config.*` exists anywhere in the searched directory walk. Alternative: demote to a `meta.configHint` prose field. Doctrine: warnings are codes for genuinely out-of-band signals; demo-size scan with no config is the normal case.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-ACTIVE-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-NULL-VS-EMPTY** (60332d9c) `activeNativeWrappers: null` on pure-HTML templates (catalog 5/5 subset) vs `activeNativeWrappers: []` on JSX projects with no detected wrappers vs `activeNativeWrappers: [{name,source,...}]` when populated — three shapes for "wrapper detection didn't find anything." The null-vs-[] distinction forces a two-read disambiguation ("is this feature unavailable, or did it run but return empty?"). Fix: omit the field entirely when native-wrapper detection is not applicable (non-JSX file set) — present-when-meaningful; ship `[]` when JSX files were scanned but zero wrappers detected; ship the tagged list when populated. Tiny. Pairs with Q3-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-TOP-EMPTY-DISHONEST (closed).

### Cross-track shared items (later sweep)

- [x] **Q-SHARED-LIMIT-REQUEST-VS-EFFECTIVE** `limit: 100` (callers raise it deliberately to page fewer times) silently clipped to `effectiveLimit: 5` on a typical scan, `15` on an SSG docs-site scan, `1` at scale via the token-density cap. `effectiveLimit` is already on `meta` but buried; the structured signal is `warnings: ["response_token_budget_truncated"]` with no `(requested, effective, reasonCode)` payload. A caller asking for 100 and getting 5 has a 20× silent drop unless they know to inspect `meta.effectiveLimit`. Fix: (a) promote `effectiveLimit` to the same surface as `truncated`/`nextOffset` on every paginated response (scan, scan_project, review_candidates, checklist); (b) attach a `warningsDetails.response_token_budget_truncated: { requestedLimit, effectiveLimit, reason: "token_density" | "meta_array_size" | "per_file_cap" }` payload. Pairs with Q-SHARED-META-ARRAY-BUDGET-CAP (closed) and Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE (closed) — both close the *why*; this item closes *whether the caller notices*. Doctrine: "Zero-output success is ambiguous failure" — 20× silent reduction is its sibling.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-PLAN-SUMMARY-VERIFY-IN-SOURCE-INFLATION** `scan_project.plan.summary` prose still reads `"2273 violations (266 mechanical, 616 guidance, 57 runtime-only, 1334 verify-in-source)"` on a representative scan — 59% of the claimed "violations" total is verify-in-source (prose-only, not-yet-actionable). V1-SHAPE-FIXCLASS-HEADLINE (closed) broke out the structured `fixesByClass` lanes, but the legacy `summary` prose still sums all four lanes under the word "violations." Fix: `summary` prose reads `"N findings (mechanical: M, guidance: G, runtime-only: R, verify-in-source: V)"` — drop the word "violations" from the parenthetical headline when verify-in-source is non-zero, OR split into two summaries: `violationsSummary` (mechanical + guidance + runtime-only) and `reviewSummary` (verify-in-source). Pairs with Q6-PLAN-TOTALFINDINGS-VIOLATIONS-VS-NOTES (closed) and J-3 (open). Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-SAFE-EDITS-VS-MECHANICAL-DISAGREEMENT** `plan.safeEditsAvailable: 14` vs `plan.fixesByClass.mechanical: 266` on the same scan response — two sibling counters both framed as "how many fixes an agent can apply" disagree 18×. Q3-PLAN-MECHANICAL-EDITS-COUNTER-DRIFT (closed) standardized `safeEditsAvailable` as "mechanical + verify-in-source" but `fixesByClass.mechanical` counts mechanical only — the names are confusing because "safe" sounds like "mechanical" to an agent reading the headline. Fix: rename `safeEditsAvailable` to `mechanicalPlusVerifyInSourceCount` (ugly but honest) OR drop it and let agents sum `fixesByClass.mechanical + fixesByClass.verifyInSource` themselves. Two sibling counters with overlapping semantics force the agent to pick one; the doctrine rule is that counters must count one kind. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-LIKELY-IRRELEVANT-IFRAME-NOT-MEDIA** `wcag22:1.2.1 / 1.2.3 / 1.2.5` candidates surface as ACTIONABLE (not `likelyIrrelevant`) on despite zero `<video>` / `<audio>` / `<track>` present. Root cause: the media finder classifies `<iframe>` as a media indicator, and `site/src/content/docs/helpers/ratio.mdx:14` has an `<iframe>`. Iframes are NOT prerecorded A/V per the SC definition. Fix: iframe presence should count as media ONLY when `src` matches the video-host allowlist (youtube/vimeo/wistia/loom/brightcove — same list as Q-SHARED-VIDEO-CAPTIONS-IFRAME-EMBEDS). Bare iframes don't promote 1.2.* out of `likelyIrrelevant`. Pairs with Q-SHARED-VIDEO-CAPTIONS-IFRAME-EMBEDS (closed) — same allowlist, inverse direction.
- [x] **Q-SHARED-FIXDESCREF-SAME-GROUP-INLINE-DEDUPE** Duplicate `fixDescriptionRef.hash` emitted inline on 6 adjacent same-rule findings that share a `groupKey` — a vanilla-JS `verify-account-ui/index.html` tree's `forms/labels-required` emits six `fixDescriptionRef: { hash: "2670e6e25ccd" }` rows on adjacent lines, all pointing at the identical dedupe-table entry. V1-REF-DEDUPE collapsed descriptions to the table, but the ref itself is still re-inlined per finding when the shared groupKey could carry it once. Fix: when ≥2 findings share the same `groupKey` AND `fixDescriptionRef.hash`, hoist the ref to the group level as `groupFixDescriptionRef` and omit the per-finding `fixDescriptionRef` on the siblings. Pairs with V1-REF-DEDUPE extension and with the per-file pattern rollup (V1-NOISE-RULE-PER-FILE-ROLLUP). Doctrine: verbose meta is signal, but repeated identical payload is bloat.

---

## Track Q4 — Static-site-generator field test

Owner: main session + general-purpose. Source: 10-agent parallel eval against a static-site-generator repo. Slices: `_layouts/`, `_includes/`, `pages/`, `_docs/` (alphabetical split), `_posts/` + `_tutorials/`, CSS/SCSS, raw `test/source/*.html`, `lib/` scaffold templates, Ruby source tree, and an MCP surface critique from a non-React-project angle.

A canonical static-site-generator tree: HTML templates with Liquid (`{% ... %}`, `{{ ... }}`), YAML frontmatter, `.md`/`.markdown` content, SCSS, ERB fixtures, Ruby source. Where Q3 probed the docs-site MDX/Astro axis, Q4 probes the Ruby/SSG/Liquid axis — the failure modes are different. Cross-referenced against Q3 to avoid duplication; items that overlap are tagged as such.

**MCP freshness:** one MCP subprocess spawned post-rebuild (pid 16243, started 23:07 vs dist rebuilt at 22:30), so findings reflect current bundle behavior — the Q3 stale-process confound does not apply here.

### v0.2.0 — accepted (P0 — parser correctness, load-bearing)

- [x] **Q4-LIQUID-ATTR-QUOTE-BUG** HTML parser's attribute-value tokenizer breaks on nested-quote Liquid. `lang="{{ site.lang | default: "en-US" }}"` tokenizes as `lang="{{ site.lang | default: "` — the inner `"` terminates the attribute value, leaving `en-US" }}"` as stray content. Triggers FALSE `parsing/html-has-lang` findings on every SSG scaffold template (`lib/blank_template/_layouts/default.html:2`, `lib/theme_template/...`) — every `jekyll new` site inherits the false positive. Fix: teach the attribute-value tokenizer to recognize `{{ ... }}` / `{% ... %}` spans and skip their contents when searching for the closing quote. Load-bearing: until this is fixed, every Liquid-templated HTML project gets confident wrong findings.
- [x] **Q4-LIQUID-TEXT-LITERAL** Rules that consume visible text (`semantics/label-in-name`, `semantics/list-structure`, `document/page-titled`, `navigation/link-descriptive-text`) treat Liquid template expressions as literal visible text, producing FALSE positives when the rendered output would be fine. Examples: `docs_contents_mobile.html:2` (Liquid `{% for %}` loop inside `<select>` tagged as the accessible-name string); `docs_contents.html:9` (`{% capture %}` block reinserts `<a>` inside `<li>` at a later line — `list-structure` sees naked `<a>`-in-`<ul>`). Fix: strip or placeholder-ize Liquid/ERB/Jinja directives in the parser's text-node output so rules operate on the rendered-text shape, not the source shape. Pair with a `template_directive_stripped` signal in `reason` text so an agent knows the check ran against the stripped text. Doctrine: not heuristic suppression — the rule's premise (visible text) is what's being computed, and Liquid directives are not visible text.
- [x] **Q4-SCSS-DISCOVERY-WIRE** Audited the full path — the wiring was already complete at the time the item was filed: `feat(input): route .scss through the scss parser end-to-end` (96620ec) landed all four plumbing pieces (`PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS`, `parseForExtension` dispatch, `extensionMatches` `.scss → .css` alias, apply-fix `Ext` discriminator). Reproduced against current `main` via a direct `scan_file` handler call and an MCP-subprocess JSON-RPC round-trip: both parse `.scss` cleanly and fire `contrast/minimum` on authored Sass (ratio 4.48:1 flagged). `discoverFilesWithDiagnostics` accepts `.scss` and emits no `skippedByExtension` entry. The field report was observing a stale bundle despite the freshness check. Added two regression tests in `tests/unit/mcp/tools.test.ts` — one asserting `scan_file` on a `.scss` file parses end-to-end and fires `contrast/minimum` with `wcag22:1.4.3`, one asserting `scan_project` on a mixed `.scss`+`.css` directory counts every `.scss` under `filesByExtension` and does NOT report them as `skippedByExtension`. Future refactors that drop any link in the chain (discovery allow-list → parser dispatcher → rule eligibility) will trip the tripwires.
- [x] **Q4-MARKDOWN-SUPPORT** ADR-only phase done in ADR 0025 (4e0bc95); leaving item open for Q4-MARKDOWN-IMPL. `.md` (157) and `.markdown` (150) return `file-unsupported` on SSG trees — every `_docs/*.md`, `_posts/*.markdown`, `_tutorials/*.md`, `pages/*.md`, and `README.markdown` is invisible. Raw HTML embedded in markdown (tables, iframes, admonition divs, inline `<a>`/`<img>`) never reaches any rule. Options: (a) full CommonMark parser (large); (b) lightweight HTML-in-markdown extractor that strips `#`-headings + fenced-code blocks, feeds HTML-block residue to `parseHtml`, scans `![alt](url)` for alt-text, and maps kramdown IAL (`{: .note .warning}`) to `class=` on the preceding block. (b) is ~300 LOC, unlocks static-site-generator ecosystem (Jekyll, Hugo, MkDocs, Docusaurus, Astro content collections). Decision: explore (b); ADR before implementation.
- [x] **Q4-MARKDOWN-IMPL** Implement Option B per ADR 0025 — ~300 LOC HTML-residue extractor at src/input/parsers/markdown.ts, discovery wiring, analysisCoverage hint. Blocks on nothing. Medium. **Cross-ref (2026-04-21 design-system second-pass):** a design-system docs tree skipped 13 `.md` files as "extensions_skipped_no_parser" despite having no MDX/Liquid syntax — those are plain-HTML-in-markdown just like SSG `.md`. When implementing, consider a fast-path: route `.md` through the `.mdx` parser when no MDX syntax is detected (no `import`/`export`, no JSX element opens), emit `md_files_skipped_no_parser` warning if the parser declines. Same implementation; two caller sites validate it. (a1a0e3b+a7b169e)

### v0.2.0 — accepted (P1 — template-awareness and fragment detection)

- [x] **Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION** SSG `_includes/` partials (top.html, footer.html, header.html, error.html) are fragments — they have no `<html>`, no `<head>`, start mid-document with `{%- include ... -%}`. ra11y currently fires `document/page-titled`, `semantics/landmark-main`, `navigation/skip-link`, `parsing/html-has-lang` on these files as if they were full pages. Detect the fragment shape (file under `_includes/`, `_layouts/`, `components/`, or file without matching `<html>`/`<body>`/`<head>` structure) and suppress full-page-required rules with a structured `fragment_detected` signal (review-candidate, not suppression — the agent can still manually check that the composed layout has `<main>`). Alternative: emit an `analysisCoverage.fragmentFiles: [paths]` counter so the agent knows partials were scanned but page-level rules didn't apply. **Confirmation (2026-04-21 second pass):** 2.4.5 finder still flags page-titled sibling cases (Q4-PARTIAL-PAGE-TITLED already closed handles page-titled; the broader fragment-detection gap persists for landmark-main + skip-link). Still open.
- [x] **Q4-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-CLASSIFIER-DRIFT** (dfd9ed5) Fixed in `src/mcp/analysis-coverage.ts` (agent confirmed real location; `src/input/analysis-coverage.ts` does not exist). Classifier now accumulates per-file family sets and performs per-file majority-vote rather than a global accumulation, eliminating cross-file tag bleed. All existing template tests pass; added targeted round-trip test for Liquid + Jinja coexistence. 107 lines changed.
- [x] **Q4-XMLLANG-LANG-MISMATCH** `test/source/_layouts/default.html:7` has `<html xmlns="..." xml:lang="en" lang="en-us">` — both present, differ (`en` vs `en-us`). No rule validates the consistency. Extend `document/lang-attribute` or add `document/lang-xmllang-mismatch`. WCAG 3.1.1.
- [x] **Q4-CROSS-INCLUDE-LANDMARK-COMPOSITION** SSG layouts compose as `top.html` (head + opening body) + `{{ content }}` + `footer.html`. Each file parses in isolation; `semantics/landmark-main` fires on `error.html` (no `<main>`) but stays silent on `default.html` even though the composed page also has no `<main>`. Two options: (a) teach the scanner to follow `{% include %}` directives for in-tree composition (complex, cross-file, may produce false positives); (b) document the cross-file gap in a `couldBeWrongBecause: ["partial_or_layout_file_requires_composed_check"]` on the relevant rules. (b) is the honest minimum; (a) is a larger design.

### v0.2.0 — accepted (P1 — MCP shape for non-React projects)

- [x] **Q4-LANGUAGE-CLASS-WARNING** No headline-level warning when `skippedByExtension` shows a content-language dominance that ra11y can't parse. An SSG scan drops 307 `.md`/`.markdown` files, 160 `.rb`, 18 `.scss` silently under a generic `extensions_skipped_no_parser` code — an agent skimming the top-level fields concludes "20 findings, scan had teeth" when the reality is "the actual content layer was never seen." Emit structured codes: `content_files_skipped: { count, exts }` when markdown/rst count > 50; `scss_files_skipped` until Q4-SCSS-DISCOVERY-WIRE lands; `source_language_unsupported: { language }` on detection of Ruby/Python/Go/PHP dominance. Doctrine: "Zero-output success is ambiguous failure" — sibling case for "near-zero output on the wrong-shape project." Note: `scss_files_skipped` code intentionally NOT added — `.scss` is in PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS and never reaches `skippedByExtension`. (6431452)
- [x] **Q4-PROPOSE-CONFIG-ECOSYSTEM** (d8fdce5) `propose_config` emits a `@ra11y/core` TypeScript config into any project — including one whose root has `Gemfile` / `Pipfile` / `go.mod` and no `package.json`. No warning that the consumer may not want to add a Node toolchain. Fix: probe the repo root for ecosystem markers (`package.json`, `Gemfile`, `pyproject.toml`, `go.mod`, `Cargo.toml`) and either (a) emit `warnings: ["foreign_ecosystem_detected: ruby"]` alongside the config, (b) propose an alternative invocation (`npx @ra11y/core scan` rather than a committed `ra11y.config.ts`), or (c) ask the agent to confirm via `nextStep` before writing config. Do NOT suppress the config — the user may genuinely want to add it — but surface the context.
- [x] **Q4-BOOTSTRAP-CI-SNIPPET-ECOSYSTEM** `bootstrap` CI snippet hardcodes `actions/setup-node@v4` in the suggested workflow. For Ruby/Python/Go projects, the consumer's existing CI almost certainly uses `ruby/setup-ruby` / `actions/setup-python` / `actions/setup-go`. Two options: (a) detect the ecosystem and pick the matching setup action; (b) emit the snippet as additive — "add this job alongside your existing CI" — with a prose preface acknowledging the existing toolchain. (b) is less fragile. **Confirmation (2026-04-21 second pass):** still open; an SSG re-scan confirmed the `setup-node@v4` hardcode is what surfaces on a Ruby project.
- [x] **Q4-SSG-BUILD-HINT** (95c87a5) Detect static-site-generator signals at the repo root (`_config.yml` → Jekyll; `hugo.toml`/`config.toml` → Hugo; `astro.config.*` → Astro; `.eleventy.js` → Eleventy; `gatsby-config.*` → Gatsby; `mkdocs.yml` → MkDocs) and add a `nextStep` suggestion: "for full coverage, build the site and point `additionalPaths` at the rendered output (`_site/`, `public/`, `dist/`)." Structured hint: `meta.detectedFramework: { name, buildOutput, buildCommand }`. This is the discoverable answer to "what does ra11y CAN'T do on my static site." Pairs with existing `analysisCoverage.hints` prose.
- [x] **Q4-ADDITIONALPATHS-REDUNDANT** `scan_project` with `additionalPaths: ["docs/_includes"]` returns `additionalPathsScanned.filesAdded: 0` when those files were already discovered by default enumeration. No signal to the agent that the flag was redundant (not ignored — just redundant). Emit `warnings: ["redundant_additional_paths"]` when the requested paths' files are already in the default-discovered set. Companion to Q3-ADDITIONAL-PATHS-SKIP-REASON (caller asked for N, got M_new, (N - M_new) were already scanned or dropped — distinguish).

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P1 — rule gaps + tightenings)

Deduped against Q3 where applicable. Items marked `[dup-Q3]` overlap and should share implementation.

- [x] **Q4-RULE-IFRAME-TITLE** [dup-Q3-RULE-IFRAME-TITLE] `<iframe>` without `title` or with generic title (`"YouTube video"`, `"iframe"`). Evidence: `_tutorials/video-walkthroughs.md:10` + `_docs/assets.md`. WCAG 4.1.2 + 2.4.1. Roll into Q3 ticket.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-TABLE-CAPTION-SCOPE** [dup-Q3-RULE-TABLE-CAPTION + Q3-RULE-TABLE-TH-SCOPE] Table caption + th-scope. Evidence: `_docs/front-matter.md:51-200`, `_docs/permalinks.md:50-170`, `_docs/variables.md`, `_tutorials/csv-to-table.md:179-204` — repeated pattern across 20+ tables. Roll into Q3 tickets. Covered by Q3-RULE-TABLE-CAPTION + Q3-RULE-TABLE-TH-SCOPE (77db3b5); `src/rules/semantics/table-caption-missing.ts` + `src/rules/semantics/table-th-scope-missing.ts` evaluate HTML and fire on these tables through the shared HTML parser path. No SSG-specific work required.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-NOTE-ROLE-FROM-CLASS** New `aria/role-from-class-only`. Admonition patterns (`.note`, `.warning`, `.alert`, `.tip`, `.info`) — HTML `<div class="note warning">` or kramdown IAL `{: .note .warning}` — without `role="note"` / `role="alert"` / textual severity prefix. WCAG 1.3.3 + 1.4.1 + 4.1.2. SSG docs sites use this pattern ~30 times across `_docs/`, all invisible today (even without markdown support, the pattern will apply to every SSG). Distinct from Q3-RULE-COLOR-SOLE-INDICATOR which is about status-colored text anywhere — this is admonition-widget-specific.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-EXTERNAL-LINK-NEW-WINDOW** New `navigation/link-target-blank-announcement`. `<a target="_blank">` without `rel="noopener noreferrer"` + no new-window announcement (visible text, sr-only span, or `aria-label` containing "opens in new window"). WCAG 3.2.5. Evidence: `showcase.html:14` + `_tutorials/navigation.md` have the pattern. (713c7fe+a362448)
- [x] **Q4-RULE-DUPLICATE-LANDMARK-UNLABELED** New `semantics/duplicate-landmark-unlabeled`. Two or more `<nav>` / `<main>` / `<aside>` / `<form>` landmarks on a rendered page, none or fewer-than-N with `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby`. WCAG 1.3.1 + 2.4.1. Evidence: `header.html` ships two `<nav>` elements (mobile + desktop), neither labeled. Requires cross-file composition awareness (Q4-CROSS-INCLUDE-LANDMARK-COMPOSITION) to fully catch. **Confirmation (2026-04-21 second pass):** in-file single-nav variant also worth covering — `header.html` on the second pass surfaced a single unlabeled `<nav>` in a fragment file; widen the rule's fragment-file single-nav eligibility alongside the multi-nav case.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-SECTION-ACCESSIBLE-NAME** New `semantics/section-accessible-name-missing`. `<section>` used as a content wrapper without `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` / enclosing heading. WCAG 1.3.1 / 4.1.2. `<section>` without name is semantically a generic `<div>` for AT. Low priority (many `<section>` usages are fine as-is per spec — only flag when section is a direct child of `<body>` or sibling to other landmarks).
- [x] **Q4-RULE-PLACEHOLDER-ALT** New `media/alt-text-placeholder`. `<img alt="...">` / `alt="image"` / `alt="screenshot"` / `alt="TODO"` / `alt="placeholder"` — generic alt that teaches bad patterns when copy-pasted. WCAG 1.1.1. Evidence: `_tutorials/` docs + `pages/showcase.html` + `_docs/posts.md` teach `![My helpful screenshot](...)` as canonical — docs propagate weak patterns to every user. (0177360)
- [x] **Q4-RULE-PAGINATION-GLYPH-NAME** New review finder `navigation/pagination-glyph-accessible-name`. Pagination controls using `«` / `»` / `‹` / `›` as sole link text without `aria-label` or sr-only expansion. WCAG 2.4.4 + 4.1.2. Evidence: `_docs/pagination.md:112-169` teaches the glyph pattern; similar patterns across design-system docs.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-DISABLED-LINK-SURROGATE** New `aria/disabled-link-surrogate`. `<span class="disabled">` / `<span class="previous">` used as a disabled control where the live state is a `<a>`. Without `aria-disabled="true"`, AT announces the span as static text, losing the disabled-state signal. WCAG 4.1.2.
- [x] **Q4-RULE-XMLLANG-LANG-MATCH** [cross-ref Q4-XMLLANG-LANG-MISMATCH] Same rule — the tightening doubles as a new rule if `document/lang-attribute` doesn't already cover `xml:lang`. (Redundant: `document/lang-attribute.ts` already reads `xml:lang`, validates BCP 47 agreement at line 69-85, emits a tailored violation when the two disagree; Q4-XMLLANG-LANG-MISMATCH already closed this gap)

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P2)

- [x] **Q4-WARNING-DOWNGRADE-NOISE** (623c4c42) `warnings: ["template_files_parsed_as_literal"]` fires on every Liquid-templated scan — it's a constant on any Jekyll/Hugo/Eleventy repo. Consider demoting to a `meta.templateDirectivesFound` hint when no findings hit lines containing directives (i.e. the literal-parse didn't actually pollute results). Keep the warning when it matters; drop it when it's constant noise.
- [ ] **Q4-LABELS-REQUIRED-VS-LABEL-ADJACENT-OVERLAP** (follow-up from Q5-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED) The new `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` rule can fire alongside `forms/labels-required` on the same input when there is an adjacent `<label>` without `for=`. Both emissions are honest per AI-first surface-don't-suppress doctrine, but field use may reveal whether the overlap is readable or noisy. Observation path: if field reports cite "same input flagged twice," consider de-overlapping by making `labels-required` silent when a label sibling exists (and let `label-adjacent-unassociated` be the canonical voice for that shape). Do NOT preemptively suppress — wait for evidence that the dual-emission confuses consumers. WCAG 1.3.1 / 3.3.2 / 4.1.2. Cross-ref commits 5fdc228+a3f04657.
- [x] **Q4-RULES-EVALUATED-COMPOSITE** (bc29957) `rulesEvaluated: 52` reports total loaded rules, not rules-with-eligible-inputs. On a pure-HTML/SSG project, many React-specific rules show `filesEligible: 0` in `perRuleCoverage` — they didn't fire because they couldn't. Replace the single count with a split: `rulesEvaluated: { loaded, withEligibleInputs, fired }` so an agent sees "52 loaded, 18 had eligible inputs on this file set, 9 fired." Doctrine: "Composite headline counts are dishonest."
- [x] **Q4-PARTIAL-PAGE-TITLED** `document/page-titled` fires on `top.html` (a `<head>` fragment) because the rule sees `{% seo %}` as literal text, not a rendered `<title>`. Pairs with Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION — the rule should skip fragment files, or at minimum emit `couldBeWrongBecause: ["title_may_be_template_injected"]` when the file lacks `<html>`/`<body>`.

### Considered and rejected (per CLAUDE.md §1)

- **Auto-suppress React-specific rules on non-React projects** → rejected per §1 "Labeled buckets are suppression too." The rules correctly no-op when `filesEligible: 0`; the agent sees that in `perRuleCoverage`. Don't filter — surface. Q4-RULES-EVALUATED-COMPOSITE is the right fix (make the empty-eligible-set state legible at the headline level, keep the per-rule data honest).
- **Auto-exclude `_site/`, `vendor/bundle/`, `node_modules/` in `propose_config`** → **accepted in a narrower form**: these are definitionally build output / vendor dumps where findings are not the user's to fix. Fold into `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_PATTERNS` (see `src/input/discover.ts`) rather than `propose_config` — the exclude belongs at discovery, not config. CLAUDE.md §1 "Default-exclude globs are suppression too" permits this because findings in those paths are "definitionally wrong for any consumer." Add `_site/`, `.jekyll-cache/`, `vendor/bundle/`, `_build/` (Hugo), `public/` (Gatsby — though `public/` is ambiguous). Tracked as **Q4-DEFAULT-EXCLUDE-BUILD-DIRS** in P2 above if landed.
- **Heuristic "this looks like an SSG include, suppress skip-link finding"** → rejected as heuristic suppression. Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION is the principled alternative: detect partial/fragment structurally, not by path-pattern guessing.
- **Full markdown CommonMark parser as a P0** → deferred. Q4-MARKDOWN-SUPPORT option (b) — a lightweight HTML-in-markdown extractor — covers 80% of real findings at ~15% of the implementation cost. Revisit full parser after the extractor ships.
- **Auto-detect and scan `_site/` output when Jekyll is detected** → rejected as "tool should not run build toolchains." The agent reads `_config.yml` + `Gemfile` + knows `bundle exec jekyll build`; ra11y should point at the output, not produce it. Q4-SSG-BUILD-HINT is the right mechanism.

### Additional items

- [x] **Q4-PARSE-ERROR-DETAIL** `parse_errors_present` / `parseErrorFileCount: 1` surfaced on `docs/_includes/top.html` with no detail — which parser failed, where, why. Pair the top-level boolean with a structured `analysisCoverage.parseErrors: [{ path, parser, reason }]` so an agent has a fix-pivot beyond "one file didn't parse." Doctrine: verbose meta is scan-confidence signal; the bare boolean is the silent-failure shape. Distinct from Q3-PARSE-ERROR-FILE-CONTRADICTION (that item distinguishes total vs partial failure; this item populates the detail payload for both classes). Landed as `{ path, parser, reason }` entries on both existing buckets (`parseErrorFiles` invisible + `partialParseFiles` partial), always shipped when non-empty (no verboseMeta gate) — the split is the honest shape the prior Q3 work established; enriching both with the per-entry detail is the minimal additive fix.
- [x] **Q4-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON** `warnings: ["scanned_build_artifacts_present"]` fires with paths but no per-path classification reason. Under the doctrine's "provable from code" bar (`ai-first-consumer.md` §labeled-buckets), the label has to survive inspection — `scannedBuildArtifacts` should be `[{ path, reason: "contains-data-url-gradient" | "minified" | "dist-path" | "hashed-filename" | "sourcemap-sibling" }]` so the agent can triage without reading each file. Pairs with Q-SHARED-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SASS-PARTIAL (that item fixes the classifier's predicate; this item makes the classifier's verdict inspectable). Structured over `warnings: string[]` — reason is a per-item payload, not a response-level code.
- [x] **Q4-REVIEW-1.4.5-SR-ONLY-SIBLING** `header.html:7` logo image flagged for `wcag22:1.4.5` (images of text) but the reason doesn't mention the `<span class="sr-only">` sibling inside the same `<a>`. The sibling is a strong dismissal signal: the image is the visual rendering of the text, and an SR-only text equivalent is already carried in the DOM. Extend the 1.4.5 finder to detect `sibling_sr_only_text_present` when the flagged image shares a parent with an element carrying `.sr-only` / `.visually-hidden` / `aria-label` on the parent anchor; enrich `reason` with "note: a visually-hidden text sibling (`.sr-only` span) is present — if the image is the textual logotype and the sibling is the SR-accessible equivalent, this is the documented logotype pattern." Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays visible.
- [x] **Q4-REVIEW-SANITIZE-LIQUID-FINDERS** `showcase.html:16` 1.4.5 reason literally quotes the Liquid template expression `{{ entry.name }}` as the repeated surrounding text. Q4-LIQUID-TEXT-LITERAL stripped Liquid in the rule-side text-node paths, but the fix didn't propagate to `src/review/finders/**` — the same `template_directive_stripped` sanitizer must run on text the finders harvest for reason-building. Apply the existing stripper helper at the finder-side surrounding-text extraction sites; add a fixture under `tests/fixtures/real-world/liquid-review-finder/` with a `{{ entry.name }}` repetition asserting the reason shows the rendered placeholder form (or omits the directive entirely) rather than the source token.
- [x] **Q4-RELEVANCE-REASON-PARSE-COVERAGE-CAVEAT** `likelyIrrelevant` on 1.2.x criteria reports "no `<video>` or `<audio>` elements" as if the file set were fully inspected, when 307 `.md`/`.markdown` files were silently skipped. When `skippedByExtension` includes content extensions above a high-confidence threshold (`.md`, `.markdown`, `.rst`, `.adoc` — authored-content extensions that commonly embed media), enrich `relevanceReason` with a parse-coverage caveat: "note: X files in content extensions ({exts}) were skipped — if the site embeds `<video>` / `<audio>` in markdown content, the media check is under-covered." Doctrine: `likelyIrrelevant` is one of the few labels that must be provable from the code (`ai-first-consumer.md` §labeled-buckets); the bucket claim is only honest when the input set was actually inspected. Pairs with Q4-MARKDOWN-IMPL — once markdown parsing lands, the caveat vanishes automatically for those repos.
- [x] **Q4-TEMPLATE-CLASSIFIER-LIQUID-AS-MUSTACHE-SINGLE-FILE** Q4-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-CLASSIFIER-DRIFT fixed the cross-file majority-vote drift, but a single-file variant remains: an SSG `_layouts/default.html` (pure Liquid with `{%- include -%}`, `{%- comment -%}`, `{{- content -}}`) is classified as `handlebars-or-mustache` because the per-file majority-vote drops `{%- ... -%}` Liquid-only tokens and counts `{{ ... }}` tokens — which the two families share. Fix: treat `{%- ... -%}` / `{% capture %}` / `{% if %}` as high-confidence Liquid-only evidence that decisively wins the per-file vote even when `{{ ... }}` tokens outnumber them. Pairs with Q3-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-STARLIKE-MISDETECT (the Astro/YAML half). Doctrine: verbose meta integrity — wrong template-directive classification leads the agent to reach for the wrong strip rules downstream.
- [x] **Q4-WARNING-DETAILS-CROSS-SURFACE-UNIFY** `warningsDetails.extensions_skipped_no_parser` is populated on `coverage` responses but absent from `scan_project` responses — same warning code, two shapes. Website-template scans reproduce the same pattern for `scanned_build_artifacts_present`, `parse_errors_present` (populated on some surfaces, bare code elsewhere). Fix: ensure every response carrying `warnings: [code]` also carries `warningsDetails: { [code]: { ... } }` when details exist for that code; when no details exist, omit the key rather than shipping empty. Typed-test the cross-surface matrix: for each warning code, it must appear or not appear consistently across `scan_project` / `scan_file` / `coverage` / `checklist`. Doctrine: cross-surface consistency + present-when-meaningful.
- [x] **Q4-LABEL-IN-NAME-LIQUID-STRIP-MISSING** Q4-LIQUID-TEXT-LITERAL was marked closed but `semantics/label-in-name` specifically still quotes raw Liquid `{% for ... %}` as "visible text" on `docs_contents_mobile.html:2` (and two sibling files). Reach: the strip helper was applied to some rules but `label-in-name` never picked it up. Fix: audit every rule under `src/rules/semantics/**` + `src/rules/navigation/**` that harvests visible-text strings, ensure each calls the `stripTemplateDirectives` helper before comparing, and add a rule-inventory regression test that mounts a fixture with `{% for %}` / `{{ var }}` tokens and asserts NO rule emits a finding quoting the raw directive. Pair with Q4-REVIEW-SANITIZE-LIQUID-FINDERS (finder-side already tracked).
- [x] **Q4-SUGGEST-FIX-PROPAGATES-POISONED-VISIBLE-TEXT** `suggest_fix` primary proposal for `semantics/label-in-name` on `docs_contents_mobile` findings builds an `aria-label="{% for section in site.data.docs_nav %}..."` — pasting the raw Liquid template expression into a static ARIA attribute. If an agent applied this mechanically, the page would ship a broken accessible name on every render. Root cause: `suggest_fix` reads the rule's `visibleText` field, not a sanitized version. Fix: run `stripTemplateDirectives` on any text that `suggest_fix` interpolates into `newText`; when the result is empty or nonsense, fall through to a guidance-only suggestion with `fixClass: "guidance"`. Doctrine: verbose meta integrity — `suggest_fix.safety: "safe"` on a proposal that would break the page is a false safety claim.
- [x] **Q4-DOCUMENT-PAGE-TITLED-LIQUID-STRIP** `document/page-titled` fires "empty `<title>`" on `test/source/_layouts/default.html:10` where `<title>` contains `{{ page.title }}`. Q4-LABEL-IN-NAME-LIQUID-STRIP-MISSING (closed) audited `src/rules/semantics/**` + `src/rules/navigation/**`; `src/rules/document/**` was out of scope. Widen the audit to `document/*` rules that consume visible text (page-titled, lang-attribute's xml:lang cross-check, charset/viewport text — any rule whose predicate reads rendered-text content). Fix: run `stripTemplateDirectives` before the emptiness check; when post-strip text is empty AND `templateDirectivesFound` is non-empty on the file, emit a candidate (not a violation) with reason "title is template-interpolated — verify the rendered output carries a non-empty title." Satisfies wcag22:2.4.2.
- [x] **Q4-AUTOCOMPLETE-MISSING-IMPLICIT-BUTTON-TYPE** `forms/autocomplete-missing` fires on `<button>` elements missing `type="button"` in SSG-rendered layouts — the button is adjacent to form-like patterns but the rule conflates "form-adjacent control" with "form field." Fix: gate the rule on input-shaped elements (`<input>`, `<select>`, `<textarea>`); emit a sibling review candidate for `<button>` without explicit `type` inside a `<form>` under a distinct rule (`forms/button-type-explicit`) — two distinct bugs, two distinct rules, no cross-firing. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.5 + 3.3.2.
- [x] **Q4-LIQUID-ROOT-LAYOUT-PARSE-RECOVERY** `docs/_layouts/default.html` fails the HTML parser with "Stray closing tag at top level" because Liquid `{%- include top.html -%}` at line 1 wraps the opening `<html>` (which lives in `top.html`), so the literal top of the layout file is `<body>` + `</html>` without a matching open. `scan_file` on the file runs 64 rules with `fired: 0` (telemetry reads "clean" — dishonest because the DOM was truncated before rules ran). Every `jekyll new` site inherits this root-layout shape. Fix: when the HTML parser encounters a top-level stray closing tag AND the file's first non-whitespace line is a Liquid `{%- include ... -%}` directive (OR the file lives under `_layouts/`/`_includes/`), switch to fragment-mode parsing instead of triggering stray-tag recovery. Pair with a `partialParseFiles` entry noting the shape. Alternative: expose a `parseShape: "fragment-in-included-layout"` hint so downstream rules can adapt. Pairs with Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION (closed) and Q3-PARSE-ERROR-FILE-CONTRADICTION (closed). Satisfies wcag22 coverage: unblocks ~all rules on any Liquid-based root layout.
- [x] **Q4-LIQUID-LINE-DRIFT-SASS-SELECTOR** 2.3.1 flash-threshold candidate cites `docs/_sass/_docsearch.scss:119` for the `:valid ~ .searchbox__reset` animation selector, but the actual selector lives at line 133 — a 14-line drift. Verified by reading the file. The finder's selector-to-line mapping is picking the first token match rather than the structural selector match. Fix: walk the CSS AST by position, not by first-token regex; ensure the reported line is the opening line of the ruleset containing the flagged declaration. Add fixture `tests/fixtures/real-world/ssg-docsearch-scss-line-drift/` with sibling-selector ambiguity, asserting the finder points at the correct line. Pair with Q2R2-FORM-TIMING regression guard (similar "evidence line != finder-reported line" shape). Satisfies wcag22:2.3.1.
- [x] **Q4-AUTOCOMPLETE-1.3.5-EXCLUDE-SEARCH-INPUT** `wcag22:1.3.5 Input Purposes` finder fires on a site-search `<input>` (e.g. `docs/_includes/header.html` docsearch-input with `type="search"` + `aria-label="Search"`). WCAG 1.3.5 lists 53 Input Purposes — `name`, `email`, `tel`, `cc-number`, `bday`, etc. — "search" is explicitly NOT one of them. Fix: scope the finder out when ANY of (a) `type="search"` OR `role="searchbox"` OR (b) `name | id | aria-label` matches `/search|query|q$/i`. The autocomplete-token set doesn't apply to free-form query inputs; flagging them is spec-incorrect. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.5.
- [x] **Q4-HEADING-HIERARCHY-PARTIAL-ENRICH-REASON** `semantics/heading-hierarchy` fires 32× on `_docs/*.md` partials because each partial's first heading is `<h5>` while the composed layout supplies `<h1>` via `page.title` front-matter. Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION (closed) skips page-level rules on structural fragments, but heading-hierarchy's eligibility is "any file with ≥1 heading" regardless of fragment-ness. Fix: when the rule fires on a file whose first non-whitespace token is a Liquid/MDX template directive (OR the file lives under known partial paths `_docs/`, `_includes/`, `_layouts/`, `_posts/`, `_partials/`), enrich reason with "note: partial/layout file — heading hierarchy depends on the composed layout. Verify the rendered page has `<h1>` before acting." Reason-enrichment only — candidate stays in the primary list so the agent reads the composed layout. Pairs with Q4-PARTIAL-PAGE-TITLED (closed, `document/page-titled` variant) — same enrichment shape, different rule. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 2.4.6.
- [x] **Q4-SCAN-FILE-PARSE-ERROR-LIMITATIONS-FIELD** `scan_file` on a parse-error file (`docs/_layouts/default.html` per Q4-LIQUID-ROOT-LAYOUT-PARSE-RECOVERY above) returns `fired: 0`, `findings: []`, `warnings: [parse_errors_present]` — but no top-level signal that the scan was degraded. Agent has to cross-read `analysisCoverage.parseErrorFiles` to learn the DOM was truncated. Add a top-level `limitations: [{ reason: "parse_error" | "partial_parse", file, parser, detail }]` on any `scan_file` response where the parse failed or recovered partially — mirrors how runtime-only checks surface their limitation in other responses. Scan_project's per-file entries should carry the same per-file `limitations` when that file was parse-errored. Pairs with Q4-PARSE-ERROR-DETAIL (closed, populates the detail payload); this item surfaces the detail as a load-bearing top-level signal so `fired: 0` + `limitations: [...]` reads as "scan ran but output is degraded" not "scan ran clean."
- [x] **Q4-LIKELY-REDUCIBLE-SIBLING-IMAGE-DEDUPE** `a README with 10 sponsor logos` — ten `wcag22:1.4.5` candidates, one per sponsor logo (`<a><img alt="Sponsor N"/></a>`), each with identical "alt text repeated in surrounding text" shape. When ≥4 adjacent images share a structural pattern (same parent, same `<a><img/></a>` wrapping, alt-text differing only by enumerated tokens), emit ONE consolidated candidate with `siblingOccurrences: [{ line, alt, href }]` instead of N individual ones. Per surface-don't-suppress doctrine, this is honest pattern aggregation — the agent reads one candidate carrying the full sibling list, not N near-identical ones. Pairs with Q6-ICON-FONT-HIDDEN-GROUP-DEDUP-CLASS-PATTERN (closed, same shape, different rule) and Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open, cross-file variant). Satisfies wcag22:1.4.5.

---

## Track Q5 — Vanilla HTML/CSS/JS field test

Owner: main session + general-purpose. Source: first pass against a hand-authored vanilla-JS tree — 50 pedagogical vanilla `.html` + `.css` + `.js` bundles, no framework, no build step, no template directives. Exercises ra11y on the shape most "starter" tutorials propagate to learners; findings that stick here ship to every hobbyist project that copies these patterns. Triaged against CLAUDE.md §1 — all items are rule/finder correctness or new detection on patterns statically provable from the source.

Why this axis matters: most field tests so far (Q3 design-system, Q4 SSG, Q6 bulk templates) exercise framework-heavy or bundled shapes. A vanilla-JS tree is the control — clean, hand-authored, no build pipeline confounds. When a rule misfires here, the miss is on bare markup the author wrote.

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P1)

- [x] **Q5-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED** `src/rules/forms/labels-required.ts` fires on `<input id="length">` when a `<label>` sits one line above, but the `<label>` has no `for=` attribute. Pervasive pattern (≥21 instances across the repo). The rule's fix text ignores the existing adjacent label. Two paths: (a) widen `labels-required` to skip when an immediately-preceding `<label>` sibling is present (match browser-less-strict label association — the accessibility-inspector-friendly authoring pattern) and enrich `suggestion` to "add `for=\"<id>\"` to the adjacent label at line N"; (b) add a sibling rule `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` firing specifically on the adjacent-but-no-for shape, with a mechanical fix that inserts `for="<id>"`. Prefer (b) — the fix is mechanical, and the existing rule's "no label at all" reason should stay clean for the truly-missing case. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 3.3.2 + 4.1.2.
- [x] **Q5-BUTTON-NAME-ICON-GLYPH-MAP** `src/rules/semantics/button-name.ts` correctly fires on `<button><i class="fa-*"></i></button>` but the fix text says "add visible text" as the primary. When the only child is `<i class="fa-X">` with a well-known glyph name, the primary fix should be `aria-label="<derived-from-glyph-name>"` (small glyph→label map: `fa-bars` → "Menu", `fa-times`/`fa-xmark` → "Close", `fa-arrow-left` → "Previous", `fa-arrow-right` → "Next", `fa-search`/`fa-magnifying-glass` → "Search", `fa-bell` → "Notifications", `fa-user` → "Account"); secondary stays "add visible text." ≥5 instances in typical scans; the same pattern hits every icon-framework-using project. Extends V1-FIX-BUTTON-NAME with the Font Awesome glyph lookup. Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2. (db7a1c0)
- [x] **Q5-CAROUSEL-SETINTERVAL-PAUSE-MISSING** `image-carousel/script.js:9` has `setInterval(run, 2000)` with no pause UI anywhere in the DOM or script body — the candidate surfaces under `wcag22:2.2.1` with a generic reason. When the setInterval callback mutates DOM state (style/class/attribute change on a known-visual target), escalate the reason to cite `wcag22:2.2.2` "Pause, Stop, Hide" and name the missing pause UI. Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays in the primary list; agent reads and decides. Pairs with Q3-RULE-PAUSE-STOP-INLINE-STYLE and Q3-FINDER-CAROUSEL-PATTERN. Satisfies wcag22:2.2.1 + 2.2.2.
- [x] **Q5-SETINTERVAL-SHORT-DURATION-SEIZURE** `blurry-loading/script.js:6` has `setInterval(blurring, 30)` — ~33Hz visual-state mutation. The existing 2.2.1 finder doesn't connect fast intervals to `wcag22:2.3.1` (Three Flashes or Below Threshold). Enrich the finder's `reason` when the callback mutates a visual property (class/style affecting opacity, transform, filter, color, background) AND the duration literal is below 333ms: "note: callback runs at ~{rate}Hz — if the visual effect is a flash/blink, verify against SC 2.3.1 (must not flash more than 3× per second)." Additive context — no threshold-based candidate filtering (doctrine: numeric thresholds are suppression). The duration/rate goes in the reason text as evidence for the agent; the candidate always surfaces. Pairs with R-2.3.1-FLASHING (closed) — that finder already exists; this is integrating the timing-finder output with the flashing-finder criterion so one callback-with-short-interval finding carries both SCs when evidence supports it.
- [x] **Q5-LANDMARK-MAIN-HEADING-UNDERCOUNT-AUDIT** 52 files with `<!DOCTYPE html>` + `<body>` structure produced only 1 `semantics/landmark-main` finding and 6 `heading-hierarchy` findings. Suggests over-permissive fragment-or-layout heuristic in the rule's eligibility predicate. Audit `src/rules/semantics/landmark-main.ts` + `src/rules/semantics/heading-hierarchy.ts` against these files: compare which files are considered "full pages" vs "fragments/layouts" and whether the distinction matches the DOCTYPE + html/body presence. If files with a clear full-page shape are being skipped, tighten the fragment heuristic to require the fragment file to genuinely lack `<html>`/`<body>` (not merely look small). Add 3-4 fixtures under `tests/fixtures/real-world/50p-vanilla-doctype/` covering the actual-shape distribution. Candidate root-cause for the scan looking "clean" when real violations are present. (f5fa5a0 — fixture RED by design per CLAUDE.md §7; rule tightening in looksLikeFullPage is follow-up work)
- [x] **Q5-LANDMARK-FULLPAGE-HEURISTIC-TIGHTEN** Follow-up to Q5-LANDMARK-MAIN-HEADING-UNDERCOUNT-AUDIT. `src/rules/semantics/landmark-main.ts::looksLikeFullPage` currently gates on presence of `<header>`/`<nav>`/`<footer>`/`<aside>`; a file with `<!DOCTYPE html>` + `<html>` + `<body>` (no other landmarks) is mis-classified as a fragment and the rule never fires. The naive fix (accept DOCTYPE + `<html>` + `<body>` as sufficient full-page evidence) was tried inline and reverted — it broke 8 independent tests across integration/CLI/MCP/bootstrap test suites because ~30 good-path HTML fixtures under `tests/fixtures/good/**/*.html` share that minimal full-page shape without a `<main>` landmark (they were authored under the old heuristic). A correct tightening needs a stronger signal than DOCTYPE+html+body alone — candidates: body descendant count (≥N interactive/content elements), form/interactive-element density, multiple-sibling structural content, or a content-vs-fixture classifier that reads file size / script count / title presence. Whatever heuristic is chosen, budget for either (a) updating ~30 good-path fixtures to add `<main>` wrappers, or (b) passing a "fixture mode" flag from the real-world harness that skips the rule. Evidence fixtures at `tests/fixtures/real-world/50p-vanilla-doctype/` (f5fa5a0); the failing assertion there is currently COMMENTED OUT pending this design decision. WCAG 1.3.1 / 2.4.1.
- [x] **Q5-RULE-ICON-FONT-ARIA-HIDDEN** New `aria/icon-font-hidden` rule (note severity). `<i class="fas fa-arrow-left">` / `<i class="fa-*">` / `<span class="material-icons">` inside a labeled parent (button with aria-label, link with text sibling) should carry `aria-hidden="true"` to avoid AT double-announcement. ~10+ instances in typical scans; same pattern across every icon-font project (Font Awesome, Material Icons, Bootstrap Icons, Ionicons). Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2 + 1.3.1. Distinct from button-name / link-descriptive-text — those fire when the parent has no name at all; this rule fires when the parent is named AND the icon child is unannotated. Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/icon-font-hidden/`. Mechanical fix. (b704a95+e24f64f+d0776c5+1deeab0)
- [x] **Q5-NAV-ROLE-SINGLE-PAGE-REASON** `wcag22:2.4.5 Multiple Ways` fires on every single-page HTML fragment with an identical boilerplate reason. The spec scopes 2.4.5 to "sets of Web pages" — single-page standalone files are a documented exception. Tighten the finder's `reason` when exactly one HTML file is being scanned OR the scan root has no multi-page signals (no router config, no `pages/` directory, no `<a href>` chain into sibling HTML files): "note: SC 2.4.5 applies to sets of Web pages — if this is a single-page app or standalone file, the criterion may not apply; verify whether the scanned file is part of a multi-page set." Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays visible per surface-don't-suppress. Distinct from Q4-FRAGMENT-DETECTION (that item is about partials/layouts within a larger template system; this item is about standalone single-file HTML projects).

### Additional items

- [x] **Q5-RADIO-GROUP-NAKED-NAME-SHARING** (already covered by Q3-RULE-RADIO-GROUP-FIELDSET, 5777e89+27083fc) Verified no-op — `forms/radio-group-without-fieldset` already fires on naked-name-sharing via `summarizeKinds` → `no-wrapper`. Unit test (`tests/unit/rules/forms/radio-group-without-fieldset.test.ts:7-24`) + bad fixture (`no-wrapper.html`) + real-world fixture (`tests/fixtures/real-world/50p-radio-group-naked-name-sharing/`, commit 1c37465) all green today; the signal that motivated this item was stale MCP subprocess state, not a rule gap. No widen needed.
- [x] **Q5-OTP-INPUT-CLUSTER-DETECTION** `verify-account-ui/index.html` has a cluster of 6 `<input type="number" maxlength="1">` for a 6-digit OTP; `forms/labels-required` fires 6× individually but nothing detects the cluster pattern. New review finder `forms/otp-input-cluster`: detect ≥4 sibling single-character inputs (`type="number"`/`type="text"` with `maxlength="1"` or similar) within a common parent; recommend `autocomplete="one-time-code"` on at least one, plus a single group-level `<label>` / `aria-labelledby` covering the cluster. Candidate, not assertion — cluster heuristic may false-positive on non-OTP patterns; agent reads surrounding code to verify. WCAG 1.3.1 + 1.3.5 + 3.3.2.
- [x] **Q5-DUPLICATE-LINK-TEXT-SAME-HREF** `split-landing-page/index.html` has two `<a>Buy Now</a>` both targeting `href="#"` with no distinguishing accessible name. `navigation/link-descriptive-text` fires on keyword-literal generic phrases but not on same-accessible-name-on-≥2-links. Extend the rule with a same-page pass: when ≥2 anchors share the same normalized accessible name and share the same `href`, flag all occurrences with reason "N links on this page use the same accessible name and href; a screen reader user navigating by link list cannot distinguish them." WCAG 2.4.4 + 2.4.9 (AAA). Reason-enrichment path if the hrefs differ but the names match: separate candidate tier (same-name-different-href is lower-confidence — legitimate pattern in some nav designs).
- [x] **Q5-DECORATIVE-IMG-WITH-ADJACENT-MEANING** `feedback-ui-design/index.html` has emoji images marked `alt=""` adjacent to `<small>Unhappy</small>` / `<small>Happy</small>` — the image IS the visual indicator of the named affect, so `alt=""` removes meaning the sibling text doesn't replace (the text names the affect; the image shows the affect). Distinct from 1.4.5 images-of-text. New finder `media/decorative-img-with-adjacent-meaning`: when an `<img alt="">` is the sole image child of a parent that also contains meaning-bearing text referring to an affect/status/value (via a short dictionary: "happy", "sad", "warning", "success", etc.), surface as a review candidate — "image marked decorative but an adjacent sibling names the visual's meaning; verify the image is truly redundant or populate `alt=`." WCAG 1.1.1. (committed d337137b)
- [x] **Q5-NUMERIC-ONLY-LABEL-CONTENT** `custom-range-slider/index.html` has `<label for="range">50</label>` — `forms/non-empty-label` passes because the label has text content, but "50" is a value display, not a semantic name. Enrich the rule's reason when the label's `textContent` matches a value-shape regex (`^\d+(\.\d+)?$` / currency / percentage / date / time patterns): "label text `{text}` looks like a value, not a name — verify whether this element labels the control or displays its current value; a labelling element should name what the control controls." Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays in the primary list (the distinction is context-dependent; agent reads surrounding code). WCAG 2.4.6 + 3.3.2.
- [x] **Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER** (5da32248) `keyboard/handler-missing` never fires when click handlers live in external `.js` files — the rule only inspects inline `onClick=` on HTML elements. Reproduced across expanding-cards (`script.js:4` attaches click to 5 `<div class="panel">`, zero findings despite `rulesEvaluated.fired: 2` showing the rule ran), drag-n-drop, drawing-app, button-ripple-effect. Biggest single coverage hole on vanilla-JS trees.
