Fix: extend the rule to detect `.addEventListener('click', …)` and `element.onclick = …` patterns on JS/TSX AST that are NOT paired with a sibling `keydown`/`keyup` listener on the same target, emitting the candidate on the element selector resolved in the HTML input (match by `id=`/`class=` to the query-selector string) or on the JS line when HTML resolution fails. Pair with Q3-RULE-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-ATTR (closed, `data-bs-toggle`/inline `onclick=`) — same rule, widened substrate. Satisfies wcag22:2.1.1. Cross-refs "Needs judgment" J-7 — this is the static-analysis path (matching handler presence on the same file), not the full cross-file identifier resolution. Doctrine: detect what's statically provable; let the agent resolve what isn't.
- [x] **Q5-POINTER-GESTURES-SUBSTRING-FALSE-POSITIVE** `wcag22:2.5.1` finder flags `const panels = …` because the identifier "panels" contains the substring "pan" (treated as pan-gesture evidence). Classic identifier-substring match without word-boundary or richer signal. Fix: require word-boundary match (`/\bpan(?:ning|Gesture)?\b/i`) AND a companion signal — a `touchstart`/`touchmove`/`pointermove` listener OR a pointer-event library import (hammer.js, use-gesture, @use-gesture). When neither signal is present, the identifier match alone is insufficient evidence; drop the candidate. Reproduced on a vanilla-JS expanding-cards demo. Satisfies wcag22:2.5.1.
- [x] **Q5-RULE-CUSTOM-WIDGET-KEYBOARD-INOPERABLE** New rule `keyboard/widget-inoperable`. `<canvas>` / `<div>` / `<span>` elements with click handlers but no `role`, no `tabindex`, no keyboard handler. drawing-app's `<canvas id="canvas">` plus sibling `#decrease`/`#size`/`#clear` buttons — ra11y flags the canvas for `media/alt-text-missing` but misses the widget-level keyboard-inoperability. Fix: rule fires when (a) element is a generic/presentational tag (`div`/`span`/`canvas`/`p`/`section`/`header`/`footer`) AND (b) rule-local evidence shows an attached click handler (inline or detected via Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER pass) AND (c) no `tabindex` AND (d) no `role` that implies interactivity (`button`, `link`, `checkbox`, `tab`, `menuitem`) AND (e) no paired keyboard handler. Mechanical fix: "add `role=\"button\"` + `tabindex=\"0\"` + `onKeyDown` that invokes the same click handler, OR replace with `<button type=\"button\">`." Satisfies wcag22:2.1.1 + 4.1.2. Pairs with Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER (prerequisite). **Superseded by Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER (5da32248).** Scope-diagnosis confirmed `keyboard/handler-missing` already fires on every generic/presentational tag in the enumerated set, on both inline and external-JS click handlers, and crucially does NOT exempt on role or tabindex presence (line 186 of `src/rules/keyboard/handler-missing.ts` documents this explicitly). Implementing widget-inoperable as a new rule would either duplicate handler-missing or silently narrow coverage by requiring both role and tabindex to be absent — the latter a silent-miss per AI-first doctrine. Residual delta is fix-suggestion wording (generic-rendering-tag-aware enrichment inside `handler-missing`'s `buildSuggestion`) — tracked separately under Q5-HANDLER-MISSING-GENERIC-TAG-SUGGESTION below.
- [x] **Q5-FOCUS-OUTLINE-VISIBLE-BUTTON-SEVERITY** `focus/outline-visible` fires on 3d-boxes `.magic:focus { outline: none }` at `severity: "info"`, `confidence: "low"` — even though `.magic` selects a real `<button>` element, not a class that might apply to non-interactive wrappers. Bare `outline: none` on a `<button>` selector without a replacement focus indicator in the same block is a high-confidence 2.4.7 violation. Fix: upgrade severity to `"error"` + confidence to `"high"` when (a) the selector resolves to a concrete interactive element (`button`, `a[href]`, `input`, `select`, `textarea`, `[role="button"]`) in the scanned HTML AND (b) the same CSS block contains no `outline:` replacement declaration AND (c) no sibling selector in the same stylesheet adds `box-shadow`/`border`/`text-decoration` as a focus indicator. Reproduced on button-ripple-effect (correct severity) vs 3d-boxes (low-info); the difference is whether Tailwind focus-ring cross-reference fired (on this tree it didn't). Satisfies wcag22:2.4.7.
- [x] **Q5-HANDLER-MISSING-GENERIC-TAG-SUGGESTION** Refinement of `keyboard/handler-missing`'s `buildSuggestion`/`buildAttributeSuggestion` for generic rendering tags (`canvas`, `section`, `header`, `footer`, `p`). Current suggestion text is `<button>`-centric ("change to `<button type=\"button\">`") — accurate for `<div>`/`<span>` but awkward for `<canvas>` (the rendering surface is the point, not the button-ness) or for structural landmarks where the right answer is to move the handler to a child button. Add a tag-specific branch: for `canvas`, suggest wrapping a child `<button>` or adding `role="button"` + `tabindex="0"` + `onKeyDown`; for `section`/`header`/`footer`, suggest moving the click handler to a child interactive element rather than making the landmark itself a widget. Mechanical refinement; satisfies wcag22:2.1.1 follow-up. Spawned from the Q5-RULE-CUSTOM-WIDGET-KEYBOARD-INOPERABLE scope-diagnosis.
- [x] **Q5-HEADLINE-COUNT-DRIFT-THREE-TOTALS** Three different violation totals from one vanilla-JS scan: `plan.violations: 222`, `sum(perRuleCoverage.findingsEmitted): 242`, `sum(paginated files[*].findings) across all offsets: 227`. The delta `242 − 222 = 20` matches `plan.notes: 20` (notes excluded from plan.violations but included in perRuleCoverage), but 227 from paginated files reconciles with neither. Likely: `groupKey` dedup is applied in `plan.violations`, not applied in `perRuleCoverage.findingsEmitted`, partially applied in the paginated file entries. Fix: pick ONE canonical count for violations and document the delta at the response shape level — `plan.violationsCanonical` (one number) + sibling `meta.countsBySurface: { plan, perRuleCoverage, paginated }` when they disagree (tripwire for future drift). Add cross-surface invariant test: on a non-truncated scan, `sum(files[*].findings.length)` equals `plan.violations + (plan.notes ?? 0)` minus a documented dedup delta (or zero). Pairs with Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT (closed) — same cross-surface-consistency doctrine, different counter.
- [x] **Q5-PAGINATION-SOFT-CAP-REASON** `limit: 100` on returns 29 files at `offset: 25` (nextOffset 54), then 24 files at `offset: 54` (nextOffset 78), then 2 files at `offset: 78` (no `truncated` field on the last page → implicitly false). Soft cap below requested limit on every page with no structured reason code. Either the density cap is per-page (should emit `effectiveLimit` + `response_token_budget_truncated` on every clipped page) or the page was bounded by available files-at-that-offset (should emit an `exhaustedAtOffset` signal). Fix: every paginated response carries `effectiveLimit` on every page, AND a `pageClipReason: "token_density" | "end_of_results" | "per_criterion_cap"` when the returned count is less than the requested limit. Pairs with Q-SHARED-LIMIT-REQUEST-VS-EFFECTIVE (open cross-repo companion) — same honesty shape, explicitly per-page.
- [x] **Q5-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-HONESTY-CROSS-FILE-BLINDSPOT** `keyboard/handler-missing` still reports `coverageConfidence: "high"` on HTML files even though the rule architecturally cannot resolve cross-file `addEventListener` wiring (inline-only for HTML substrate). Q5-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-EXTERNAL-JS-ADDEVENTLISTENER (closed) widened detection on the JS side, but the HTML-scoped `scan_file` still claims "high coverage" when the evidence-gathering is bounded by single-file substrate. Fix: every rule whose evaluation scope is "one file" but whose target spec spans cross-file wiring must downgrade `coverageConfidence` to `"medium"` on HTML-only scans with `reason: "cross_file_listener_resolution_limited_on_this_input"`. Audit: `keyboard/handler-missing`, `aria/labelledby-target-exists`, `navigation/skip-link-target-valid`, `forms/error-message-not-associated`, `pointer/drag-alternative` — any rule whose predicate could match on evidence in a sibling file we didn't walk in THIS call. Pairs with J-7 (open doctrine decision about cross-file identifier resolution). Doctrine: coverage confidence is scan-confidence telemetry — dishonest when the rule can't see the evidence.
  (blocked 2026-04-22 — classification_mismatch: needs type-smith widening of PerRuleCoverage.coverageConfidence union from `'high' | 'low'` to `'high' | 'medium' | 'low'` + addition of Rule.crossFileCapable?: boolean primitive; the five audit-list rules flag + the downgrade emission then land in a follow-up)
- [x] **Q5-TYPE-SMITH-CROSS-FILE-CONFIDENCE-PRIMITIVE** Widen `PerRuleCoverage.coverageConfidence` to `'high' | 'medium' | 'low'` and add `Rule.crossFileCapable?: boolean`. Type-smith-only change; no finding-emission logic. Unblocks Q5-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-HONESTY-CROSS-FILE-BLINDSPOT. Widen the union, update `buildPerRuleCoverage` builders + `ruleCoverage` derivatives + any tests hardcoding the literal union. Then Q5-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-HONESTY-CROSS-FILE-BLINDSPOT can re-dispatch to apply the flag + gate the downgrade. (50d1ed62)
- [x] **Q5-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-SC-23-VS-22-CONFLATION** `motion/pause-stop-hide` emits findings under `wcag22:2.2.2` on vanilla HTML/JS projects, but all 28 hits are CSS `transition:` declarations inside `:hover`/`:focus`/`:active` pseudo-class contexts (user-triggered motion) — the correct SC is `wcag22:2.3.3 Animation from Interactions` (AAA). 2.2.2 targets auto-updating content (`setInterval`, `<marquee>`, auto-advancing carousel); 2.3.3 targets animation from user interactions. Fix: differentiate at rule-evaluation time. Build two variants on the same rule family: (a) emit under 2.2.2 when the animation trigger is timer-based OR scroll-based OR auto-playing (`setInterval`, `animation:` keyframes with no user-trigger selector, `data-bs-ride="carousel"`); (b) emit under 2.3.3 when the trigger is a user pseudo-class (`:hover`, `:focus`, `:active`) OR a user-event JS handler. Each finding carries a single `satisfies` criterion matching the trigger. Pairs with J-1 (open doctrine decision about signal-vs-noise aggregation) — the spec-correctness fix is independent of the aggregation question. Satisfies wcag22:2.2.2 + 2.3.3 (separate lanes).
- [x] **Q5-LANDMARK-MAIN-INCONSISTENT-PER-FILE** `semantics/landmark-main` fires on `drink-water/index.html` (no `<main>`) but does NOT fire on `expanding-cards/index.html` (also no `<main>`, same DOCTYPE+html+body shape) on the same scan. Predicate is inconsistent — some eligibility short-circuit fires on one file but not the other. Audit `src/rules/semantics/landmark-main.ts::looksLikeFullPage` with both files as real-world fixtures; the inconsistency suggests a silent fall-through based on presence/absence of some other landmark the predicate treats as "already has landmarks, skip the main check" gate. Pairs with Q5-LANDMARK-FULLPAGE-HEURISTIC-TIGHTEN (open) — likely the same heuristic tightening closes both. Add fixture `tests/fixtures/real-world/50p-landmark-main-per-file-inconsistency/` with both files asserting the rule fires on both OR on neither.
- [x] **Q5-DRAG-ALTERNATIVE-KEYBOARD-COMPOSE** `pointer/drag-alternative` fires on `<div draggable="true">` in `drag-n-drop/index.html:11` but does NOT compose with `keyboard/handler-missing` for the same element. Both rules apply: 2.5.7 (drag alternative) AND 2.1.1 (keyboard accessible). Two honest findings; currently only one. Investigate whether the rule-runner dedupes findings from different rules on the same element (probably doesn't — verify) OR whether `keyboard/handler-missing`'s eligibility predicate short-circuits when `draggable="true"` is present (probably does — tighten). Fix: draggable elements without keyboard wiring fire BOTH rules; the agent sees two distinct criteria mapped to one element and two distinct fix paths. Satisfies wcag22:2.1.1 + 2.5.7.

## Track Q6 — Bulk-template catalog field test

Owner: main session + general-purpose. Source: first pass against a 40+ bulk-template tree. Each template is a pre-built marketing/landing-page bundle shipping jQuery-era vendor CSS/JS (bootstrap.css, font-awesome.css, jquery.fancybox.pack.js) alongside authored HTML. Exercises ra11y at scale — the scan hit the MCP token ceiling, surfaced several rule misfires against vendor bundles, and exposed at-scale quality gaps in the proposed config + build-artifact warning path.

Why this axis matters: a bulk template catalog is the "buy a template kit" shape that a huge fraction of small-business and freelance sites ship. The vendor-CSS noise ratio and jQuery-era link antipatterns (`href="javascript:void(0)"`, icon-only social links) are not framework-authoring problems — they are the default ra11y-on-real-purchased-templates experience. When the token budget overruns, legitimate findings never reach the agent.

Three cross-repo items moved out: `PROPOSE_CONFIG_EMITS_ABSOLUTE_PATHS_AND_TRAILING_COMMA` folds into `Q-SHARED-PROPOSE-CONFIG-RELATIVE-PATHS` (Track Q3 cross-repo section). Two Q6 items consolidate under one roof each: Q6-1 + Q6-13 → `Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE`; Q6-10 + Q6-11 + SSG-4 share scope with Q4-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON (cross-refs inline).

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P0 — response budget + signal-to-noise)

- [x] **Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE** Default `limit: 25` collapsed to `effectiveLimit: 1` via the density cap when vendor files dominated — `bootstrap.css` emitted 5,458 findings and `font-awesome.css` emitted 8,940 on a single scan, exhausting the 88K-char budget on one file. Two compounding gaps: (a) the token-density cap (V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET-DENSITY-2) held the envelope but starved the response to one file's worth of findings; (b) a single criterion can carry 200+ review candidates on one file (see Q6-MEANINGFUL-SEQUENCE-UTILITY-CLASSES — `.order-0..13` utilities flagged 200+ times). Two additive fixes: per-file finding cap (hard limit ≈100 findings per file per response, with `perFileTruncated: true` + `findingsTotalInFile` on the file's entry) to prevent one file hoarding the budget; per-criterion byte cap on checklist responses (clip to first 3 candidates per criterion when response bytes exceed threshold, with `perCriterionClipped: true`). Both caps are additive labels — findings are not suppressed, they are paginated per scope. Consolidates Q6-1 + Q6-13. Pairs with `scanned_build_artifacts_present` — vendor bundles drive the budget overrun. Doctrine: pagination is honest; suppression is not.
- [x] **Q6-PLAN-TOTALFINDINGS-VIOLATIONS-VS-NOTES** `plan.totalFindings: 24772` buries `notes: 1079` under the same counter (violations: 23693). Composite headline count per `ai-first-consumer.md` §composite-headline-counts. Either rename to `plan.totalFindingsComposite` with a clearer breakdown elsewhere, or split into `plan.violationsCount` + `plan.notesCount` at the same level (matches the shape `ScanSubset` already carries post-V1-COMP-BOOTSTRAP-SCAN-TOTAL). The `--format agent` CLI summary already breaks out "N violations + M notes"; align the MCP plan counter with the same split.

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P1 — rule/finder correctness)

- [x] **Q6-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-JS-STRING-LITERALS** Review candidates for 1.2.x / 4.1.2 iframe criteria include `jquery.fancybox.pack.js:4` with snippet `"<iframe id=\"fancybox-frame{rnd}\"..."`. The iframe-review finder line-scans `.js` files as if the literal string contents were HTML elements on the page. Restrict the finder to HTML-family inputs: `.html` / `.htm` / `.jsx` / `.tsx` / `.vue` / `.svelte` (the files ra11y actually parses as DOM). JS string literals containing `<iframe>` are not rendered iframes — they are library code building DOM at runtime; the agent reads the surrounding file to verify. Mirrors the TSX-text-corpus fix in V1-FP-IMAGES-OF-TEXT-SVG-DATA-URI's shape: the text-matching pass must run on DOM-origin text only, not on string-literal payloads inside source code.
- [x] **Q6-MEANINGFUL-SEQUENCE-UTILITY-CLASSES** 1.3.2 review candidates fire on every `.order-0` .. `.order-13` utility-class selector in `bootstrap.css` / authored compiled CSS — 14 selectors × ~40 templates = ~560 candidates per scan, all pointing at the same utility definitions rather than at any consumer. Finder must operate on HTML/JSX *consumers* that apply `class="order-N"`, not on the CSS selector definition site. Tighten: lift the 1.3.2 finder from the CSS-selector scope to the HTML/JSX usage scope (match `class="...order-N..."` / `className="...order-N..."` on elements); silence on the CSS definition when no consumer in the scan applies the class. When the finder *does* hit a CSS-only definition, enrich the reason with "note: utility-class definition; verify consumers apply this class intentionally." Doctrine: the finder's premise (sequence-dependent visual order) is real; the current location (CSS definition) is wrong.
- [x] **Q6-RULE-HREF-JAVASCRIPT-VOID** New `navigation/href-javascript-void` rule. 120 occurrences of `<a href="javascript:void(0)">` across 10 sampled files; zero findings today. Covers `href="javascript:void(0)"`, `href="javascript:;"`, and `href="#"` when no click-handler delegation is detectable (e.g. no matching jQuery `.on("click")` selector, no event delegation parent). All three forms are `<button>` roles masquerading as links; AT announces "link," keyboard Tab lands with no navigation. Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2 + 2.1.1. Mechanical fix: "change `<a href=\"javascript:void(0)\">` to `<button type=\"button\">` — this control mutates state, not navigates." Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/href-javascript-void/`. (0b942fc)
- [x] **Q6-LINK-ICON-ONLY-ACCNAME** `src/rules/navigation/link-descriptive-text.ts` does not flag `<a><i class="fa fa-twitter"></i></a>` — the anchor has no text, no `aria-label`, no labeling child. Extend the rule to compute the full accessible name: ignore presentational descendants (`<i class="fa-*">`, `<span class="material-icons">`, decorative `<img role="presentation">`), then flag when the computed name is empty. Pairs with Q5-RULE-ICON-FONT-ARIA-HIDDEN — that rule enriches labeled parents with icon children; this rule flags unlabeled parents with only icon children. Satisfies wcag22:2.4.4 + 4.1.2. (2ce309f+8932f04+481c86f)
- [x] **Q6-AUTODETECT-WRAPPERS-LEAKS-JS-IDENTIFIER-NOISE** `autoDetectWrappers: true` at scale returned `opaqueCustomComponentNames: ["AG.y","B","H.length","J","J.length","Math.abs"]`. The extractor walks `.js` files grepping for identifier expressions; single-letter variables, member-access expressions (`J.length`), and built-ins (`Math.abs`) are not component names. Restrict extraction to JSX/TSX element tag positions only — `<ComponentName ... />` / `<ComponentName>...</ComponentName>` where the tag name starts with an uppercase letter and is a bare identifier (no `.`, no single-char unless explicitly allowlisted). Skip the `.js` extension entirely for JSX-tag harvesting (plain `.js` has no JSX syntax). Paired with Q3-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-TOP-EMPTY-DISHONEST under opaque-component quality, but the extraction-source filter is the root-cause fix.
- [x] **Q6-PLACEHOLDER-IN-FIX-SUGGESTION** Minor refinement on `forms/labels-required`: when the rule fires AND the offending `<input>` has a `placeholder="..."` attribute, include the placeholder verbatim in the fix suggestion ("`<label for=\"email\">Email address</label>` — placeholder text `\"Enter email\"` may carry the intent; verify it's accurate before using as label copy"). Pairs with Q3-RULE-PLACEHOLDER-AS-LABEL (new rule for placeholder-as-only-label antipattern) — this item is the fix-suggestion enrichment for the already-firing `labels-required` rule when the placeholder is additive evidence for the correct label text. Extends V1-FIX-NON-EMPTY-LABEL's placeholder path.

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P2 — build-artifact rollup + signal routing)

- [x] **Q6-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-GROUP-BY-BASENAME** Companion to Q4-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON. When `scannedBuildArtifacts` surfaces 301 entries (bootstrap.css: 45, font-awesome.css: 80, etc.), the flat list is unactionable — the agent sees 301 individual paths and cannot tell that one `exclude` glob covers most of them. Group by basename with a suggested glob: `scannedBuildArtifacts: { grouped: [{ basename, count, pathHint, suggestedGlob }], ungrouped: [paths] }`. `suggestedGlob` is inline-ready for `propose_config`. Additive shape over the existing flat `scannedBuildArtifacts: [paths]` array (consumers that iterate the flat list still work; the grouped form is a sibling field). Pairs with Q-SHARED-PROPOSE-CONFIG-RELATIVE-PATHS — collapsing 301 paths to ~5 glob patterns.
- [x] **Q6-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-PER-FILE-AGGREGATION** `motion/pause-stop-hide` produced 8,940 findings (36% of the scan's total) against vendor `bootstrap.css` alone. Every CSS animation keyframe / transition declaration is technically a 2.2.2 concern; vendor bundles carry hundreds. When a single rule fires >N× in one file AND that file is in `scannedBuildArtifacts`, add `perRuleCoverage[ruleId].vendorConcentration?: { file, count }` pointing at the densest vendor file with its total count. Findings still emit individually under `files[]` per surface-don't-suppress (pairs with V1-NOISE-RULE-PER-FILE-ROLLUP, which rolls up the same signal for non-vendor files above a concentration threshold). Additive label — zero information loss, one more field for the agent's triage. The V1 item handled the non-vendor case; this item extends the same shape to vendor bundles explicitly so the agent sees "most of this rule's fire is one vendor file" without having to sort findings themselves.
- [x] **Q6-NEXTSTEP-AVOIDS-VENDOR-CSS** `nextStepStructured.file` pointed at `bootstrap.css:365` — vendor CSS is rarely the file the agent should act on first. When the top-ranked violation or next-step target sits in `scannedBuildArtifacts`, rank the suggestion toward a non-vendor file with a finding of the same rule family (if one exists in the same response); fall back to the vendor file only when every violation sits in vendor code. Doctrine-adjacent to "surface-don't-suppress": the vendor finding stays in `files[]`, but the agent's first action should land where they can actually edit. Reason-text when this rerouting applies: "note: highest-severity finding in this scan is in `vendor/bootstrap.css`; next-step points at `authored/site.css` where a same-family fix is applicable." Additive — when no rerouting applies, `nextStepStructured` behaves exactly as today.

### Additional items

- [x] **Q6-RULE-ARIA-REF-MISMATCH-WIDEN-ATTRS** Website-template `coffee-shop/index.html:162` has `aria-labelledby="myModalLabel"` pointing at an id that does not exist anywhere in the document. Q3-RULE-LABELLEDBY-TARGET (closed) covers `aria-labelledby` / `aria-describedby` / `aria-controls` but did NOT fire here — first investigate whether this is rule-registry drift (see Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT); once the registry is fixed and the rule fires, widen coverage to all eight ARIA ID-ref attributes: `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-controls`, `aria-owns`, `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-errormessage`, `aria-flowto`, `aria-details`. Deterministic, zero-false-positive check. Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/aria-ref-widen/` covering each of the eight attributes. WCAG 4.1.2.
- [x] **Q6-RULE-INVALID-ID-SHAPE** Website-template `agile-agency/index.html:60` has `id="#top"` — the `#` is an invalid id-start character per HTML-living-standard (ids may not contain whitespace but are otherwise any non-empty string; however, `id="#top"` is the author confusing id syntax with href-fragment syntax). `parsing/duplicate-id` only fires on duplicates, not on malformed shapes. Add a companion `parsing/invalid-id-shape` rule (or extend duplicate-id): flag ids that start with `#`, contain whitespace, are empty-string, or collide with well-known CSS-escape-required characters when used unescaped. Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/invalid-id-shape/`. WCAG 4.1.1 (parsing) — note: WCAG 2.2 removed 4.1.1 as a testable SC; keep the rule under `parsing/` as best-practice with `satisfies: [wcag21:4.1.1]` (parsing-failure in WCAG 2.1) only, not 2.2.
- [x] **Q6-ICON-FONT-LABELED-PARENT-REGRESSION** Q5-RULE-ICON-FONT-ARIA-HIDDEN (closed) should fire on `<button><i class="fa-bars"></i></button>` patterns in coffee-shop (lines 59, 82, 86), but the rule emitted zero findings. Investigate: (a) registry drift (covered by Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT); (b) the rule's "labeled parent" predicate may not cover `<button>` wrapping `<i>` without any other child text when the parent's accessible name comes solely from an `aria-label` rather than a text node — re-read the rule and add `tests/fixtures/real-world/bulk-template-icon-button/` asserting the rule fires. If (b), widen the labeled-parent signal to include `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` / `aria-describedby` on the parent. WCAG 4.1.2 + 1.3.1.
- [x] **Q6-ANCHOR-ICON-ONLY-NAME-REGRESSION** Q6-LINK-ICON-ONLY-ACCNAME (closed) should fire on `<a href="#home"><img src="logo.png" alt="..."/></a>` patterns in coffee-shop (`index.html:35`), but the deeper scan emitted `media/alt-text-missing` at the `<img>` level only — not a secondary finding at the anchor level for missing accessible name. When the only child of an `<a>` is a decorative/presentational element (icon `<i>`, `<img role="presentation">`, `<svg aria-hidden>`) AND the anchor has no `aria-label`, the anchor has no accessible name regardless of the child's alt. Investigate: registry drift (see Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT) vs rule predicate gap (img with non-empty alt counted as giving the anchor a name, masking the distinction between "anchor name = alt" vs "anchor unnamed"). WCAG 2.4.4 + 4.1.2.
- [x] **Q6-MINIFIED-FILE-SNIPPET-COLUMN-ENRICHMENT** Website-template scans cited `bootstrap.min.js:6` and `col 1` eight times for setTimeout review candidates on a 6-line minified file — every match points at the same line/col because the entire file is one line. The agent cannot locate the specific call site. When the cited file matches minified heuristics (single line > 1000 chars, `.min.` infix, sibling sourcemap present), enrich `reason` with (a) byte-column offset within the single line (so `col 47201` disambiguates multiple matches), (b) a pointer to the unminified sibling if one exists (`authored: "src/bootstrap.js"` when `src/bootstrap.js` exists alongside `dist/bootstrap.min.js`), and (c) the ~80-char context window around the match. Additive reason enrichment — findings remain in `files[]`; the agent gets a usable pointer. Doctrine: "The tool's job is to point" — a line:col pointer to a minified file that resolves to "the whole file" is a dishonest pointer. Shipped (a) byte col + (c) ~80-char context window via `src/review/finders/timing-minified.ts`; sibling resolution (b) dropped per backlog "if sibling-resolution gets expensive, skip" clause — node-scoped finders can't see other files without a scope widening that's out of proportion with the pointer-disambiguation value.
- [x] **Q6-SCSS-PARSING-DISCLOSURE** Website-template `filesByExtension` reports 584 `.scss` files, but `perRuleCoverage.filesEvaluated` numbers don't cleanly account for them (`1637 ≠ 1053 + 584`). Response does not disclose whether `.scss` is parsed as CSS (via `extensionMatches` alias) or skipped. Add `analysisCoverage.sassParsingMode: "css-alias" | "skipped" | "native"` (or equivalent) so the agent knows whether a `.scss`-heavy repo was fully inspected by CSS-family rules. Alternative: enrich `filesByExtension` entries with a `parsedAs` field (`"css"` / `"skipped"` / `"native"`). Doctrine: "Ambiguous field shapes are dishonest" — a filesByExtension count that doesn't match the per-rule file count forces the agent to guess coverage.
- [x] **Q6-RULE-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED-NO-IDS** `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` never fires on the canonical orphan-label shape `<label>Text Input</label><input class="form-control">` — 12 instances at `sb-admin/forms.html:142,148,158,163,230-ish` (and similar counts across other templated forms). The rule's predicate requires the `<input>` to already carry an `id` so it can match against the absent `for=`; real templates routinely omit the id entirely. Without it, `forms/labels-required` takes over with generic guidance and the richer "add `for=\"<id>\"` + add `id=\"<id>\"`" mechanical pair never surfaces. Fix: adjacency predicate fires on `<label>` immediately preceding an input-shaped element regardless of whether either carries an `id`; mechanical fix proposes a synthesized id and both attributes in one edit. Most common form-a11y bug in the wild; rule misses it. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 3.3.2 + 4.1.2. Cross-refs Q5-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED (closed, different shape) and Q4-LABELS-REQUIRED-VS-LABEL-ADJACENT-OVERLAP (open dedup policy).
- [x] **Q6-FINDINGID-COLLISION-SAMEFILE-SAMELINE** `navigation/link-descriptive-text` emits both "not descriptive" AND "N duplicates" findings on the same anchor elements at the same file:line — 8 findings for 4 links on `super-simple-site/index.html:14-17`, with same `findingId` hash (`87dd7b97d2eb`) appearing twice at L14. Both findings are legitimate (the rule satisfies 2.4.4 for descriptiveness AND 2.4.9 for duplicate names), but the `findingId` collision breaks the agent's dedup and the suppress-by-findingId flow. Root cause: the hash input excludes the rule's sub-variant discriminator ("not descriptive" vs "duplicates"). Fix: include the variant key (e.g. `reasonKind`/`messageKey`) in the `findingId` hash computation in `src/engine/rule-runner.ts` so two findings with distinct sub-variants get distinct ids. Add a cross-invariant test: `new Set(violations.map(v => v.findingId)).size === violations.length` per file-level response. Doctrine: the `findingId` is a load-bearing primary key for the suppress + dedup flows.
- [x] **Q6-ICON-FONT-HIDDEN-GROUP-DEDUP-CLASS-PATTERN** `aria/icon-font-hidden` emits 180 findings on sb-admin — the bulk are identical `<i class="fa fa-*">` patterns inside sibling buttons. `groupKey` dedup operates at the per-element level but doesn't collapse structurally-identical occurrences within one file. Per surface-don't-suppress, the candidates stay visible — but a per-site concentration summary (`perRuleCoverage[ruleId].concentration: { file, count, classPattern }`) would let the agent triage 180 into "one fix pattern, applied site-wide." Fix: compute per-file-per-class-pattern concentration on rules that fire >N× on one file with identical `class` evidence; emit `concentration` as a meta field, not a filter. Pairs with V1-NOISE-RULE-PER-FILE-ROLLUP (same shape, different rule family). Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2 (unchanged scope).
- [x] **Q6-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT** Three fields re-emit the same "no config found" signal: `configSource: null`, `configSearchedFrom: "<path>"`, `configNote: "No ra11y.config found at … — using built-in defaults"` (200-char boilerplate). All three appear together on every response when no config exists; `configNote` duplicates what the warning + `configSource: null` already say, and the 200-char repetition inflates response size on tiny-repo scans. Fix: collapse to `configSource: null` (present on every response) + `configSearchedFrom: <path>` (present-when-meaningful — omit when identical to cwd); drop `configNote` as a user-facing field, or demote to `_meta.configNote` for debugging only. Pairs with Q-SHARED-NO-CONFIG-WARNING-TINY-REPO (whether the warning itself should fire). Doctrine: present-when-meaningful.
- [x] **Q6-CONTRAST-INLINE-STYLE-REGRESSION-AUDIT** Evidence: `vteam.../index.html:89 <strong style="color:#FFF317">` (1.17:1 yellow-on-white), `street-life/index.html:13 <h1>Street <span style="color:#FFEA00">Life</span></h1>` (1.25:1). `perRuleCoverage.remediation` still says `"add CSS source files or additionalPaths"` — wrong guidance when the color lives in `style=""`. `Q-SHARED-INLINE-STYLE-CONTRAST` (marked [x]) prescribed an inline-style evaluation path, but symptoms reproduce on this scan. Three possibilities: (1) closure shipped but this subprocess is serving a pre-closure bundle (check Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT-SUBPROCESS-RACE), (2) closure was narrower than the spec (only `<style>` blocks, not `style=""` attrs), (3) closure covered HTML scans but per-rule coverage text wasn't updated to match. Investigation path: `git log src/rules/contrast/minimum.ts` + `git log src/input/parsers/html.ts`; confirm against a fresh-subprocess `scan_file` on `vteam.../index.html`. Fix the smaller delta revealed. Add a real-world fixture `tests/fixtures/real-world/inline-style-contrast-yellow-on-white/` asserting the rule fires on `<strong style="color:#FFF317">Text</strong>`. Satisfies wcag22:1.4.3.
- [x] **Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE** No cross-template pattern dedupe at scale. `<button class="navbar-toggle" data-toggle="collapse">` appears in ≥8 sibling template dirs and emits 8 distinct `findingId`/`groupKey` values per rule per template. At 174 templates × 3 rules firing on this vendor-snippet = ~500 findings from one canonical pattern copied across the catalog. Agents have no affordance for "dismiss this pattern across all siblings" — each finding gets a distinct id. Fix: add `patternId` as a sibling field on each finding, computed as `hash(ruleId + canonicalize(snippet))` where canonicalize strips attribute order, trivial whitespace, unique id/class tokens, and template/SSG interpolation placeholders. Agents can bulk-dismiss or bulk-fix by `patternId` across sibling template copies. Surface-don't-suppress: findings still appear individually; `patternId` is additive affordance, not a filter. Pairs with Q6-ICON-FONT-HIDDEN-GROUP-DEDUP-CLASS-PATTERN (closed, single-file pattern rollup) — this item is the cross-file sibling. Satisfies wcag22 all.
- [x] **Q6-CATALOG-REPO-SIBLING-HINT** `scan_project` at scale (174 top-level sibling dirs, each carrying its own `index.html` + `css/` + `js/`) treats the whole tree as one flat repo — `detectedFramework: "jekyll"`, `parseErrorFileCount: 501`, `opaqueCustomComponentNames: ["AG.y","B",...]` are all at-scale fallout. Angle-2 confirmed per-subdir scans DO recover signal (per-template `rulesFired`, `parseErrorFileCount` 2-6 instead of 501, `opaqueCustomComponentNames` collapses to 0-1). Add a meta-signal: when the root has ≥N (say 5) top-level siblings each carrying a recognizable site-entry-point shape (`index.html` + sibling `css/` or `images/` or `assets/`), emit `meta.catalogHint: { topLevelSiblings: N, exampleSiblings: [names] }` + a `nextStep` suggestion: "this looks like a catalog of N sibling sites; consider `scan_project({ cwd: \"<subdir>\" })` for per-site signal." Pairs with Q6-NEXTSTEP-AVOIDS-VENDOR-CSS (closed) — same routing shape, different trigger. Doctrine: "One tool call should answer 'what next?'" — catalog-shape detection gives the agent the right second call.
- [x] **Q6-FRAMEWORK-DETECT-JEKYLL-FALSE-POSITIVE** `meta.detectedFramework: "jekyll"` fires at scale because a stray `_config.yml` at the root triggers the Jekyll detection. No `_layouts/`, no `_includes/`, no Gemfile, no `Gemfile.lock`. Single-filename evidence is insufficient. Fix (tighten heuristic in `src/mcp/framework-detection.ts` or equivalent): the Jekyll detection requires `_config.yml` AND at least one of (a) `_layouts/` dir, (b) `_includes/` dir, (c) a Gemfile mentioning `jekyll`, (d) a top-level `_posts/` or `_drafts/` dir. When only `_config.yml` is present, emit `frameworkCandidate: "jekyll" (weak)` as a candidate rather than a confident detection, OR omit the detection entirely and let the agent inspect. Per-subdir scans (angle-2 confirmed) correctly emit `detectedFramework: null` because no `_config.yml` is present in the subdirs. Pairs with Q3-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-STARLIKE-MISDETECT (closed) — same class of over-confident detection from weak evidence.
- [x] **Q6-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-MINIFIED-JS-REGRESSION** (e8e1a7d7) `opaqueCustomComponentNames: ["AG.y","B","H.length","J","J.length","Math.abs"]` on a at-scale full scan. `Q6-AUTODETECT-WRAPPERS-LEAKS-JS-IDENTIFIER-NOISE` (marked [x]) restricted wrapper extraction to JSX/TSX tag positions with active unit-test coverage (`tests/unit/mcp/analysis-coverage.test.ts`), but symptoms reproduce. Root cause is most likely the **reopened** Q-SHARED-TSX-PARSER-FALSE-JSX-CONTEXTS (now `[~]` above): 501 `.js` files routed through the TSX fallback error-parse, contributing fake tag positions to the wrapper extractor that the JSX-tag-position filter then accepts as legitimate. Two-layer fix: (a) the upstream TSX-parser fix stops `.js` from entering JSX mode at all — closes most of the bleed; (b) belt-and-braces, wrapper extraction must skip files in `parseErrorFiles` AND `scannedBuildArtifacts` regardless of how they got there. Add an invariant test: `for every name in opaqueCustomComponentNames, the source file must not be in parseErrorFiles`. Pairs with Q3-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-TOP-EMPTY-DISHONEST (closed, single-file shape) and Q3-BOOTSTRAP-WRAPPER-DETECT-EMPTY-REASON (closed, MDX/Astro case). Track this item against (b) specifically; (a) lands via the parent TSX item.
- [x] **Q6-CONTRAST-VENDOR-CSS-CROSS-FILE-DEDUPE** `contrast/minimum` fires 5458× across a at-scale scan, with the vast majority on vendor `bootstrap.css` / `animate.css` duplicated across 100+ template subdirs. One fix to `bootstrap.css` in one subdir does not propagate; the agent has no affordance to say "this is the same finding as N siblings." Add per-basename cross-file dedupe: when the same `(selector, declaration)` pair in files sharing the same basename (`bootstrap.css`) fires the same rule across sibling dirs, collapse to one canonical finding with `vendorOccurrences: [{ path, line }]` sibling list. Pairs with Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open, element-level) and Q6-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-GROUP-BY-BASENAME (closed, meta-level). Surface-don't-suppress: findings still enumerable via the occurrences list; the headline is deduped. Satisfies wcag22:1.4.3.
- [x] **Q6-SUGGEST-FIX-MECHANICAL-VS-GUIDANCE-DRIFT** `suggest_fix` returns `kind: "guidance"` on `navigation/href-javascript-void` findings at scale despite the scan's `plan.fixesByClass.mechanical` counting them as mechanical (and the rule's docs advertise a trivial `<button type="button">` replacement). Cross-surface contradiction: the plan says "mechanical," the fix call says "guidance." Root cause candidates: (1) `suggest_fix` degrades mechanical → guidance when the specific finding's replacement text depends on surrounding context it can't statically resolve; (2) the fix handler reads a different class lookup than the plan. Fix: when `suggest_fix(ruleId, file, line)` can produce a well-formed `newText` for a rule the scan-plan counts as mechanical, return `kind: "edit"` with populated `newText`; when the context makes the replacement ambiguous, emit `kind: "guidance"` BUT also emit `meta.mechanicalInPrinciple: true` so the agent knows the rule family supports a mechanical path. Pairs with Q-SHARED-SUGGEST-FIX-GUIDANCE-PRIMARY (closed) — different fix call shape. Doctrine: cross-surface consistency.

### Considered and rejected (per CLAUDE.md §1)

- **Auto-exclude `bootstrap.css` / `font-awesome.css` / `jquery*.js` via `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_PATTERNS`** → rejected per §1 "Default-exclude globs are suppression too." Vendor-bundle findings ARE real findings — contrast in `bootstrap.css` is a real contrast issue on the rendered page, just not one the consumer edits. The existing `scannedBuildArtifacts` label + proposed rollups (Q6-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-GROUP-BY-BASENAME + Q6-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-PER-FILE-AGGREGATION) give the agent the signal without discarding the findings. Pairing with the build-artifact-aware next-step rerouting (Q6-NEXTSTEP-AVOIDS-VENDOR-CSS) closes the "agent lands on unactionable file" failure mode.
- **Default-downgrade vendor findings to `severity: "note"`** → rejected per §1 "Don't downgrade priority to hide things." Severity is for sorting; a downgrade hides real contrast violations from agents that filter by severity. The `scannedBuildArtifacts` membership is the honest label — agents decide triage; the tool does not pre-decide severity.

---

## Track Q7 — Real-world OSS field test

Owner: main session + general-purpose. Source: 2026-04-24 multi-repo OSS field test — 20 scan agents (5 waves × 4 anonymized open-source codebases) probing detection accuracy, false-negative coverage, checklist quality, fix-suggestion quality, and response-shape adherence to AI-first doctrine. Sites span four shapes: a CSS-framework + docs tree, an SSG-templated content site, a vanilla HTML/CSS/JS demo collection, and a bulk-template catalog (≈4k files). Wave 5 is the doctrine cross-check; Waves 1-4 are correctness/coverage.

Cross-cutting themes (≥3-of-4-site recurrence) drive the P0 items below. Items with strong overlap against prior tracks are marked as cross-track shared rather than re-listed. All accepted items are correctness, surface-don't-suppress, or shape-honesty fixes — no heuristic suppression accepted (re-affirmed against doctrine).

### v0.2.0 — accepted (P0 — shape honesty / silent-failure)

- [x] **Q7-SUGGEST-FIX-EDIT-LANE-UNREACHABLE** Despite scans reporting hundreds-to-thousands of `mechanical` findings (266 / 8 / 7 / 2036 across the four sites), every `suggest_fix(ruleId, file, line)` call across 32 attempts returned `kind: "guidance"` — never `kind: "edit"`. The mechanical lane the docstring promises is unreachable through the documented input shape. Either the rule fixers don't ship `oldText`/`newText` payloads, or `suggest_fix` ignores them. Fix: audit every rule whose `fixClass: "mechanical"` and confirm it emits `Violation.fix.{oldText,newText}` (or equivalent fix-path payload), then verify `suggest_fix` returns `kind: "edit"` with both fields populated when the inline data is present. Cross-surface contradiction with `plan.fixesByClass.mechanical` headline is the canonical dishonest-shape — agents budget against the headline and never reach the lane. Pairs with Q6-SUGGEST-FIX-MECHANICAL-VS-GUIDANCE-DRIFT (closed, contradiction shape) and Q-SHARED-SUGGEST-FIX-GUIDANCE-PRIMARY (closed, primary/alternatives gap).
- [ ] **V1-SUGGEST-FIX-MECHANICAL-LANE-REMAINING-RULES** (extends Q7-SUGGEST-FIX-EDIT-LANE-UNREACHABLE) Audit and wire kind: "edit" emission for remaining ~17 rules tagged fixClass: "mechanical": document/lang-attribute, media/alt-text-missing, plus ~15 others. Either populate Violation.fix.{oldText,newText} or re-tag fixClass to verifyInSource if the edit is content-dependent. Cross-references V1-FIX-LANG-AUTOCOMPLETE-ALT-MECHANICAL-DOWNGRADE (turn 6) and V1-SUGGEST-FIX-MECHANICAL-LANE-EMIT-EDIT (turn 7).
- [x] **Q7-SUGGEST-FIX-NONE-NEAREST-FINDING** `suggest_fix` returns `kind: "none"` when `(file, line)` doesn't match a known finding (paginated scans drift the line, agents lose the original line, rule renames). Today the response carries no `nearestFinding`, no `didYouMean`, no `lineHint` — dead-end response forcing the agent to re-scan. Fix: when `kind: "none"` would emit, walk the per-file findings within ±10 lines of the requested line; if exactly one same-rule finding sits in that window, attach `nearestFinding: { ruleId, line }`; if multiple, attach `didYouMean: [{ ruleId, line }]` (top 3). Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest — `kind:"none"` with no breadcrumb is the canonical dead-end shape.
- [ ] **Q7-SUGGEST-FIX-VENDOR-CONTEXT** `suggest_fix` proposes editing vendor files (third-party CSS frameworks, icon-font stylesheets, minified vendor JS bundles) directly — even when the file is already labeled in `scannedBuildArtifacts`. Half of the assessed cases on one bulk-template site landed inside vendor files; one suggestion proposed a CSS color edit inside a third-party stylesheet with a copyright banner; another proposed editing a minified template literal inside a packed vendor JS bundle (would corrupt the bundle). Fix: when the target file is in `scannedBuildArtifacts` OR matches a vendor-banner heuristic (top-of-file copyright + `min.css`/`min.js` infix), attach a structured `vendorContext: { signal, redirectTo: "consumer-override" }` field to the response; rewrite the primary fix to "override the failing selector in your own stylesheet (loaded after) — do not modify the vendor file"; demote the in-vendor-file edit to an alternative with explicit "vendor-file edit; consumer-override is preferred." Pairs with Q6-NEXTSTEP-AVOIDS-VENDOR-CSS (closed, file-routing axis) — this is the suggest_fix-content axis. Doctrine: surface-don't-suppress applies to the vendor finding; vendor-context labeling helps the agent route without losing the candidate.
- [x] **Q7-CHECKLIST-META-PARITY** `checklist` response routinely ships only `stale_mcp_subprocess` in `warnings` even when the same scan corpus had `scan_project` emitting `template_files_parsed_as_literal`, `parse_errors_present`, `extensions_skipped_no_parser`, `source_language_unsupported` — the checklist consumer in isolation has no idea the scan was degraded. On one bulk-template site `checklist` returned no `meta` key at all. Fix: when `checklist` is invoked against a previously-cached scan or a freshly-run scan, propagate the same `warnings[]` + `meta` block (configSource, scanned.root, rulesEvaluated, filesByExtension) that `scan_project` would emit on the same input. Cross-surface meta + warnings consistency is a doctrine invariant for tools sharing a scan basis. Pairs with Q4-WARNING-DETAILS-CROSS-SURFACE-UNIFY (closed, scan_project ↔ coverage axis) and Q-SHARED-PASS-RATE-COMPOSITE (closed). Doctrine: cross-surface drift forces wasted round trips.
- [x] **Q7-CRITERION-ID-FIELD-NAME-DRIFT** Same concept under two field names across tools: `checklist` items use `criterionId`, `coverage.failingAutomatedCriteria` entries use `id`. Same scan corpus, same concept, two field names — agents joining the two surfaces by criterion silently mis-match. Fix: pick one canonical name (`criterionId` matches the namespaced-id convention used elsewhere — `wcag22:1.4.3`); rename the other; accept the legacy name as a deprecated alias for one minor with a `warnings: ["deprecated_field_id_renamed_criterionId"]` annotation. Doctrine: cross-tool meta + warnings consistency.
- [ ] **Q7-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON-MISLABEL** `scannedBuildArtifacts[].reason: "minified"` shipped on hand-authored long-line files (template-literal islands inside JSX-flavored SSG sources, prop-bundle wrappers, SCSS function bodies with long type signatures) — the heuristic is dressed as a deterministic fact, the label is wrong. Q3-BUILD-ARTIFACT-SINGLE-LONG-LINE-SECOND-PROBE (closed) tightened the `minified` predicate; this item is the cross-cutting recurrence (4/4 sites observed mis-labeled entries on at least one file class). Fix: audit every `reason` token emitted by `src/mcp/build-artifacts.ts` against the doctrine "label provable from code" bar — drop reason-tokens whose predicate is heuristic; surface the underlying signal (line-length distribution, sourcemap-sibling presence, hashed-filename pattern) as additive evidence the agent can read without the deterministic-sounding label. Cross-ref Q4-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-REASON (closed) for the per-path classification reason field. Doctrine: heuristic-mislabeled meta sub-fields are dishonest.
- [x] **Q7-BYPRIORITY-ZERO-INFORMATION-FIELD** Closed as duplicate of V1-CHECKLIST-PRIORITY-AXIS-DEGENERATE. `summary.byPriority` is already fully removed from `src/mcp/tool-checklist.ts` (see drop-rationale comment around :443-450), and `tests/integration/mcp-tools.test.ts:1089` guards `expect(body.summary).not.toHaveProperty("byPriority")`. The earlier fix took path (b) (drop entirely) rather than path (a) (present-when-meaningful) because `priorityFor()` returned `"high"` uniformly across all A/AA criteria — even the conditionally-spread high-only variant would have shipped a degenerate single-key object. Per-candidate `confidence` is the honest signal-bearing axis. No new code change needed.
- [ ] **Q7-AAA-RULE-LOADER-SILENT-NORUN** (2/4 second-sweep targets) Rules whose `satisfies` list contains only AAA criteria silently fail to fire even when `level: 'AAA'` is requested. Concrete observation: an `<a href="..." target="_blank">` rule (registered in `list_rules`) was absent from `perRuleCoverage` on every scan; `meta.rulesEvaluated.loaded: 74` vs `list_rules` total of 75 — exact off-by-one. Either the level=AAA codepath does not load rules whose `satisfies` is AAA-only, or eligibility filtering elides them. Either way, the agent has no way to discover that a rule exists but isn't running. Fix: (a) audit the level=AAA loader codepath against `rule.satisfies` so AAA-only rules actually run when level:'AAA' is requested; (b) when a rule is loaded but skipped by level filtering, surface a structured `perRuleCoverage` entry with `skipReason: "gated_by_level", requiredLevel: "AAA", requestedLevel: "AA"` rather than silent omission. Doctrine: silent under-surfacing is the asymmetric failure mode; the agent reading the response cannot distinguish "rule clean" from "rule never ran." Add a regression test scanning a fixture and asserting the AAA-only rule fires when level:'AAA' is requested.
- [ ] **Q7-PARTIAL-PARSE-STRUCTURAL-RULE-DEGRADATION** (2/4) When a file enters `parseErrorFiles` / `partialParseFiles` state, structural rules that depend on full-document scope (`semantics/landmark-main`, `semantics/heading-hierarchy`, `document/page-titled`, `parsing/html-has-lang`, `document/lang-attribute`) silently return 0 findings — indistinguishable from "rule ran clean." The agent reading `perRuleCoverage` sees `findingsEmitted: 0` with no degradation signal. Fix: when a file is in `partialParseFiles`, structural rules either (a) run with a `coverageConfidence: "low"` decoration on the per-file `perRuleCoverage` entry, or (b) get explicitly listed with `skipReason: "partial_parse — likely impacted"`. Bites hardest on layout/include files which are precisely where landmark-main matters most. Pairs with Q7-PERRULECOVERAGE-EXTENSION-PRESENT-OUT-OF-SCOPE-SUBKIND (silent-low-confidence shape, different cause).
- [ ] **Q7-PLAN-VIOLATIONS-COMPOSITE** (1/4) `plan.violations: 323` ships as a top-level integer next to `plan.fixesByClass: { mechanical, guidance, runtimeOnly, verifyInSource }`. Sums categorically different lanes (8 mechanical + 244 verify-in-source + 63 guidance + 8 runtime-only = 323) — agents budget against the headline as if it were 323 actionable edits. Same shape as the `plan.totalFindings` precedent (deleted 2026-04-24) and `plan.safeEditsAvailable` (deleted 2026-04-24). Fix: drop `plan.violations` as a top-level integer; `plan.fixesByClass` is the structured tally. The plan summary prose echoes the same number — same fix applies there. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest. Pairs with Q-SHARED-SAFE-EDITS-VS-MECHANICAL-DISAGREEMENT (closed precedent).
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-PASS-RATE-COMPOSITE** (1/4) `checklist.summary.automatedCoverage.automatedCriteriaPassRate: 45` is a single composite number that mixes "rule fired clean" with "rule never had eligible inputs" with "rule found violations." Pass-rate composites that conflate ran-clean with no-eligible-inputs misrepresent coverage. Fix: drop the composite headline; the structured `meta.perRuleCoverage` already distinguishes `coverageConfidence: "high"` with `findingsEmitted: 0` from `coverageConfidence: "low"` with `reason: "no files matching ..."`. If a rate is wanted, split into two counters (`criteriaWithRulesAllClean` + `criteriaWithoutEligibleInputs`) — split rather than summed. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest.
- [ ] **Q7-PERRULECOVERAGE-EMPTY-ELIGIBLE-COLLAPSE** (3/4) Project-level scans ship `perRuleCoverage` with full entries — including remediation prose — for rules where `filesEvaluated: 0` AND `filesEligible: 0`. On HTML-only or CSS-light scans, ~30 of ~70 entries fit this shape, each ~200 chars of boilerplate. Concrete observation on a single-subdir scope: 347KB response was dominated by these entries. Fix: collapse rules with `filesEvaluated: 0` AND `filesEligible: 0` into a single `meta.rulesNotEvaluatedDueToInputType: { count: N, byExtension: {".css": 12, ".tsx": 8, ...} }` entry; keep the full `perRuleCoverage` shape only for rules that had eligible inputs. The agent reads the by-extension counter once and decides whether to widen scope. Doctrine: verbose meta is signal, but boilerplate repeated across rules with zero eligible inputs is not telemetry. Pairs with Q7-RESPONSE-TOKEN-BUDGET-DETAIL (different lever; this is the upstream payload reduction).
- [ ] **Q7-DETECT-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-EMPTY-REASON-DISCRIMINATOR** (1/4) `detect_native_wrappers` returns `emptyReason: "no-jsx-onclick-candidates-found-but-opaque-components-present"` on a pure-HTML codebase containing zero JSX/TSX files (verified: zero `.tsx`/`.jsx`/`.svelte`/`.vue`). The discriminator-sounding token then propagates a wrong `nextStep` ("open the opaque components listed under analysisCoverage.opaqueCustomComponentNames") against a list that doesn't exist for this codebase. Fix: compute `emptyReason` from `filesByExtension` first — when there are zero entries for `.tsx/.jsx/.svelte/.vue`, emit `no-parseable-files`; when JSX/TSX files exist but none have PascalCase component usage, emit `no-pascalcase-components`; only emit `no-jsx-onclick-candidates-found-but-opaque-components-present` when scanned files DID contain PascalCase components. Add a fixture covering pure-HTML projects to the regression suite. Doctrine: heuristic-mislabeled meta sub-fields are dishonest; an `emptyReason` that lies misroutes the agent silently.

### v0.2.0 — accepted (P1 — shape honesty)

- [x] **Q7-RESPONSE-TOKEN-BUDGET-DETAIL** `warnings: ["response_token_budget_truncated"]` fires on bulk scans but the structured detail (`warningsDetails`) is bare — `requestedLimit: 25 → effectiveLimit: 5` doesn't explain *which* finding was the budget-blower (a long fix description, a wide snippet, a deep `criteria` array). Fix: extend `warningsDetails.response_token_budget_truncated` with `{ requestedLimit, effectiveLimit, reason: "fix_description_long" | "criteria_breadth" | "snippet_width" | "meta_array_size", topContributorRule?, topContributorByteCount? }` so the agent knows whether to retry with a narrower scope or a different surface. Pairs with Q-SHARED-LIMIT-REQUEST-VS-EFFECTIVE (closed, the `effectiveLimit` surface axis).
- [x] **Q7-SCAN-ONE-FILE-PER-PAGE-PATHOLOGY** Bulk-template scans paginated with `limit: 25` returned exactly 1 file per page on multiple sites — `totalFilesWithFindings: 1793` translates to 1793 sequential calls. The token-density cap (V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET-DENSITY-2) is doing its job, but at scale the per-call throughput hits a degenerate floor. Fix: when the density cap clips a page to ≤ 2 files AND `totalFilesWithFindings > 100`, emit a structured `nextStep` that nudges toward `findings_by_rule` or a per-rule narrowing (`scan_project({ ruleIds: [topRuleId] })`) rather than continuing to page file-by-file. Pairs with V1-TOOL-FINDINGS-BY-RULE (open) — that item adds the `findings_by_rule` primitive; this item adds the routing hint.
- [ ] **Q7-LANDMARK-MAIN-REASON-IDENTICAL** `semantics/landmark-main` reason text is identical across every fire on a single scan (18 fires, one identical sentence). Fix: enrich with file-context evidence ("document has only `<body>` root with no landmark elements; a single `<main>` wrapping primary content would establish landmark structure for AT" plus the body's child-element kinds — "body contains [n] sibling sections / divs / forms"). Reason-text enrichment only — candidate stays in primary list. Pairs with the broader doctrine that reason text should encode dismissal/triage signal per-finding, not boilerplate.
- [x] **Q7-NAVIGATION-HREF-RULE-RENAME** `navigation/href-javascript-void` rule ID is misleading: the rule fires on `href="javascript:void(0)"` (the literal name implies) AND on `href="#"` (placeholder href, no fragment target). On `href="#"` the message reads "no fragment target" — correct, but the rule ID suggests the JS-scheme variant. Fix: split into two rules — `navigation/href-javascript-scheme` (matches `href="javascript:..."`) and `navigation/href-empty-fragment` (matches `href="#"` and `href=""`) — both `satisfies wcag22:2.1.1 + 4.1.2`. Migration: ship the new IDs alongside the old for one minor with the legacy ID emitting a `warnings: ["deprecated_rule_id_navigation_href_javascript_void"]` annotation. Doctrine: rule IDs that mislead the agent triaging by ID are dishonest. Pairs with Q6-RULE-HREF-JAVASCRIPT-VOID (closed, the rule landing).
- [ ] **Q7-DUPLICATE-INPUT-SIBLING-COLLAPSE** `forms/labels-required` (and any input-shape rule) emits N findings on N visually-grouped inputs sharing identical class+type+attributes inside one parent (one-time-code clusters, quiz radio-button sets, day-of-week checkbox sets). Six identical OTP-digit inputs produced six findings with identical `groupKey`. When ≥3 input siblings share `(class, type, attributes-modulo-id)` inside one parent, collapse to ONE finding with `siblingInstances: [{ line, id? }]`. Surface-don't-suppress applies — the consolidated finding carries the full sibling list; agent reads one row, not N. Pairs with Q4-LIKELY-REDUCIBLE-SIBLING-IMAGE-DEDUPE (closed, image variant) + Q5-OTP-INPUT-CLUSTER-DETECTION (closed, OTP review finder) — those address the cluster-detection axis; this is the rule-emission dedupe axis.
- [ ] **Q7-FIXDESCRIPTIONREF-PAGINATION-DICT** `fixDescriptionRef.hash` saves bytes via shared-hash dedup, but the `fixDescriptions` lookup table is only inlined on the page where the first finding using each hash appears. Out-of-order paged retrieval (offset-jump, parallel page fetch) sees `fixDescriptionRef` referencing a hash that isn't in the response's lookup. Fix: every paginated response carries the full lookup table for hashes referenced by *that page's* findings (compute the union per page); duplicate-content cost is bounded by per-page hash count, never larger than re-inlining. Cross-ref V1-REF-DEDUPE (closed) and Q-SHARED-FIXDESCREF-SAME-GROUP-INLINE-DEDUPE (closed).
- [ ] **Q7-MOTION-FINDING-SELECTOR-LINE** `motion/pause-stop-hide` cites the line where the `animation:` declaration appears, but the underlying violation is the entire ruleset (the selector at the rule's opening line is the structural anchor). One observed case: an icon-spinner selector opens at line 80; unprefixed `animation:` declaration at line 84; finding cites line 84 — the agent reading the file sees the declaration but the selector context (which determines whether the rule is interaction-triggered or auto-playing) is 4 lines up. Fix: for selector-scoped findings, report the rule selector start line as `line`, the declaration line as `decline`. Pairs with J-1 (open, the spec-correctness narrowing question) — independent of that.
- [ ] **Q7-FRAGMENT-FILE-HEADING-HIERARCHY** (3/4 second-sweep targets) `semantics/heading-hierarchy` "Document has no `<h1>`" branch fires on partial / fragment files: component fragments without root `<html>`/`<body>`/`<main>` (rendered inside parent layouts), content-fragment files with front-matter (H1 supplied by the build-time layout), and partial includes under conventional partials directories (`_includes/`, `_layouts/`, `partials/`, `components/`). Treating each parseable file as a standalone document is a category error for any template/SSG/component-framework repository — observed across three target shapes. Fix: detect "fragment files" via either (a) absence of top-level `<html>`/`<body>`/`<head>` root, (b) presence of front-matter delimiter block, OR (c) path matching conventional partials globs. Either skip the no-h1 branch on fragments OR demote to info severity with reason "fragment file; assumes parent supplies `<h1>`." Pairs with Q7-RULE-LANDMARK-MAIN-FRAGMENT-SCOPE (same scope tightening, different rule). Same scope-tightening should propagate to any structural rule that asserts top-level outline shape.
- [ ] **Q7-MARKDOWN-FENCED-CODE-BLOCK-SCOPE** (1/4 — high volume: 800+ findings on one site) DOM-shape rules (`navigation/href-placeholder`, `navigation/link-descriptive-text`, `aria/expanded-on-disclosure`) evaluate HTML inside ` ```html ... ``` ` fenced code blocks in markdown / MDX as live markup. The hint string "Markdown files parsed as HTML residue" is in the response, but rule fires concentrate in MDX docs files where the HTML inside fenced blocks is *prose for the reader*, not rendered DOM. On one target this dominated the high-frequency rule counts (single rule emitted 801 findings; another 608). Fix: in the MDX/markdown ingest path, exclude content nested in ` ``` `-fenced code blocks from rule evaluation. Live MDX expressions (`<Example code={`...`} />` template strings) are similarly literal-not-live; same treatment. Doctrine: don't duplicate capability the agent already has — the agent reading the file distinguishes fenced blocks from live JSX in one read. Pairs with Q-SHARED-TSX-PARSER-FALSE-JSX-CONTEXTS (parser-side fix) and Q7-HTML-SHAPE-RULES-GATE-NON-JSX-JS (rule-side gate) — this is the markdown ingest variant.
- [ ] **Q7-OPAQUE-COMPONENT-HEAD-DELEGATION** (1/4) `document/page-titled` and `document/charset-first-1024-bytes` fire on layout files whose `<head>` is delegated to opaque PascalCase components (`<Head title={title} />` or similar). The component is opaque to the scanner; the rule sees no literal `<title>`/`<meta>` and asserts they're missing. Symmetrical to Q7-RULE-LANDMARK-MAIN-FRAGMENT-SCOPE for the head-metadata rule family. Fix: when `<head>` contains only opaque PascalCase components and zero literal `<title>`/`<meta>`, demote severity to info with reason "head delegated to opaque component `<Name>`; verify `<title>` is set inside the component." Same family covers `document/lang-attribute` when `<html>` is itself wrapped in an opaque component. Doctrine: don't claim absence-of-evidence as evidence-of-absence when an opaque component is the named child.
- [ ] **Q7-OPAQUE-COMPONENT-SKIP-LINK** (1/4) `navigation/skip-link` predicate requires literal `<nav>` in-file; layouts that compose nav inside an opaque PascalCase component (`<Header />`, `<Topbar />`, `<Sidebar />`, `<AppBar />`, `<Chrome />`) silently pass the predicate even though the rendered page does have navigation that needs a skip link. Concrete observation: `BaseLayout` renders `<body>` → `<Header />` → `<main>` directly with no skip link before `<Header />`; rule fires zero times. Symmetrical bug to the head-delegation false-positive: same opaque-component blind spot, opposite direction (false-negative here, false-positive there). Fix: when a layout file's first `<body>` child is an opaque PascalCase component named like `/header|nav|chrome|topbar|appbar|sidebar/i`, treat as "navigation present, opaque" and apply the skip-link predicate (or emit info-severity candidate with reason "first `<body>` child is opaque component `<Name>`; verify a skip link is the first focusable descendant"). Pairs with Q7-OPAQUE-COMPONENT-HEAD-DELEGATION + Q7-RULE-LANDMARK-MAIN-FRAGMENT-SCOPE (same opaque-component cluster).
- [ ] **Q7-SKIP-LINK-NESTED-NAV-DETECTOR** (1/4) `navigation/skip-link`'s "primary nav" detector misses the `<body><header><nav>...</nav></header>...</body>` pattern (nav nested one level inside header). Concrete observation: rule fired on a sibling page with top-level `<body><nav>` shape but produced zero findings on a page with `<body><header><nav>` shape — same SC 2.4.1 obligation, different DOM shape. Fix: loosen the primary-nav detector to include `<nav>` directly inside `<body>` OR one level inside `<body>`'s first `<header>` child. Equivalently: when no skip-link-shaped anchor precedes the first `<nav>` on the page (regardless of nesting depth ≤2), fire the warning. Add a fixture asserting the rule fires on the nested shape.
- [ ] **Q7-PARTIAL-PARSE-PARSED-THROUGH-LINE** (1/4) `partialParseFiles[]` entries ship `{ path, parser, reason }` but no indication of WHERE parsing stopped — e.g. `parsedThroughLine: N` or a byte/char range. The agent has no way to bound trust in per-rule findings on the file: the rule may have visited the first 5 lines or the first 500. Without the stop point, the agent must read every partial-parse file in full to verify coverage, defeating the static-scanner premise. Fix: emit `parsedThroughLine: N` (or `parsedThroughByte`/`parsedThroughOffset`) on every `partialParseFiles` entry. 12 partial-parse files on one observed scan — non-trivial fraction. Pairs with Q7-PARTIAL-PARSE-STRUCTURAL-RULE-DEGRADATION (different lever; this is metadata, that is rule-emission shape).
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-FINDING-STRUCTURED-EVIDENCE-FIELDS** (1/4 — high volume) High-density rules bury the discriminating evidence in prose: `semantics/list-structure` (59 fires) does not name `offendingChildTag` / `offendingChildClass` as structured fields, so an agent triaging cannot distinguish "intentional `<hr>`-as-divider sibling under `<ul>`" from "real list misnesting" without reading source. `aria/expanded-on-disclosure` (169 fires) does not surface which predicate-branch fired (`branch.kind: "data-toggle" | "class-token" | ...`). For any rule whose finding density is >50, expose the discriminating evidence as a structured field, not buried in the prose `reason` text. Fix: audit the high-density rules, add `evidence: { offendingChildTag?, offendingChildClass?, predicateBranch? }` substructure to their findings; promote the discriminator from prose. Doctrine: agents read structured fields fast; prose is a fallback. Pairs with Q7-LANDMARK-MAIN-REASON-IDENTICAL (different rule, same structural-evidence pattern).
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-CANDIDATE-SNIPPET-ANCHOR** (2/4) `checklist` candidates ship `snippet` containing the surrounding paragraph or a fixed-width window around the line, NOT the literal sentence/clause containing the matched evidence. Concrete observations: (a) sensory-characteristics finder cites line N with phrase "button below" but `snippet` shows a *different* sentence on the same line (the matcher knew the offset but didn't anchor the snippet to it); (b) sponsor-image finder ships ~80-char window when only one image matched. Agent must search the file to find the actual match — the location pointer's snippet contradicts the location's claim. Fix: anchor `line` and `snippet` at the literal occurrence of the matched evidence (the matcher already knows the byte offset; emit it). When multiple matches exist in one file, emit one candidate per match. Pairs with Q6-MINIFIED-FILE-SNIPPET-COLUMN-ENRICHMENT (closed, vendor-snippet variant) — same shape, different cause.
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-CANDIDATE-PREDICATE-FALSE-EVIDENCE** (1/4) `color/meaning-by-color-only` review finder ships reason text "no visible text, icon, or aria-label" on elements where text is demonstrably present (`<span class="badge bg-success">New</span>` — text "New" is the badge body). The predicate checks for *external* sibling text rather than the element's own innerText. False reason text trains the agent to mistrust the candidate even on real misuse: the agent reads "no visible text," sees clear text, dismisses, and the same dismissal pattern propagates to legitimate cases. Fix: predicate must read the element's own normalized textContent first; reason text should reflect what's actually there ("badge text \"New\" is generic; verify a status word would help colorblind users" vs "no visible text"). Same shape applies to any predicate whose reason text is computed without consulting innerText. Pairs with Q7-RULE-COLOR-TEXT-CONTAINMENT-CHECK (rule-side fix; this is the finder-side recurrence).
- [ ] **Q7-DETECT-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-INLINE-OPAQUE-LIST** (1/4) When `detect_native_wrappers` returns `emptyReason: "no-jsx-onclick-candidates-found-but-opaque-components-present"`, the response does NOT include `opaqueCustomComponentNames` inline — the agent must call `scan_project` first to get the list, then come back. Two-call dance. The detector already walked the AST to count them; surfacing the names costs nothing. Fix: when `emptyReason` references opaque components, INCLUDE the `opaqueCustomComponentNames` array inline in the response. Doctrine: one tool call should answer "what next?" — if the next call's input is data this call already computed, ship it. Pairs with Q7-DETECT-NATIVE-WRAPPERS-EMPTY-REASON-DISCRIMINATOR (the discriminator correctness; this is the data-co-location).
- [ ] **Q7-SCAN-FILE-PERRULECOVERAGE-EXTENSION-SCOPE** (3/4) `scan_file` returns a `perRuleCoverage` array with 70+ entries; roughly half say `coverageConfidence: "low"` / `reason: "no files matching .css were scanned"` (when the scanned file is `.html`) or analogous for `.tsx`/`.js` etc. The agent passing `path.html` already knows the extension — telling them "no CSS files were scanned" on a single-`.html`-file call is structural noise. For a fix-verify loop where the agent edits → scans → re-edits repeatedly, this baseline overhead dwarfs the actionable content (response routinely exceeds 80,000 chars on a small file). Fix: on `scan_file`, restrict `perRuleCoverage` to rules whose eligible inputs match the scanned file's extension(s); collapse the rest into a single `rulesSkippedExtensionMismatch: N` counter. Sharpen the existing `verboseMeta: false` contract to gate `perRuleCoverage` (currently it only gates `analysisCoverage`). Pairs with Q7-PERRULECOVERAGE-EMPTY-ELIGIBLE-COLLAPSE (project-scan variant).
- [ ] **Q7-PERRULECOVERAGE-EXTENSION-PRESENT-OUT-OF-SCOPE-SUBKIND** (1/4) `perRuleCoverage` entries with `coverageConfidence: "low"` conflate two different gaps: (a) `extension-absent` — the scan tree contains zero files of this extension (current message is correct); (b) `extension-present-but-out-of-scope` — files of this extension exist at the cwd but `additionalPaths`/`exclude` pruned them out (current message wrongly steers the agent toward "add additionalPaths for compiled output" when the actual issue is "narrow additionalPaths excluded source"). Fix: differentiate via `subkind: "extension-absent" | "extension-present-but-out-of-scope"`; emit `(b)` with remediation "files of this extension exist at cwd but were pruned by `additionalPaths`/`exclude`; rerun with broader scope or unset the path filter." Doctrine: present-when-meaningful structured data; agent decides remediation from the structured discriminator, not by guessing the right call.
- [ ] **Q7-WARNINGS-DETAILS-SCHEMA-DISCIPLINE** (1/4) `warnings: string[]` declared 8 codes (`stale_mcp_subprocess`, `no_config_found`, `template_files_parsed_as_literal`, `scanned_build_artifacts_present`, `extensions_skipped_no_parser`, `parse_errors_present`, `source_language_unsupported`, `response_token_budget_truncated`) but `warningsDetails` carried payload for only 1 (`response_token_budget_truncated`). The other 7 sit in "I have a problem but no payload" state — agent has no way to know which extensions were skipped, which language was unsupported, etc. Asymmetric discipline: half the codes are paired with details, half are not. Fix: pick one shape — either (a) every warning code in `warnings[]` has a corresponding key in `warningsDetails` (use `{}` for codes that genuinely have no further detail), or (b) restructure `warnings` to `Array<{ code, details? }>` where details is present-when-meaningful. The current free-floating string-list with selective-details map is a typed-poorly union. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest.
- [ ] **Q7-RESPONSE-TOKEN-BUDGET-SORT-ORDER** (1/4) `warnings: ["response_token_budget_truncated"]` ships `warningsDetails: { requestedLimit: 25, effectiveLimit: 5 }` but does NOT document the sort order on truncation. Agent paginating doesn't know if `nextOffset: 5` orders by alphabetical / finding-density / severity; with N files the page-walk strategy depends on the order. Fix: extend `warningsDetails.response_token_budget_truncated` with `sortOrder: "<order>"` so the agent's page-walk strategy is informed. Pairs with Q7-RESPONSE-TOKEN-BUDGET-DETAIL (different metadata axis: that's the budget-blower; this is the iteration order).
- [ ] **Q7-CATALOG-SCAN-GROUPBY-DIRECTORY** (1/4) Bulk-template repos with N parallel sub-project subdirs at the top level have no first-class workflow for "scan all sub-projects but aggregate per-sub-project." Current options: (a) one whole-tree scan that exceeds token budget and conflates findings across templates; (b) N hand-scoped `scan_project` calls with `additionalPaths` set to each sub-project (174 round trips, 174 separate result blobs to merge); (c) per-file `scan_file` in a loop (loses rule-coverage telemetry's per-project meaning). Fix: add `groupBy: "directory" | "firstChildDir" | "extension"` to `scan_project`; on `groupBy: "firstChildDir"`, the response carries `summary.byGroup: { "<dir>": { violations: N, mostCommonRule, ... }, ... }` — one response with N rows. The full `files[]` payload can be lazily fetched per group via follow-up calls (offset/limit already supports paging — extend with group filter). Pairs with Q6-CATALOG-REPO-SIBLING-HINT (closed, the catalog *detection*) — this is the *aggregation* response shape. Doctrine-aligned: additive surface, no bucket-then-filter.
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-PERCRITERION-CLIPPED-HEADLINE** (1/4) When `perCriterionClipped: true`, summary headline `actionable: 2` reads as "2 things to verify" — but the actual unclipped count under each criterion can be 10+ (e.g. `maxCandidatesPerCriterion: 10` × 2 criteria = 20 unread, with more elided beyond the 10-cap). Composite headline that conflates "criteria with candidates" and "candidates" under one number. Fix: when `perCriterionClipped` is true, surface per-criterion uncapped count: `summary.actionable: { criteria: 2, candidatesUncapped: 24, candidatesReturned: 11 }`. Also: when `totalCandidates > limit*0.8`, emit `nextStepStructured: { tool: "checklist", args: { offset: <n>, limit: <n> } }` rather than today's generic "iterate items[]" prose. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest; clipped pages hidden behind a complete-sounding count is silent under-surfacing.
- [ ] **Q7-VENDOR-DISMISSAL-KEY-FINGERPRINT** (1/4) Logo-image candidates emit identical reason text on every brand mark — observed 174× on a bulk-template tree, every dismissal a fresh decision because attestation is per-criterion, not per-candidate-pattern. Same issue applies to any high-cardinality candidate family with structurally identical evidence (sponsor avatars, contributor lists, vendor-CSS contrast violations sharing one selector). Fix: compute `candidate.dismissalKey: hash(rule, filename-pattern, src-basename-pattern)` (or analogous discriminator); the agent records one verdict ("logo images are brand marks, dismissed under 1.4.5 logotype exemption") keyed on the hash, and subsequent matches auto-apply via `attest`. Workflow scaffolding rather than rule-suppression — fully consistent with surface-don't-suppress doctrine. Pairs with Q7-CHECKLIST-1.4.5-SR-ONLY-DEDUPE (in-page contiguous-range axis; this is the cross-page pattern axis).
- [ ] **Q7-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-CHILD-ONLY-CONTENT** (1/4) Heading and link rules that infer "empty" / "no accessible name" fire on elements whose only descendant is a template-directive interpolation (`{{...}}`, `<%= ... %>`, `{expr}`, etc.). Concrete observations: `<h2 itemprop="headline"><a href="{{ post.url }}">{{- post.title -}}</a></h2>` flagged by `semantics/empty-heading` and `navigation/link-descriptive-text` simultaneously — the rules say "empty" / "no accessible name," but the body is interpolated content the static parser cannot resolve. Fix: when the only descendant text node is a parsed template directive, demote severity to info OR rephrase to "heading/link content is interpolated (template directive); verify rendered output is non-empty/descriptive." Do not assert absence-of-content as a static fact when the static parser has no evidence of it. Pairs with Q7-FRAGMENT-FILE-HEADING-HIERARCHY (different angle on template/SSG content; both depend on reading the template-directive disposition correctly).

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P1 — rule correctness)

- [ ] **Q7-RULE-MOTION-DURATION-AND-TRIGGER-GATE** Cross-cutting theme #1 (4/4 sites). `motion/pause-stop-hide` fires on every CSS `animation:` / `transition:` regardless of duration or trigger; produced 8,940 / 55 / hundreds of findings on the four sites with most below 1s and many user-trigger-pseudo-class scoped. WCAG 2.2.2 normatively applies to motion lasting >5s. Fix: parse the duration from the shorthand, gate the rule's "violation" emission on parsed duration AND trigger evidence — `animation: ... infinite` OR `animation: ... [duration > 5s]` OR auto-playing carousel attribute (`data-bs-ride`, `data-ride`, JS-init slider classes `.flexslider`/`.camera_wrap`/`.sl-slider-wrapper`) emits at 2.2.2; transitions inside `:hover`/`:focus`/`:active` selectors OR animations with duration ≤5s and no infinite loop emit at 2.3.3 (Animation from Interactions, AAA). Encode the parsed duration in `reason` text as additive evidence ("animation duration 0.6s, trigger: user-pseudo-class :hover"). Build on Q3-RULE-PAUSE-STOP-INLINE-STYLE (closed, inline-style scanning) + Q5-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-SC-23-VS-22-CONFLATION (closed, two-lane split). Cross-cutting evidence: 4/4 sites in the 2026-04-24 sweep. Blocks J-1 resolution.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-EXPANDED-DISCLOSURE-NAMESPACE-AUDIT** Cross-cutting theme #2 (1 site, 169 findings — single-site recurrence but high volume warrants action). `aria/expanded-on-disclosure` overgeneralizes the toggle-attribute namespace: tooltips, popovers, and button-pressed are NOT disclosure widgets (popover/tooltip closed in Q3-RULE-POPOVER-NOT-DISCLOSURE + Q3-RULE-TOOLTIP-NOT-DISCLOSURE, but the broader audit is open); tabs use `aria-selected`, modals use `aria-haspopup="dialog"`. Fix: audit the disclosure-toggle predicate against the WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices §disclosure pattern — restrict to genuine disclosure shapes (`collapse`, `dropdown`, `offcanvas`, classList-toggle predicates with no role override) and document the exclusion list. Add separate companion rules for the tab-pattern (`aria/tab-pattern-roles`) and modal-pattern (`aria/modal-trigger-haspopup`) so the toggle-attribute namespace's other widget kinds get correct findings under the right axes.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-COLOR-TEXT-CONTAINMENT-CHECK** `color/meaning-by-color-only` fires on elements whose visible text already contains the status keyword (`<button class="btn btn-danger">Danger</button>`) — the rule's reason text contradicts itself when textContent literally is the status word. Q3-COLOR-MEANING-READS-TEXT-CONTENT (closed) added the textContent pre-check on the design-system axis; the cross-cutting recurrence (≥65 instances on one site, dozens on another) confirms the closure didn't propagate. Re-audit: when the element body's normalized textContent contains the status keyword associated with the color class (`text-danger` → `danger`/`error`/`alert`/...), emit the candidate at lower confidence with reason "text already carries status word — verify color is not the *sole* signal for users who cannot distinguish color"; when textContent is absent or doesn't carry the keyword, fire at default confidence.
