Pairs with Q3-RULE-COLOR-SOLE-INDICATOR (closed). Cross-cutting evidence: 2/4 sites in the 2026-04-24 sweep.
- [ ] **Q7-HTML-SHAPE-RULES-GATE-NON-JSX-JS** Cross-cutting theme #4 (1 site dominant — `.js` was 1808 of 4043 scanned files). HTML-shape rules (`document/iframe-title`, `navigation/href-javascript-void`, `tooltip/dismissable`, etc.) fire on JavaScript string-template literals inside packed plugins, treating raw substring matches as rendered DOM. Fix: gate every HTML-shape rule's `appliesTo` to `.html`/`.htm`/`.jsx`/`.tsx`/`.vue`/`.svelte` (DOM-origin) inputs only; emit on bare `.js` only when the parser identifies a JSX element node (not on regex/substring matches against string literals). Pairs with Q-SHARED-TSX-PARSER-FALSE-JSX-CONTEXTS (closed/reopened — that item is the parser-side fix; this is the rule-side gate). Belt-and-braces: even if the parser-fix lands cleanly, the rule-side gate on `appliesTo` extension is the durable invariant. Cross-cutting evidence: 1/4 sites at high volume.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-INLINE-STYLE-CONTRAST-WIDEN** `contrast/minimum` and `contrast/non-text` are gated on `.css`-file presence; HTML files with inline `style="color:#FFF317; background:#FFF"` declarations produce zero contrast findings. Q-SHARED-INLINE-STYLE-CONTRAST (closed) added the inline-`<style>` block path; the cross-cutting recurrence confirms `style=""` attribute scanning is still missing on multiple sites. Fix: synthesize a per-element style block from the parsed `style=` attribute and feed it into the contrast rules' CSS-rule pipeline (mirrors the inline-`<style>` closure). Add a real-world fixture asserting `<strong style="color:#FFF317">Text</strong>` on a white background fires `contrast/minimum`. Cross-ref Q6-CONTRAST-INLINE-STYLE-REGRESSION-AUDIT (closed). Cross-cutting evidence: 2/4 sites.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-MEDIA-PLACEHOLDER-DIMENSION-SRC** `media/alt-text-missing` (and any rule that quotes the `<img>` identifier) emits `<img>` `'700x400'` when the image src is `http://placehold.it/700x400` — the dimension token from the URL is treated as a meaningful identifier. Fix: when `src` filename matches `/^\d+x\d+$/`, has no extension, or is a known-placeholder host (`placehold.it`, `via.placeholder.com`, `picsum.photos/<dim>x<dim>`), drop the quoted identifier from the reason and add "src looks like a placeholder image" as additive context. Reason-enrichment only — candidate stays. Pairs with Q4-RULE-PLACEHOLDER-ALT (closed, placeholder alt text) — different axis: the alt is the issue elsewhere; here the reason text quotes the wrong identifier.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-LANDMARK-MAIN-FRAGMENT-SCOPE** `semantics/landmark-main` fires on demo / visual-test HTML pages (single-component test pages under `tests/visual/`, `examples/`, `demos/`) where the page is intentionally a one-component rendering with no surrounding chrome. Cross-cutting recurrence: dozens of identical fires on one site, every-page on another. Fix: when the scan source includes `scanned_build_artifacts_present` for the file's path tree OR the page's `<body>` contains ≤2 immediate-child elements (single component + maybe a script), surface the candidate with `couldBeWrongBecause: ["isolated_component_demo_page"]` rather than as a confident violation. Reason-enrichment + couldBeWrongBecause additive — surface stays. Pairs with Q3-RULE-NESTED-INTERACTIVE-ROLE-WRAPPER (closed, similar role-context narrowing).
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-ROLE-FROM-CLASS-CHILD-LABEL** `aria/role-from-class-only` fires on admonition wrappers (`<div class="note info"><h5>Tip</h5><p>...</p></div>`) where the immediate child `<h5>` carries the severity label — the rule claims "no severity word" but the child heading IS the label. Fix: when the flagged element's first child is a heading (`<h2>`-`<h6>`), check the heading's textContent against the severity-word dictionary; when present, treat it as supplying the wrapper's accessible label and demote the finding to a candidate with reason "wrapper's first child heading carries severity word — verify the visual/AT mapping rather than adding role." Pairs with Q4-RULE-NOTE-ROLE-FROM-CLASS (closed, the rule landing) — this is the child-label refinement.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-TOOLTIP-DISMISSABLE-TITLE-EQ-TEXT** `tooltip/dismissable` fires on `<a title="front matter">front matter</a>` — the title equals visible text exactly; SC 1.4.13 "content on hover" failure does not apply when no additional content is conveyed. Fix: when the flagged element's trimmed `textContent` (case-insensitively) equals the `title` attribute value, drop the finding or downgrade it to a `parsing/redundant-title-attribute` note (best-practice nudge, not a 1.4.13 violation). Pairs with V1-TOOLTIP-DISMISSABLE-VISIBLE-TEXT-EQ-TITLE (closed, the suggest_fix-side fix that drops redundant alternatives) — that closed the alternatives; this closes the fact-of-firing.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-MEDIA-SVG-ACCESSIBLE-NAME** Cross-cutting theme #15 (3/4 sites). Inline `<svg>` accessible-name semantics not modeled — `<title>`, `role="img" + aria-label`, `<use xlink:href>` references — produce zero findings on codebases with hundreds of inline SVGs. New rule `media/svg-accessible-name`: `<svg>` used non-decoratively (any of: parent has interactive role, parent is `<button>`/`<a>`, sibling text references it as content) without (a) child `<title>` element, (b) `role="img"` + `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`, (c) `<use xlink:href="#id">` where `#id` resolves to a named symbol. Decorative case: `<svg aria-hidden="true">` (or absent role with no labeling parent) silent. WCAG 1.1.1. Pairs with Q5-RULE-ICON-FONT-ARIA-HIDDEN (closed) + Q6-LINK-ICON-ONLY-ACCNAME (closed) — those cover icon-font and anchor-name axes; this is the inline-SVG axis.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-ARIA-PRESENTATION-ON-SEMANTIC-ANCESTOR** New rule `aria/presentation-on-semantic-ancestor`. WCAG F92: `role="presentation"` (or `role="none"`) on an element whose descendants include focusable controls — the role removes semantics the descendants depend on. Single-site evidence (n≥6) but spec-deterministic rule with high signal. WCAG 4.1.2.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-FORMS-SELECT-ONCHANGE-CONTEXT-CHANGE** New rule `forms/select-onchange-context-change`. `<select onchange="...navigate|location|window.open|submit...">` — selecting an option triggers context change without user activation. WCAG 3.2.2. Single-site evidence (n=3) but pervasive antipattern; rule is statically deterministic. Pairs with Q-SHARED-FORM-SUBMIT-ONCHANGE (covered in untargeted-but-groundable below).
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-VISUAL-DISABLED-NON-CONTROL** New rule `semantics/visual-disabled-non-control`. `<span class="prev disabled">Back</span>` — visual-disabled appearance on a non-interactive element with no semantics; AT users see no disabled-state signal. WCAG 1.3.1 + 4.1.2. Pairs with Q4-RULE-DISABLED-LINK-SURROGATE (closed, anchor variant) — this is the broader span/div variant.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-FORMS-ASTERISK-REQUIRED-MARKER** New rule `forms/asterisk-required-marker`. Label/placeholder text ending `*` on an input that has no `required`/`aria-required` attribute and no document-level legend explaining the convention. WCAG 3.3.2. Pairs with Q2R2-FORM-REQ (closed, wrapper-forwards-required-without-marker axis) — different direction: that detects the wrapper-forwards-without-marker case; this detects the marker-without-attribute case.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-LINK-NAME-ONLY-SYMBOL** New rule `navigation/link-name-only-symbol`. Anchor whose normalized accessible name is ≤2 punctuation/HTML-entity characters (`&lsaquo;`, `&times;`, `«`, `»`). WCAG 2.4.4. Pairs with Q4-RULE-PAGINATION-GLYPH-NAME (closed, pagination-specific finder) — this is the broader rule that fires regardless of pagination context.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-DUPLICATE-LANDMARK-HEADER-FOOTER** Extend `semantics/duplicate-landmark-unlabeled` to cover multiple `<header>` / `<footer>` siblings (banner / contentinfo landmarks). Currently covers `<nav>`/`<main>`/`<aside>`; adds `<header>`/`<footer>` to the family with the same labeling-or-collision predicate. WCAG 1.3.1.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-MULTIPLE-H1-BRANCH** (1/4 second-sweep targets) `semantics/heading-hierarchy` has no branch for "two `<h1>` elements on a single page" — multiple top-level outline anchors (e.g. `<h1 class="logo">Site Name</h1>` at top + `<h1>Page Title</h1>` mid-page). Existing branches cover "no `<h1>`" and "level skipped"; multiple-h1 is an outline concern many style guides flag. Fix: add a multiple-h1 branch citing both line numbers and noting that the document outline has two top-level headings. Severity: warning. WCAG 1.3.1.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-COLOR-STATE-CLASS-COLOR-ONLY** (1/4) `color/meaning-by-color-only` scopes only to status-utility class names (`text-danger`, `alert-success`, `btn-warning`); misses tutorial / hand-authored CSS that uses non-utility class names like `.current`, `.active`, `.selected`, `.correct`, `.incorrect` and changes ONLY `color` between paired state classes (no text-decoration, no font-weight change, no border, no icon swap). Concrete observation: `.nav.active a { color: #000 }` paired with `.nav a:hover { color: #c0392b; font-weight: bold }` — the active state is color-only. Fix: widen the rule to also flag selectors that toggle ONLY `color` between paired state classes (sibling selectors differing only in state-class suffix), OR add a 1.4.1 review-candidate finder that points at color-toggling state classes and asks the agent to verify a non-color signal also changes. WCAG 1.4.1. Pairs with Q7-FINDER-1.4.1-COLOR-CLASS-CROSSREF (related cross-class finder — different angle: that targets `.correct/.incorrect`-style class definitions; this targets `.foo` vs `.foo.active` paired definitions).
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-ANIMATION-ITERATION-COUNT-GATE** (1/4) `motion/three-flashes-or-below-threshold` (or whichever rule emits 2.3.1 candidates) synthesizes a per-second cycle rate from `animation-duration` alone, treating one-shot transitions (`animation: slideOff 0.3s linear forwards;` with no `infinite` and default iteration count of 1) as candidate-flashing. WCAG 2.3.1 requires three flashes per second sustained — a single-cycle 300ms animation is mathematically inapplicable. Same false positive on `animation: hide 0.2s ease-out` (single-shot reveal). Fix: gate the cycles/s synthesis on `animation-iteration-count: infinite` (or numeric > 1 with a high-enough product). When `forwards`/`backwards`/`both` is the only fill specifier and iteration count is the default 1, treat as a one-shot transition and don't synthesize a frequency. Single-cycle animations should not produce a 2.3.1 candidate at all. Doctrine note: numeric-threshold heuristics are suppression — but iteration-count is provable from code (deterministic), not heuristic; this is correctness, not threshold-suppression. WCAG 2.3.1.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-IMG-ALT-REPEATS-PROSE-SC-MISMAP** (2/4) 1.4.5 "Images of Text" finder fires when alt text repeats a sibling label or surrounding prose: `<img alt="fly">` next to `<p>Fly</p>`; sponsor-avatar grids with sequential alt-prefix-plus-index. SC 1.4.5 governs whether the image's PIXELS render text — orthogonal to alt-prose redundancy (which is a 1.1.1 concern). Concrete observations across two targets: 10+ candidates each on pictorial-image clusters and sponsor avatars. The criterion mismatch trains the agent to mistrust 1.4.5 candidates uniformly. Fix: either (a) drop the alt-repeats-prose finder for 1.4.5 entirely — the actual signal (text rendered in pixels) is invisible to static analysis without OCR, and the agent reading the alt+visible-prose pair is the only reliable arbiter; OR (b) keep the finder but reframe the reason as a 1.1.1 "redundant alternative text" candidate suggesting `alt=""` since the visible label already names the image. The 1.4.5 evidence (filename-suggests-text, e.g. `button.png`/`banner-headline.svg`) stays under 1.4.5; alt-repeats-prose moves to 1.1.1. Doctrine: spec-correctness on which criterion a candidate belongs to. Pairs with Q7-CHECKLIST-1.4.5-SR-ONLY-DEDUPE (different lever; that's contiguous-range dedup, this is criterion mapping).
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-TABLE-TH-SCOPE-IMPLICIT-COL-NARROWING** (1/4) `semantics/table-th-scope-missing` fires 29× on simple `<thead><tr><th>...</th></tr></thead><tbody>...</tbody>` tables. Per HTML5, `<th>` cells inside `<thead>` containing only header cells receive implicit `scope=col`, so these tables are programmatically associated. WCAG technique H63 calls explicit scope "good practice" but H43 (id/headers) covers complex tables; SC 1.3.1 does not require explicit scope on simple thead+tbody tables. Fix: tighten predicate to fire only when (a) the table has multiple header rows in `<thead>`, (b) `<th>` appears in a `<tr>` outside `<thead>` without scope, OR (c) the table mixes row-headers and column-headers. Trivial single-row `<thead>` with column-only `<th>` children should not fire — implicit scope=col covers it. Alternative: emit at info severity not warning. WCAG 1.3.1.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-TOOLTIP-DISMISSABLE-TITLE-IS-ACCNAME** (1/4) `tooltip/dismissable` fires on icon-only links where `title` is the sole accessible-name source (no inner text, no aria-label, no labeled child) — e.g. an icon-only social link with only an `<i>` child plus `title="Facebook"`. SC 1.4.13 targets supplementary content shown on hover/focus; when `title` IS the element's primary accessible name, the rule misclassifies the name carrier as supplementary tooltip content. Cross-rule disagreement compounds: `navigation/link-descriptive-text` accepts the same `title` as the name (does not fire); `tooltip/dismissable` rejects it (fires). Fix: suppress `tooltip/dismissable` on interactive elements where `title` is the sole accessible-name source (no inner text, no aria-label/labelledby, no labeled child). Cross-reference the same name-resolution predicate `link-descriptive-text` uses. Alternative: keep firing but reframe message — current "native browser tooltips are not dismissable" is misleading when title is the name carrier. WCAG 1.4.13. Pairs with Q7-RULE-TOOLTIP-DISMISSABLE-TITLE-EQ-TEXT (the redundant-title variant) and Q7-RULE-NAME-RESOLUTION-TITLE-FALLBACK-CONSISTENCY (the cross-rule consistency angle).
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-NAME-RESOLUTION-TITLE-FALLBACK-CONSISTENCY** (1/4) Two rules treat the `title` attribute differently: `navigation/link-descriptive-text` accepts `title` as accessible name unconditionally (so icon-only social links with only `<i>` child + `title="Facebook"` silently pass); `tooltip/dismissable` treats the same `title` as supplementary tooltip content (and fires). The rules disagree on the same attribute on the same element. Per ARIA 1.2 `title` is a last-resort name source — many screen readers (NVDA at default verbosity, VoiceOver in some modes) suppress it. Fix: pick one model and apply it across all name-resolution rules: either (a) treat `title` as accessible name only when no other source exists AND emit info-severity "name-via-title-fallback" candidates so the agent can verify SR support, or (b) reject `title` as name and require aria-label / inner text / sr-only span. The current schism produces silent under-fire on the canonical icon-only social-link pattern (the textbook 2.4.4 risk surface). Pairs with Q7-RULE-TOOLTIP-DISMISSABLE-TITLE-IS-ACCNAME (same observation, complementary fix lane).
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-FORMS-ALERT-CONTAINER-PREDICATE** (1/4) `forms/error-message-not-associated` recognizes `.invalid-feedback` (newer-CSS-framework convention), `.error-message` (generic), and `[role=alert]` as candidate error containers, but misses the older `.alert.alert-error` / `.alert.alert-success` / `.alert.alert-danger` / `.alert.alert-warning` family plus id-anchored containers (e.g. `id="contactError"` / `id="contactSuccess"`). Templates using these patterns place the alert div adjacent to a form but never wire it via `aria-describedby` — and the rule never flags it. Fix: extend the predicate to recognize `.alert.alert-{error,danger,warning,success,info}` and conventional id patterns (`*Error`, `*Success`, `*Message`) as candidate containers. Surface as info severity if the association is uncertain. WCAG 3.3.1. Pairs with Q3-RULE-ARIA-INVALID-MISSING (closed, different surface).
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-FORM-LANDMARK-NAME-MISSING** (1/4) New rule `semantics/form-landmark-name-missing`. `<form role="form">` (or `<form>` rendered as a landmark by browser heuristics) with no accessible name — no `aria-label`, no `aria-labelledby`, no labeled child. Per ARIA 1.2 a form landmark with no accessible name is removed from the landmark list, undermining the role declaration. `semantics/section-accessible-name-missing` covers `<section>` but not `<form>`. Fix options: (a) widen `semantics/section-accessible-name-missing` to also evaluate `<form role="form">` and `<form>` elements rendered as landmarks (sitting alongside other landmarks); (b) add sibling rule `semantics/form-landmark-name-missing` with the same heuristic. The "section sits alongside other landmarks" predicate translates directly to forms. WCAG 1.3.1 + 4.1.2.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-PAGINATION-ANCHOR-FIX-DIRECTION** (1/4) `navigation/href-placeholder` (formerly `href-javascript-void`) fires on `<a href="#">1</a>`, `<a href="#">2</a>` pagination markers and suggests "change to `<button type="button">`" — wrong direction. Pagination should remain anchors with real `href` values pointing at paginated routes (`href="?page=2"`). The rule technically detects a real defect (no fragment target), but the suggested remediation steers toward the wrong element type for paginated navigation. Fix: when the placeholder anchor sits inside a list with class containing `pagination`/`pager` OR has sibling text content of 1/2/3 numerals, prefer the navigation-with-real-href branch in the fix suggestion ("`href="?page=2"` pointing at an actual paginated route") and de-emphasize the button-conversion branch. Currently both paths read as equally weighted in the guidance.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-LINK-DESCRIPTIVE-CROSS-LANDMARK-DUPS** (1/4) `navigation/link-descriptive-text` flags identical-name + identical-href anchor pairs as confusing duplicates when they sit in different landmarks (top `<header><nav>` + bottom `<footer><nav>`). SC 2.4.5 explicitly calls helpful redundancy across regions a feature; treating header-nav/footer-nav duplicates as confusing inverts the spec. The rule cannot distinguish "helpful redundancy across regions" from "two ambiguously-similar in-content links." Fix: when the two duplicates sit in different landmarks (one inside `<nav>`/`<header>`, the other inside `<footer>`) and target the same href, downgrade or suppress — that's a navigation-redundancy pattern, not the screen-reader-rotor confusion pattern SC 2.4.4/2.4.9 targets. Keep firing when both duplicates are inside the same content region (e.g. two "Read More" in the same article). Encode the landmark-context in the reason text rather than dropping the candidate.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-1.3.6-VS-1.3.5-CANDIDATE-VIOLATION-DEDUP** (1/4) 1.3.6 "Identify Purpose" review candidate fires on inputs that ALREADY trigger `forms/autocomplete-missing` (a deterministic 1.3.5 violation) at the same line. Concrete observation: `<input>` at line 88 emits both a `forms/autocomplete-missing` violation (criterion 1.3.5) AND a 1.3.6 review candidate; the candidate adds nothing the violation didn't already say. SC 1.3.5 is a subset signal of SC 1.3.6 — the candidate is implied. Fix: per-line dedup — when an automated violation already covers a criterion subset, skip the parent-criterion review candidate at the same line. Maintain a per-line set of fired rule criteria; skip review candidates whose criteria union is a subset of an already-fired violation's criteria.
- [ ] **Q7-RULE-SC-3.2.5-LOADER-AUDIT** (2/4) Companion to Q7-AAA-RULE-LOADER-SILENT-NORUN. The new `navigation/link-target-blank-announcement` rule (Q4-RULE-EXTERNAL-LINK-NEW-WINDOW closed) is registered in `list_rules` but does not appear in any scan response's `perRuleCoverage` — observed across two of four targets. `meta.rulesEvaluated.loaded: 74` vs `list_rules` total 75. Either the rule is not wired into the engine's HTML rule-runner path, or its eligibility check filters out plain HTML files. Result: every `<a target="_blank">` without an announcement on every HTML template silently passes. Fix: audit the rule registry to confirm `navigation/link-target-blank-announcement` is in the HTML-applicable rule list; add a regression test scanning a fixture `<a href="..." target="_blank">X</a>` and asserting the rule fires (both at default level for AAA-loaded sessions and explicitly with `level: "AAA"`). WCAG 3.2.5.

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P2 — finder coverage on untargeted-but-groundable criteria)

Cross-cutting theme #18 (4/4 sites). Several criteria currently ship as `untargetedCriteria` (bare-criterion prompts) when the deterministic grounding evidence sits in the parsed source. Each item below promotes one criterion to a grounded finder.

- [ ] **Q7-FINDER-3.3.1-FORM-REQUIRED-ATTRS** `wcag22:3.3.1 Error Identification` — `<form>` blocks containing `<input required>` / `pattern` / `minlength` / `aria-invalid` are deterministically present in source. Surface as grounded review candidates pointing at the field, not as untargeted prompts. Pairs with Q3-RULE-ARIA-INVALID-MISSING (closed, the missing-aria-invalid rule) — this is the broader finder for the criterion.
- [ ] **Q7-FINDER-3.3.8-PASSWORD-INPUTS** `wcag22:3.3.8 Accessible Authentication` — every `<input type="password">` is a candidate location. Promote to grounded finder citing the input element and the form's other auth fields.
- [ ] **Q7-FINDER-3.2.2-ONCHANGE-CONTEXT-MUTATION** `wcag22:3.2.2 On Input` — JS handlers (`onchange`, `oninput`, `addEventListener('change'|'input')`) whose body mutates `location`, `window.open`, `innerHTML`, or calls a `navigate*`/`router.push*` identifier. Static evidence is grep-able. Pairs with Q7-RULE-FORMS-SELECT-ONCHANGE-CONTEXT-CHANGE (above) — different shape: that rule fires deterministically on the `<select onchange="...navigate...">` subset; this finder surfaces the broader candidate set for review.
- [ ] **Q7-FINDER-1.4.1-COLOR-CLASS-CROSSREF** `wcag22:1.4.1 Use of Color` — CSS class definitions like `.correct{color:green}` / `.incorrect{color:red}` cross-referenced with HTML usage of those classes (deterministic). Surface as candidates pointing at usage sites; reason names the color-only convention.
- [ ] **Q7-FINDER-1.3.2-CSS-VISUAL-ORDER** `wcag22:1.3.2 Meaningful Sequence` — CSS `flex-direction: row-reverse` / `order: N` / `direction: rtl` on layout containers is grep-able. Cross-ref Q6-MEANINGFUL-SEQUENCE-UTILITY-CLASSES (closed) — that fixed the false-positive on utility-class definitions; this is the consumer-side finder that fires on the actual layout-reordering call sites.
- [ ] **Q7-FINDER-2.5.1-TOUCH-POINTER-HANDLERS** `wcag22:2.5.1 Pointer Gestures` — `touchstart`/`touchmove`/`pointermove` handlers and pointer-event-library imports (hammer.js, use-gesture, @use-gesture) in JS, plus inline `ontouchstart`/`onpointermove` attributes in HTML. Static evidence. Pairs with Q5-POINTER-GESTURES-SUBSTRING-FALSE-POSITIVE (closed) — that closed the false-positive on bare identifier substring; this widens the legitimate evidence sources.

### v0.3.0 — accepted (P2 — checklist quality)

- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-ELEMENT-VS-PROSE** Iframe-as-media finders for 1.2.x cite prose mentions of `<iframe>` (text content of documentation pages) instead of actual elements. Fix: finder must traverse the parsed DOM for `<iframe>` element nodes, not regex against text content. Pairs with Q-SHARED-VIDEO-CAPTIONS-IFRAME-EMBEDS (closed, the rule extension) — different surface: that's the rule firing on real iframes; this is the finder distinguishing real iframes from prose mentions.
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-1.3.3-LOCATIVE-CALLOUT-PROSE** `wcag22:1.3.3 Sensory Characteristics` finder fires on developer documentation prose ("right side", "button below") inside Callout / Note / Tip blocks — that prose describes UI to developers, not user-facing UI instructions. Fix: when the matched text sits inside a known prose-callout container (`<div class="note|tip|warning|callout">` / `<aside class="...">`) AND no nearby element-level reference resolves the locative, drop the candidate (developer prose is not a user-facing UI instruction). Pairs with Q3-SENSORY-CHARACTERISTIC-LOCATIVE-WORD-SCOPE (closed, locative-word tightening) — this is the callout-container scope tightening.
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-1.4.5-SR-ONLY-DEDUPE** `wcag22:1.4.5 Images of Text` finder fires N times per page on adjacent sponsor-avatar / contributor-list image clusters — N near-identical candidates from contiguous line ranges. Fix: when ≥4 candidates share `(ruleId, criterionId, parent-element-shape)` within a contiguous line range, collapse to one entry with `siblingOccurrences: [{ line, alt }]` + reason "and N similar at lines X-Y." Pairs with Q4-LIKELY-REDUCIBLE-SIBLING-IMAGE-DEDUPE (closed, image-rule dedupe) and Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (closed, cross-file). This is the within-page contiguous-range axis.
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-AUDIO-VS-VIDEO-CRITERION-FAN** `<audio>` elements fan out across `wcag22:1.2.3` and `wcag22:1.2.5` (Audio Description / Synchronized Media) — but those criteria are scoped to *synchronized media* (video tracks), not audio-only. Six audio elements emitted 18 candidates across 3 criteria. Fix: when the source element is `<audio>` (no `<video>`/`<source type="video/...">`/iframe-with-video-host), include only `wcag22:1.2.1` (Audio-only and Video-only Prerecorded) in the criterion fan-out; exclude 1.2.3 and 1.2.5. Doctrine: spec-correctness on which criteria a candidate belongs to.
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-CRITERION-FAN-MULTI-CRITERION-CANDIDATE** (2/4 second-sweep targets) When the same evidence (path + line + element shape) supports multiple criteria, the checklist emits N separate candidates with identical reason text — observed: iframe-as-media finders emitting 6 candidates from 2 unique iframes across 1.2.1 + 1.2.3 + 1.2.5; audio-only finders emitting 18 candidates from 6 elements × 3 criteria. The agent dismisses the same dismissal N times. Fix: when the same evidence supports multiple criteria, emit ONE candidate with `criteria: [...]` array and a single deduplicated `reason` text. The summary headline counter then reflects unique evidence locations, not duplicated dismissals. Pairs with Q7-CHECKLIST-AUDIO-VS-VIDEO-CRITERION-FAN (different angle: that's about which criteria the candidate belongs to; this is about the dedup shape when it does belong to multiple).
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-1.3.3-LOCATIVE-WITH-NOUN** (1/4) 1.3.3 sensory finder fires on prose like "Fill out the form below with some info" because of substring "form below" — but the position word is followed by a noun (`form`) that names the target. WCAG 1.3.3 prohibits sensory-only / position-only references when there is no other identifier; when a noun anchors the reference, the position is supplementary, not the sole locator. Fix: either (a) drop the candidate when a noun follows the position word (the noun anchors the reference), OR (b) rephrase reason to "verify users who linearize content (screen readers) can locate '<noun>' by its name — '<position>' is a layout cue, not a name." Pairs with Q7-CHECKLIST-1.3.3-LOCATIVE-CALLOUT-PROSE (different angle: that's about prose-callout containers; this is about position-with-noun).
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-2.4.5-DISJUNCTIVE-PREDICATE** (1/4) 2.4.5 "Multiple Ways" review candidate emits "Likely root layout has no search, sitemap, breadcrumb, or 3-link navigation signal" on a page that DOES have a 3-link `<nav>` with 17 anchors. The reason text says "1 `<nav>`, 17 `<a>`, no `<input type='search'>`, no breadcrumb, no sitemap link" — the heuristic conjuncts all four mechanisms; SC 2.4.5 is disjunctive (any one mechanism satisfies). Fix: revise the heuristic to disjunctive — accept ANY of: search input present, sitemap link present, breadcrumb structure detected, OR primary `<nav>` with ≥3 unique links. Or rephrase reason to "Has primary navigation but no secondary mechanism (search/sitemap/breadcrumb); SC 2.4.5 is satisfied by any one — verify your nav links are unique destinations, or consider a search box for sites with deeper hierarchies." The current text reads as a false alarm on a page that probably already meets 2.4.5.
- [ ] **Q7-CHECKLIST-2.4.5-FRAGMENT-FILE-CONTEXT** (1/4) 2.4.5 finder labels a `<head>`-only partial as "Likely root layout" because of `0 <nav>, 0 <a>` evidence. A `<head>`-only file (no `<body>`) cannot be a root layout — the conclusion is heuristic and wrong. Fix: the "Likely root layout" classifier should require the file to contain `<body>` OR be the only top-level `<html>` document in its directory tree. A `<head>`-only file should be classified as "head fragment" and skipped for 2.4.5 — the rule semantically applies to renderable pages with navigable content. Pairs with Q7-FRAGMENT-FILE-HEADING-HIERARCHY (same fragment-classification surface).

### Considered and rejected (per CLAUDE.md §1)

- **Auto-suppress `motion/pause-stop-hide` on transitions ≤ Xms** → rejected per §1 "Numeric-threshold heuristics are suppression." A 200ms transition might be a hover-effect debounce or might be a fast-flashing annotation; static analysis cannot tell. The spec-correctness fix in Q7-RULE-MOTION-DURATION-AND-TRIGGER-GATE (above) does NOT use a single threshold to suppress — it gates the *criterion choice* (2.2.2 vs 2.3.3) on parsed evidence (infinite loop, auto-play attribute, user-pseudo-class scoping), and encodes the duration as additive reason context. Both lanes still emit candidates.
- **Lower confidence on JS-driven panel containers (`carousel-item`, `tab-pane`, `accordion-item`) for `semantics/heading-hierarchy`** → rejected per §1 "No heuristic suppression, even for spec carve-outs." The hierarchy violation at the rendered shape may or may not be a real problem depending on whether the panel is opened simultaneously or one-at-a-time at runtime — only the agent reading the JS controller knows. Reason-enrichment ("heading sits inside a JS-driven panel container — verify whether siblings are visible together at runtime") is acceptable; confidence-downgrade is not.
- **Filter the `likelyIrrelevant` bucket more aggressively (sponsor avatars, brand marks)** → rejected per §1 "Labeled buckets are suppression too" and the existing "logotype exemption" doctrine carve-out. The bucket label is honest only when provable from code; "this is a sponsor avatar" is not provable. Reason-enrichment ("alt text matches a known logo/brand naming pattern — verify against the logotype exemption") is acceptable; bucket-then-filter is not. Cross-ref the existing rejected entries on logotype exemption in Track Q.
- **Auto-detect SPA shells from low file count and skip 2.4.5** → rejected per the existing rejection of P1-I in Track Q (heuristic suppression on spec carve-outs). Q7-RULE-LANDMARK-MAIN-FRAGMENT-SCOPE adds `couldBeWrongBecause: ["isolated_component_demo_page"]` as additive metadata for one specific shape (single-component demo pages), but that's a couldBeWrongBecause label on a specific structural pattern, not a heuristic skip on a criterion. The 2.4.5 single-page reason-enrichment lives in V1-2.4.5-SINGLE-PAGE-REASON-ENRICH (open).

### Cross-track shared items

These themes were observed on the 2026-04-24 sweep but are fully covered by prior backlog items; cross-referenced to avoid duplication.

- **`plan.safeEditsAvailable` vs `plan.fixesByClass.mechanical` 18×–145× disagreement on 4/4 sites** → covered by Q-SHARED-SAFE-EDITS-VS-MECHANICAL-DISAGREEMENT (closed) and Q3-PLAN-MECHANICAL-EDITS-COUNTER-DRIFT (closed). The doctrine name is "composite headline counts are dishonest"; the closed items renamed/dropped the composite. If the composite still ships on current `main`, the bug is shipped-but-stale-MCP — see V1-MCP-DIST-STALE-CI-GATE (open).
- **`meta.configSearchedFrom` echoes `meta.scanned.root` verbatim on 3/4 sites** → covered by Q6-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT (closed). Same stale-MCP suspicion if reproducing on current `main`.
- **`forms/labels-required` misses adjacent unassociated label** → covered by Q5-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED (closed) and Q6-RULE-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED-NO-IDS (closed).
- **Icon-only buttons (icon-font / inline SVG) missing accessible name** → covered by Q5-RULE-ICON-FONT-ARIA-HIDDEN (closed) and Q6-LINK-ICON-ONLY-ACCNAME (closed). The new inline-SVG axis lives in Q7-RULE-MEDIA-SVG-ACCESSIBLE-NAME above.
- **Multi-project root not detected** → covered by Q6-CATALOG-REPO-SIBLING-HINT (closed). 2/4 sites in the sweep observed the same untriaged-catalog symptom; if the closed item's emission isn't firing on current `main`, the bug is shipped-but-stale-MCP — V1-MCP-DIST-STALE-CI-GATE.
- **`likelyIrrelevant` bucket sweeps real candidates (iframe embeds of YouTube/Vimeo classified as no-media)** → covered by Q-SHARED-LIKELY-IRRELEVANT-IFRAME-NOT-MEDIA (closed) and Q-SHARED-VIDEO-CAPTIONS-IFRAME-EMBEDS (closed).
- **`scan` says N, `checklist` says M for the same criterion** → covered by Q-SHARED-COVERAGE-FAILING-CRITERIA-DERIVATION (closed) and Q4-WARNING-DETAILS-CROSS-SURFACE-UNIFY (closed).
- **`plan.summary` prose sums automated + manual + bare-untargeted into one string** → covered by Q-SHARED-PLAN-SUMMARY-VERIFY-IN-SOURCE-INFLATION (closed) and Q3-PLAN-SUMMARY-PROSE (closed).
- **`stale_mcp_subprocess` warning fires every call without inline remediation** → covered by Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT (closed) + Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT-ERROR-ENVELOPE (closed) + Q3-MCP-RESTART-HINT-SUBPROCESS-RACE (closed) + Q3-META-BUILD-PROVENANCE (closed). The 4/4-site recurrence in this sweep on the same code suggests V1-MCP-DIST-STALE-CI-GATE (open) is the systemic fix.
- **`opaqueCustomComponentNames` returns minified-JS member-access expressions (`AG.y`, `H.length`, `Math.abs`)** → covered by Q6-AUTODETECT-WRAPPERS-LEAKS-JS-IDENTIFIER-NOISE (closed) + Q6-OPAQUE-COMPONENTS-MINIFIED-JS-REGRESSION (closed). Recurrence on this sweep suggests the closed items are shipped-but-stale-MCP, or the belt-and-braces second-layer fix is still pending parser-side work.
- **2026-04-24 second sweep recurrences (4 targets, second pass on the same shapes)** — the following all reproduced on at least one of the four second-sweep targets: 2.5.1 pointer-gestures identifier-substring false positive (covered by Q5-POINTER-GESTURES-SUBSTRING-FALSE-POSITIVE closed), `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` missing the no-id case (covered by Q6-RULE-LABEL-ADJACENT-UNASSOCIATED-NO-IDS closed), minified-vendor-JS snippet pointing at a single long line with no column disambiguation (covered by Q6-MINIFIED-FILE-SNIPPET-COLUMN-ENRICHMENT closed), `configSource: null` + `configSearchedFrom` + `configNote` + `warnings: ["no_config_found"]` triple-readout (covered by Q6-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT closed). All four are shipped-but-stale-MCP suspects per V1-MCP-DIST-STALE-CI-GATE (open) — same systemic fix as the warnings-mixing recurrence above.

## Needs judgment — 2026-04-21 field test

Open strategy/doctrine questions surfaced during triage that need a user-level decision before the corresponding backlog work can land. They are not tracked as dispatchable work — they are blockers on specific items above. Once answered, each resolves into an existing or new backlog item.

- **J-1 (pause-stop-hide signal-vs-noise strategy).** `motion/pause-stop-hide` produced 8,940 findings at scale and 55 on vanilla HTML/JS projects. Most are user-triggered hover/focus transitions, not the auto-playing motion WCAG 2.2.2 targets. Current reason text doesn't distinguish. Is this (a) over-surface that needs reason-text enrichment only ("auto-playing?" / "user-triggered?" signal, let the agent decide), (b) spec-correctness matter where the rule should narrow to auto-playing-only, (c) both? Surface-don't-suppress argues (a); narrowing risks silent-miss on genuine 2.2.2 auto-playing violations. Blocks Q6-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-PER-FILE-AGGREGATION scoping decision.

- **J-2 (vendor CSS noise, primary mitigation choice).** Website-template scans: 69% of 24,772 findings = 3 rules firing on vendor CSS. Doctrine forbids default-excluding vendor paths. Primary mitigation options: (a) fix the `scannedBuildArtifacts` classifier so agents can filter by it with confidence, (b) add a per-file finding cap (Q6-BUDGET-UNDER-VENDOR-NOISE), (c) both, (d) something else (e.g., surface rule-per-file concentration aggressively and trust the agent to route). Currently Q6-BUDGET and Q6-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-GROUP-BY-BASENAME are both open; the question is whether they land independently or one supersedes the other. Blocks prioritization of both items.

- **J-3 (plan.totalFindings: rename vs remove).** Every `scan_project` ships a top-level `plan.totalFindings: N` that sums violations + notes. Doctrine forbids composite headline counts. Agents may already depend on the field for aggregation. Remove entirely (breaking — v1.0 gate) or rename to `plan.totalFindingsComposite` with explicit "sum of violations+notes, prefer the split" prose (additive, non-breaking)? Blocks Q6-PLAN-TOTALFINDINGS-VIOLATIONS-VS-NOTES implementation shape.

- **J-4 (rulesEvaluated definition: loaded-vs-fired).** `rulesEvaluated` disagrees cross-surface on the same cwd: `scan_project: 53` vs `propose_config: 54` vs `list_rules: 54` (vanilla-JS tree). Likely cause: JSX/TSX-only rule counted as "evaluated on 0 applicable files" differs by tool. Which is correct: "evaluated" = "the rule ran on 0 applicable files" (54) or "the rule didn't run because no applicable files" (53)? Either is honest; they must agree cross-surface. Blocks Q-SHARED-RULES-EVALUATED-SSOT shape.

- **J-5 (likelyIrrelevant bucket: parse-coverage caveat).** `likelyIrrelevant` for 1.2.x is honest at scale ("no video/audio in scanned files"). On an SSG source tree it's technically honest but 307 `.md`/`.markdown` files were never parsed — under the doctrine rule that bucket labels must be provable, is "no video in scanned files" sufficient when the scan missed the content layer? Options: (a) enrich `likelyIrrelevant` reason with the skipped-content caveat automatically (covered by Q4-RELEVANCE-REASON-PARSE-COVERAGE-CAVEAT), (b) consider it the agent's job to reconcile `likelyIrrelevant` with `extensions_skipped_no_parser` separately, (c) suppress `likelyIrrelevant` entirely when content extensions are skipped. Blocks Q4-RELEVANCE-REASON-PARSE-COVERAGE-CAVEAT scoping.

- **J-6 (performance budget at 4k-file vendor-heavy scale).** A full-scan ran 20.5 s internal (~32 s wall-clock) against 3,973 files — 1.7× over linear extrapolation of the 1000-file 3 s budget. Options: (a) add a new bench scenario at 3-4k vendor-heavy files with an explicit budget, (b) document a "precommit use not supported beyond N files" scope limit, (c) default to `--changed`-only discovery on repos > 1000 files with a `largeRepoHint` warning, (d) accept the slowdown and document it. Blocks performance-track decisions + CLAUDE.md §11 table update.

- **J-7 (static-analysis limit vs cross-file handler resolution).** `<a href="#">` / `<li>`-acting-as-tab / `<div>`-acting-as-button patterns with click handlers in separate `.js` files (common on vanilla-JS trees + at scale). `navigation/href-javascript-void` (registry-drift notwithstanding) catches raw `href="#"`; the broader "custom interactive widget with JS-only handler" class requires cross-file resolution. Read sibling `.js` files to resolve handlers (expensive, cross-file complexity, risks wrong-identifier matches) OR accept as an inherent static-analysis limitation and document it in `limitations`? Doctrine: "don't duplicate capability the agent already has" argues for the latter — the agent can Read+Grep across files. Blocks any rule/finder that would widen predicates to cross-file handler presence.

- **J-8 (scan_file error discrimination split).** Design-system scan confirmed `scan_file` conflates `file-unsupported` (extension not in set) with file-not-found. Split options: (a) `file-not-found` / `unsupported-extension` / `file-unreadable` (three distinct codes), (b) fold not-found into a single `file-unreadable` (two codes: "can't read this file" + "can't parse this extension"), (c) keep `file-unsupported` as umbrella with a `details.reason` sub-discriminator. Doctrine prefers explicit codes (a); (b) is more compact; (c) keeps the existing discriminator stable. Blocks Q-SHARED-SCAN-FILE-ERROR-DISCRIMINATION implementation shape.

## Track C — Conformance-claim gaps

Owner: main session + `spec-researcher` + `doc-writer`. Staged; do not dispatch alongside active tracks. ra11y today produces *signals* (findings, review candidates, coverage hints). It does not produce a *claim*. Track C closes the four gaps that separate the two — attestation ledger, runtime-evidence ingest, process-level scope, conformance statement. **No new rules**; the track widens what an agent can defensibly say after using the tool.

Sequencing (cross-gap, soft): attestations first (unlocks evidence semantics everywhere else), then axe ingest (wide-reach runtime coverage), then process scope, then conformance capstone. Within each gap items are ordered by the ADR-then-foundation-then-surface pattern.

### v0.3.0 — attestation ledger (foundation)

- [x] **C-ATTEST-ADR** ADR 0011 (evidence as first-class primitive) + ADR 0012 (rule-scoped attestations) cover this slot.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-STORE** `.ra11y/attestations.jsonl` append-only store with validated envelope landed in `src/config/attestation-store.ts`.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-TOOL** `attest` MCP tool landed; accepts `criterionId`, `ruleIds?`, `verdict`, `scope`, `location?`, `reason`, `by`; stamps commit hash; rejects bare verdicts.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-LIST** `list_attestations` MCP tool landed with git-backed staleness probe + `staleProbeUnavailable` signal for non-repo environments.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-PRUNE** `ra11y attestations prune [--dry-run]` subcommand landed; pure function + injectable `fileExists` predicate.
- [x] **C-ATTEST-CHECKLIST** Checklist items spread per-criterion `attestation?: { verdict, stale?, evidence, by? }` with conditional-spread discipline.
- [x] **C-SUPPRESSION-ATTEST** Bare pragmas emit `verdict: "pending"` attestations; reasoned pragmas emit `"pass"`; `suppression/no-reason` finder retained as complementary signal.

### Rejected — runtime evidence bridge

A prior plan proposed ingesting vendor runtime results (axe-core JSON → normalized shape → ledger) as the path to close runtime-only WCAG criteria. Rejected: vendor-specific ingest adapters duplicate capability agents already have via their own test harnesses, and the tool's job is to point at the source — not at another tool's output. The ingest shape is also fragile to vendor schema drift, and the normalized layer re-buckets findings in ways the reading agent can't re-audit. Agents that run runtime checks bridge their results through the existing `attest` tool — the reason text is the evidence, and the attestation ledger is the durable, vendor-neutral channel.

Items struck: C-RUNTIME-ADR, C-AXE-SCHEMA, C-AXE-INGEST, C-RUNTIME-MAP. C-COVERAGE-MERGE is re-scoped below.

- [x] **C-COVERAGE-MERGE** Coverage report integrates attestations; per-criterion row carries optional `attested: { verdict, stale? }`. Manual criteria with fresh pass/n-a attestations count toward covered. Stale/pending/fail don't flip static verdicts.

### v0.3.0 — process-level scope

- [x] **C-PROCESS-ADR** ADR 0016 (process-level scope) landed.
- [x] **C-PROCESS-CONFIG** `processes: [{ name, pages }]` config primitive + schema validation + loader wiring.
- [x] **C-PROCESS-SCAN** `scan_process` MCP tool landed; orchestrates per-page scan with process-level frame.
- [x] **C-PROCESS-CONSISTENCY** `consistent-navigation` finder honors declared processes; heuristic path retained as fallback with a reason-text nudge to declare the primitive.
- [x] **C-PROCESS-IDENT** `consistent-identification` finder for wcag22:3.2.4; emits zero candidates when no processes config (honest needs-config gap).

### v1.0.0 — conformance capstone

- [x] **C-PROFILE-WCAG21AA** `ConformanceProfile` primitive + 8 built-ins (wcag21-a/aa, wcag22-a/aa/aaa, section508, en301549, ada); `--profile` CLI flag with explicit-override warning. Profile→reports scope filtering wired.
- [x] **C-CONFORM-ADR** ADR 0017 (conformance-statement-output) landed.
- [x] **C-CONFORM-STATEMENT** `conformance_statement` MCP tool + `src/reports/conformance.ts` landed; refuses when coverage is incomplete; blocker list cites missing criteria.
- [x] **C-CONFORM-SIGN** SHA-256 digest over (commit hash + attestations + in-scope criteria + config fingerprint); canonical JSON; `verifyConformanceBundle` for drift detection.
- [x] **C-CONFORM-DOC** `docs/conformance.md` end-to-end agent guide: scan → checklist → attest → coverage → signed statement.

### Dependencies + interactions with Track S

Track S speculative tools (`verdict-candidate`, `draft-vpat-narrative`, `resolve-component`) become concretely useful once **C-ATTEST-TOOL** lands — their output is evidence the agent writes to the ledger. Track C resolves Track S's "pick 1–2 speculative tools" decision (ADR 0005 §Follow-up): `verdict-candidate` is the natural first pick because its output IS an attestation entry. Promote Track S `[!]` items to `[ ]` once C-ATTEST-TOOL is merged.

### Considered and rejected

- **Embed axe-core as a runtime dep.** Rejected per **§3 invariant 1** (zero runtime deps). Parse axe's JSON output; don't pull axe-core into the install. Agents already run axe in their test harness; they hand us the JSON path.
- **In-tool headless browser (Playwright / Puppeteer).** Rejected per zero-deps + MCP-first-consumer framing — the agent already runs the browser in its test harness. We ingest; we don't drive.
- **Blanket "ra11y verifies conformance" marketing.** Rejected — the conformance statement will cite evidence sources; ra11y is the aggregator, the attestation is the agent's (or human's) word, and the signed bundle is what survives audit. Honesty > reach.

## Track V — v1.0.0 readiness

Owner: main session + `doc-writer` + `fixture-curator` + `test-author` + specialists as indicated per item. Source: 2026-04-19 three-agent gap analysis (rule coverage vs WCAG 2.1/2.2 A+AA, MCP shape discipline, backlog + semver + CI infra). The conformance capstone in Track C is shipped; these are the remaining gates for the "find all violations" bar.

No item here proposes new rule detection logic — Track V is about honesty surfaces, decision closure, and coverage verification. Items that WOULD add detection get logged as Track R or Track C items.

### v1.0.0 — certification claim blockers

Source: two independent pre-ship subagent sweeps (spec-researcher normative angle + general-purpose tooling/reproducibility angle). Both flagged overlapping blockers that would sink a real WCAG/VPAT/Section 508 certification submission. Each item has a concrete file:line anchor; together these are the gate to `/release 1.0.0`. Dispatch in parallel where non-overlapping; B1 and B6 block on B2's shape work.

- [x] **V1-CERT-STATEMENT-SHAPE** `src/reports/conformance.ts:101-128` `ConformanceStatement` is missing four of six W3C WCAG §5.3.1 required claim fields: `guidelinesTitle`, `guidelinesVersion`, `guidelinesUri`, `scope.files`, `technologiesReliedUpon`, `technologiesNotReliedUpon`. ADR 0017 designed these but implementation diverged. Accept ADR 0017 as the source of truth; reshape the type + `renderConformanceMarkdown` (line 308) to emit all six; update ADR 0017 status from "Proposed" → "Accepted". Blocker for B1 + B3 + B6. Medium. [579be54, e9732df — all six WCAG §5.3.1 required fields now populated on `ConformanceStatement`; `scope.commitHash` and `scope.configSnapshot` conditional-spread per the AI-first consumer model (no `""` or `{}` sentinel); `technologiesReliedUpon` defaults to `["HTML", "CSS", "ECMAScript", "WAI-ARIA"]`; ADR 0017 promoted Proposed → Accepted with an Implementation section documenting the present-when-meaningful deviations; MCP tool forwards `files[].filePath` + session-config snapshot; `renderConformanceMarkdown` now emits date / guidelines / conformance-level / scope / technologies sections in a WCAG §5.3.1 layout; nine new unit tests cover the shape.]
- [x] **V1-CERT-STATEMENT-SIGN** [bb79517, 15469b9, 5e434a1, b428896 — SignatureInput extended with per-file SHA-256 manifest (relative path keys), toolVersion, and a full configFingerprint covering standards/level/profile/rules/nativeWrappers/processes/additionalPaths; each new axis has its own ConformanceVerificationReason drift code (file-manifest-mismatch, tool-version-mismatch, config-profile-mismatch, config-rules-mismatch, config-native-wrappers-mismatch, config-processes-mismatch, config-additional-paths-mismatch); MCP tool computes commitHash via headSha (skip-with-warning `non_git_repo_signature_omitted` outside git), collects durable attestations, builds the file manifest over parsed sources, assembles the config fingerprint from LoadedConfig (falling back to session values on config-load failure), passes VERSION as toolVersion; 16 new tests cover reordering invariance, per-axis drift routing, full round-trip, minimal round-trip, and MCP-level signed-scan round-trip + tamper detection.]

### v1.0.0 — plugin API integrity

Source: 2026-04-20 architectural audit — two independent Explore passes converged on the same finding, verified by grep. `grep -rln "BUILTIN_RULES\|BUILTIN_STANDARDS" src/` returns 33 files; 31 of those are non-barrel leaks past the existing `src/engine/registry/{criteria,rules,standards}.ts` primitives. The leak means CLAUDE.md §3.8 is aspirational, not enforced, and ADR 0019's frozen `defineRule`/`defineStandard`/`defineCandidateFinder` exports function as type-inference helpers only — user-authored rules have no wiring path to `list_rules`, `suggest_fix`, or any scan. ADR 0022 has the full design and 7-commit migration plan. Dispatch sequential within this subsection: stages 1 → 2 → {3, 5} → 4 → 6 → 7, with 3/4/5 parallelizable across agents once 2 lands. Every stage is additive and reversible until V1-REGISTRY-LINT.

- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-AGGREGATE** (68e1240) New `src/engine/registry/registry.ts` wraps the existing `CriteriaRegistry` + `RulesRegistry` + `StandardsRegistry` primitives via a `Registry` class with `rules`, `standards`, `finders`, `criteria` arrays + `findRule` / `findStandard` / `findCriterion` / `rulesForCriterion` accessors. `rulesForCriterion` closes over the reciprocal equivalence graph — returns `readonly Rule[]` in input-position order so call-site swaps stay deterministic. `createBuiltinRegistry()` is the designated single-call site into `BUILTIN_RULES` / `BUILTIN_STANDARDS` / `BUILTIN_CANDIDATE_FINDERS`; no other `src/engine/**` file references those barrels. 20 unit tests cover accessor round-trips, transitive equivalence (both directions), input-order determinism, and a shipped-rule smoke (`media/alt-text-missing` surfaces under `wcag22:1.1.1` + declared equivalents). Pure addition — zero call-site change; stages 2–7 migrate consumers.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-SESSION** (06d6ea5) `McpSession` constructor now instantiates `createBuiltinRegistry()` and exposes it as `readonly registry: Registry`. Zero call-site migration — existing `BUILTIN_*` imports inside `src/mcp/**` untouched so stage 3+ can land incrementally. Three new tests in `tests/unit/mcp/session.test.ts` pin the construction contract + the `findRule("contrast/minimum")` / unknown-ID round-trip.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-HELPERS** (9ebfbce) Scope mis-sized. Four of the six call sites sit inside exported module-level helpers with no `session` parameter — `findRule` / `findStandard` / `firstUnknownStandard` / `satisfyingRulesForCriterion`. Dropping the `BUILTIN_*` barrels requires adding a `session` parameter to each, which cascades to ~9 single-line call-site updates across eight stage-4 files (`src/mcp/tools.ts`, `tool-suggest-fix.ts`, `tool-coverage.ts`, `tool-checklist.ts`, `tool-vpat.ts`, `tool-review-candidates.ts`, `tool-conformance-statement.ts`, `tool-attest.ts`). The `Registry` aggregate surface is sufficient — this is a helper-signature constraint, not an accessor gap. Two resolutions: (1) merge stages 3 + 4a into one commit that threads `session` through callers (net diff ≈20 lines outside `tools-helpers.ts`); (2) split stage 3 into 3a (migrate the two session-bearing sites, keep the four helpers reaching `BUILTIN_*` locally) + 3b (drop barrels alongside helper-signature change alongside stage 4). Prefer (1) on 2026-04-21 re-plan. Blocked per the `/continue` scope-lock; re-dispatch with widened scope.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-MCP-TOOLS** (6d6d6a8 — 6 atomic commits dd47e19..5d94912) Migrate `src/mcp/tools.ts` + `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` (16 files) to read through `session.registry`. Dispatch in parallel groups by family: {scan + scan-process + review-candidates}, {suggest-fix + apply-fix + apply-fix-internals}, {baseline + propose-baseline + attest}, {coverage + checklist + vpat + conformance-statement + explain-standard + list-suppressions + propose-config}, plus {completions + scan-project-budget + wrappers-meta}. Each group is its own commit under the 400-LOC budget. Blocks on V1-REGISTRY-HELPERS. Owner: main session + parallel agent dispatch. Medium (≈4 commits).
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-CLI** Landed across three commits (1a57d4b scan-family + 734490d explain-family + 1c46a6c list-family). All eleven `src/cli/commands/*.ts` hits and `src/cli/run.ts` now route through a `registry: Registry = createBuiltinRegistry()` parameter; `attest` dropped its local `criterionExists` / `satisfyingRulesForCriterion` helpers in favor of `registry.findCriterion` + `registry.rulesForCriterion` (equivalence-closure semantics now match MCP attest). Invariant: `rg "BUILTIN_(RULES|STANDARDS|CANDIDATE_FINDERS)" src/cli/` returns zero hits. `V1-REGISTRY-LINT` can now converge once `V1-REGISTRY-MCP-TOOLS` (blocked on `V1-REGISTRY-HELPERS`) lands.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-LINT** (f6c133a) New `scripts/check-builtins-scope.ts` modeled on `scripts/check-network-isolation.ts`. Forbids importing `BUILTIN_RULES` / `BUILTIN_STANDARDS` / `BUILTIN_CANDIDATE_FINDERS` outside `src/engine/registry/**`, the three barrel files themselves, and `tests/**`. Added to the `scripts/verify.ts` sequence (CLAUDE.md §4). Acceptance: `bun scripts/check-builtins-scope.ts` green. Blocks on V1-REGISTRY-MCP-TOOLS + V1-REGISTRY-CLI converging (every non-barrel call site must be migrated first). This is the commit that locks the invariant. Owner: main session. Small.
- [x] **V1-REGISTRY-PLUGIN-WIRE** `src/api/plugin.ts` helpers remain identity functions at the type level (ADR 0019 freeze), but `createRegistry({ rules, standards, finders })` now composes builtins with user-authored additions. `McpSession` + CLI bootstrap accept a registry override so a future `ra11y.config.ts` hook or CLI `--plugin` flag can thread one in. Working example under `examples/plugin-rule/` showing a user rule visible to `list_rules` and producing violations through `scan_project`. Integration test covering end-to-end plugin path. ADR 0022 flipped Proposed → Accepted. (b546831)

### v1.0.0 — MCP response-assembly seam

Source: 2026-04-20 architectural audit (Explore-agent survey of `src/mcp/**` + grep verification). Same class of leak as V1-REGISTRY-AGGREGATE but one layer up: the AI-first consumer doctrine in `docs/kb/architecture/ai-first-consumer.md` (conditional-spread on zero counts, `warnings` on zero-output success, split composite headline counters, present-when-meaningful optional fields, honest `fixesByClass` tally) is currently enforced in 24 separate `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` files by convention + the path-scoped rule at `.claude/rules/mcp-response-shapes.md`. Verified counts on 2026-04-20:

| measurement                                                                 | count |
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------|------:|
| `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` files                                                   | 24    |
| Tool files routing through `runScanAndFormat` (the existing near-assembler) | **2** (`tool-scan-project.ts`, `tool-scan-diff.ts`) |
| Tool files hand-rolling `meta: {` blocks directly                           | 10    |
| Tool files calling `buildAgentFinding` or composing `AgentFinding` manually | 8+    |
| `scan` + `scan_file` tool definitions                                       | inline in `src/mcp/tools.ts` (625 LOC mixing schemas + handlers) |
| `src/mcp/tools-helpers.ts` current size                                     | 856 LOC (hosts `runScanAndFormat` + fraying) |
| `scripts/check-response-nullability.ts` allowlist entries                   | 1 (catches a narrow slice; doesn't catch field-presence drift) |

The partial assembler `src/mcp/scan-assembly.ts` (`buildScanPlan` / `buildScanMeta` / `buildAnalysisCoverage` / `suppressionsMetaBlock` / `wrappersMetaBlock`) is already the right shape, but it lives underneath a non-authoritative wrapper (`runScanAndFormat`) and is only consumed by 2 of 24 tools. Every new scan-family field (future sampling outputs, new fix-class lanes, new `warnings` codes, new honest-counter splits) currently requires touching ≤24 sites with no CI gate forcing synchrony — silent cross-surface drift is the waiting failure mode, and the invariants called out in `ai-first-consumer.md` ("`scan` says 21, `checklist` says 4") are exactly what this seam prevents.

Why v1.0 blocker: the MCP response shape is part of the public contract v1.0 freezes (ADR 0019 + the migration table in `docs/migrations/0.1-to-0.2.md`). Freezing the shape before codifying the single-path invariant means third-party tooling pattern-matches on hand-rolled examples; changing the pattern later is a semver-breaking event. This is structurally identical to the Registry aggregate work (ADR 0022) — an internal seam that must be in place before the surface it backs becomes immutable.

Design captured in ADR 0024 (Proposed, 2026-04-20). Dispatch sequential within this subsection: stage 1 (assembler) → 2 (handler extract from `tools.ts`) → {3, 4, 5 parallel} → 6 (lint) → 7 (handler-size budget). Stages 3-5 are parallelizable across agents on separate worktrees (no shared files — each touches a disjoint set of `tool-*.ts`). Every stage is additive and reversible until V1-RESPONSE-LINT. Golden snapshots under `tests/snapshots/mcp/**` must stay byte-identical through every refactor commit — any intentional shape change splits into a separate `feat(mcp):` commit with rationale.

- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-ASSEMBLER** (e095fc9) New `src/mcp/response-assembler.ts` exporting `assembleScanFamilyResponse(input: ScanFamilyResponseInput, options?: ScanFamilyResponseOptions): ScanFamilyResponse`. `input` carries `violations, perRuleCoverage, reviewCandidates, discovery, wrappers, config, durationMs, toolName, scopeHint`; `options` carries `hoistReferenceGuide, includeReviewCandidates, tokenBudget`. Internally orchestrates the existing extracted helpers: `buildScanPlan` + `buildScanMeta` + `buildAnalysisCoverage` + `suppressionsMetaBlock` + `wrappersMetaBlock` + `buildAgentFinding` (per violation) + `buildReferenceGuide` + `dedupReviewCandidates` + `tokenBudget` clamping + `warnings` code emission (`scanned_zero_files`, `root_source_defaulted`, `no_config_found`, `tailwind_detected_css_undercounted`, `template_files_parsed_as_literal`). Pure addition — zero call-site change; stages 3-5 migrate consumers. Unit tests mirror the existing assembly-helper tests: honest-counter-split cases, conditional-spread fixesByClass, empty-scan warnings emission, reference-guide dedup, per-scope variants. Blocks on nothing. Owner: `feature-dev:code-architect`. Medium. ≤400 LOC net.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-CORE** (d32bdb8..7e7a523) `scan` + `scan_file` migrated through `assembleScanFamilyResponse`; handlers extracted to `src/mcp/tool-scan.ts` + `src/mcp/tool-scan-file.ts`; `src/mcp/scan-collect.ts` added as `runScanAndCollect` seam helper. `scan_process` deferred — per-page `ScanResult[]` shape is structurally different and warrants separate analysis; split into V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-PROCESS below. 4 commits: 1b273e1 64c5f21 ac7f760 61be322.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-PROCESS** Decision: `scan_process` stays OUTSIDE `assembleScanFamilyResponse`. Three converging reasons: (1) `perPageResults: ScanResult[]` is the documented primitive (ADR 0016) future process-level finders cross-index — collapsing N pages into one `files[]` discards the per-page boundary; (2) `meta` is process-level (`processName`, `resolvedProcess`, `missingPages`) not scan-level (`activeNativeWrappers`, `rulesEvaluated`, …) — merging would violate "ambiguous field shapes are dishonest"; (3) looping the assembler per-page would force composing a dishonest composite plan/meta across pages. Rationale lives as a durable docblock at the top of `src/mcp/tool-scan-process.ts` so V1-RESPONSE-LINT's allowlist entry can cite the file by path. No code changes to the handler; no snapshot drift.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-DERIVATIVE** (7007df8..7b2f49a) Migrated `checklist` + `coverage` + `conformance_statement` handlers through `buildDerivativeScanWarnings` assembler seam. `buildDerivativeScanWarnings` exported from `src/mcp/response-assembler.ts`; cross-tool invariant test guards `scan.totalFindings === checklist.automatedFindings + checklist.manualReviewRequired` drift class. No snapshot drift (derivative tools use bespoke shapes). 2 commits.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-FIX-FAMILY** — **DECISION 2026-04-21: pick (a), narrow scope.** Share the assembler's `groupByFile` helper for `baseline.check` only; drop `apply_fix` + `suggest_fix` from scope. Preserves the byte-identical-snapshots acceptance gate that v1.0.0 depends on. `apply_fix` and `suggest_fix` legitimately emit different shapes (before/after slices, primary/alternative fix paths) — forcing them through the assembler would sum categorically different data, which the "composite headline counts are dishonest" rule forbids. ADR 0024's lint gate (scripts/check-response-assembly.ts), LOC budget, and assembler for scan-core + scan-derivative tools all still land. If post-v1 an agent reports cross-tool drift between `baseline` and `scan_project`, revisit — structurally the fix-family responses carry different data.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-LINT** New `scripts/check-response-assembly.ts` modeled on `scripts/check-network-isolation.ts` + `scripts/check-response-nullability.ts`. Statically forbids scan-family tool files from defining object literals with a `plan` key or a `meta: {` block without routing through `assembleScanFamilyResponse`. Allowlist: `src/mcp/response-assembler.ts`, `src/mcp/scan-assembly.ts` (the underlying helpers), non-scan-family tools (`attest`, `suppress`, `list_rules`, `list_suppressions`, `explain_*`, `bootstrap`, `detect_native_wrappers`, `wrapper_introspect`, `sessionConfigure`), and `tests/**`. Added to the `scripts/verify.ts` sequence (CLAUDE.md §4). Acceptance: `bun scripts/check-response-assembly.ts` green; dry-run synthetic test (temporarily hand-roll a `plan:` block in `tool-coverage.ts`, confirm the check fails, revert) passes before landing. This is the commit that locks the invariant. Blocks on V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-CORE + V1-RESPONSE-SCAN-DERIVATIVE + V1-RESPONSE-FIX-FAMILY converging. Owner: main session. Small.
- [x] **V1-RESPONSE-HANDLER-BUDGET** Enforce `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` ≤ 150 LOC after migration via an extension to `scripts/check-limits.ts`. Handlers that still exceed the budget are doing work that belongs in the assembler — keep extracting until they fit (or carry an inline `// ra11y-limits-exempt: <reason>` pragma, matching the existing limits-check escape). Acceptance: `bun scripts/check-limits.ts` green; aggregate `src/mcp/tool-*.ts` LOC drops from 8,485 to ≤ 3,600 (≈150 × 24). Blocks on V1-RESPONSE-LINT. Owner: main session. Small. Final commit of the subsection — flips ADR 0024 status Proposed → Accepted.

Headroom watch: cold-start grows with CLI module graph; 1000-file throughput scales linearly with rule count.

New empty `## [Unreleased]` sits at top. Ready for `/release 1.0.0` when the user triggers.

### v1.0.0 — detection gaps

Source: "find all violations" bar re-raised 2026-04-19. Four-agent gap audit against WCAG 2.2 A+AA surface. Each item widens detection on an existing rule or adds a small new primitive — none reshape the engine. Gaps are where a real violation on common production patterns walks past our rules undetected. Dispatch in parallel — independent files.

- [x] **V1-DETECT-ALT-WIDEN** (db325c9, a174d62) `src/rules/media/alt-text-missing.ts` today only walks `<img>` and `<input type="image">` (line 13 header). Real violation patterns miss: (a) SVG `<image src="…">` inside `<svg>`; (b) `role="img"` on arbitrary elements (`<div role="img">`, `<span role="img">`); (c) `<canvas>` with no fallback text + no aria-label/aria-labelledby. Widen element walk + apply existing `hasAccessibleName*` logic. Satisfies wcag22:1.1.1, wcag21:1.1.1. Fix scope: rule extension; add three walkers with the same accessible-name check that runs today on `<img>`. Tests: `tests/fixtures/{good,bad}/alt-text-missing/` gains svg-image, role-img-div, canvas-no-fallback cases. Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-DETECT-BUTTON-NAME-SVG** (2af5caf) `src/rules/semantics/button-name.ts:130` uses `htmlTextContent` which walks HtmlElement + HtmlText but does NOT descend into SVG `<text>` / `<title>` children. `<button><svg><title>Close</title></svg></button>` computes as name-less today but is accessibly named in every major AT. Same blind spot for `<a>`/interactive controls. Extend `hasAccessibleNameHtml`/`hasAccessibleNameJsx` to walk SVG `<title>` + `<text>` as direct children of interactive controls. Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2. Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-DETECT-BUTTON-NAME-IMAGE-INPUT** (2af5caf) `src/rules/semantics/button-name.ts:155-173` filters `["button", "submit", "reset"]` only; `<input type="image" src=... title="Submit">` is never checked. Alt-text rule covers the `alt` path but not the `title`-as-name path — so a `title`-named image input reports no violation correctly, but a name-less one also reports no violation (false negative). Add `type="image"` to the button-name filter and recognize `alt` OR `title` OR `aria-label` as valid name sources. Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2, wcag22:1.1.1. Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-DETECT-LABELS-CONTENTEDITABLE** (e8ca208) `src/rules/forms/labels-required.ts:38` `LABELABLE_TAGS = ["input","select","textarea"]`. `<div contenteditable="true">` acts as a form control (Slack-style composers, Notion-style editors) and has the same labeling requirement under WCAG 1.3.2 + 3.3.2 but is not checked. Extend the element set to include `contenteditable="true"` (not `false` or `inherit`). Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-DETECT-CONTRAST-BG-IMAGE** (431cd1b, 6c49c2b) `src/rules/contrast/_shared.ts:114` resolves only `background-color` and `background` (color-only shorthand). Text over `background-image: url(…)` / `background: linear-gradient(…)` currently skips contrast evaluation silently — no violation, no `couldBeWrongBecause` signal. Two paths: (a) emit `couldBeWrongBecause: "background_image_unresolvable"` as additive signal for affected declarations (honest surfacing, no false positives); (b) defer luminance heuristic for gradients to a follow-up. Path (a) is the v1.0 scope. Effort: small.

### v1.0.0 — shape-honesty sweep

Source: same audit. Verified file:line hits on shape-honesty violations that survived Q/Q2/Q2R2. Every item below is a null-sentinel that CLAUDE.md §1 "Ambiguous field shapes are dishonest" calls out directly — forcing agents to disambiguate "field unavailable" vs "field empty."

- [x] **V1-SHAPE-NULL-SWEEP** (6b6349c) Replace 9 response-field null/empty sentinels with conditional-spread:
    - `src/mcp/tool-explain-standard.ts:65` `publisher: standard.publisher ?? null`
    - `src/mcp/tool-explain-standard.ts:66` `url: standard.url ?? null`
    - `src/mcp/tool-explain-standard.ts:68` `levelFilterApplied: level ?? null`
    - `src/mcp/tool-explain-standard.ts:75` `url: c.url ?? null`
    - `src/mcp/tool-explain-standard.ts:76` `equivalentTo: c.equivalentTo ?? []`
    - `src/mcp/rule-catalog.ts:77` `normativeQuote: rule.docs.normativeQuote ?? null`
    - `src/mcp/tool-review-candidates.ts:177` `title: criterion?.title ?? null`
    - `src/mcp/tool-review-candidates.ts:178` `level: criterion?.level ?? null`
    - `src/mcp/next-step.ts:147` `singleFilePath: options.singleFilePath ?? null`
  Exclude `detect-wrappers-core.ts:107` (`definitionFile`) — intentional per Q2-WRAPPATH ("`null` is emitted, not omitted — the attempt is meaningful"). Use conditional-spread: `...(x ? { field: x } : {})`. Audit `tests/integration/mcp-*.test.ts` for any snapshot assertions relying on the null — those are the contract tests to update. Effort: small.
- [x] **V1-SHAPE-NULL-AUDIT** Lint check shipped at `scripts/check-response-nullability.ts` (472e18b) and wired into `scripts/verify.ts` as `response-nullability`. Uses the TypeScript compiler API (not regex) to walk ancestors for `textResult(...)` / `errorResult(...)` calls — input-param normalization passes structurally, no carve-out needed. Catches `?? null`, `?? []`, `?? ""`, `?? 0`, `?? \`\``. Pre-loaded allowlist for the Q2-WRAPPATH `definitionFile` exception. 10 unit tests at `tests/unit/scripts/check-response-nullability.test.ts`. Backlog flip was lost in the 2026-04-19 history rewrite; restored here.

[d76dcfb + aeb5ddc — new `describeJsxExpressionIntent(src: string | null)` helper is text-level and deterministic: navigation identifiers (navigate/router/history/redirect/location/goto/useNavigate/useRouter), `window.location`/`document.location`, and member-call `.push(` / `.replace(` / `.go(`; mutation identifiers (toggle/open/close/show/hide/dispatch/mutate/delete/save/submit/setState), React setter convention `set[A-Z]…(`, and camelCase verb-prefix form (`toggleMenu`, `openDialog`, `closeModal`); navigation wins ties; unknown honest-fallback keeps weak-evidence cases out of heuristic suppression. [cb70741 — `looksHeaderShaped` gates on ≤40 chars, starts-upper, non-numeric (currency/percent/signed rejected), and rejects ALL-CAPS multi-word shouts. `classifyHeaders` requires ≥2 cells and ≥60% header-shaped to populate each axis. Four-branch ladder in `buildSuggestionFromDetection`: (1) both axes → inline row + column candidates and mention scope="col" / scope="row" plus scope="colgroup" for complex tables; (2) row-only → inline detected column headers (e.g. `Name`, `Email`, `Role`, `Last Login`) and recommend scope="col"; (3) col-only → inline detected row labels and recommend scope="row"; (4) no signal → fallback naming both scopes and the role="presentation" layout escape. ### v1.0.0 — external-codebase scan findings

- [x] **V1-DETECT-SILENT-EXT** `src/utils/path.ts:20-28` `PARSEABLE_EXTENSIONS` is `{.ts,.tsx,.js,.jsx,.html,.htm,.css}`. `hasParseableExtension` + `src/input/discover.ts` silently skip every other extension — no surfacing anywhere in the scan meta. On an Astro+SCSS tree, 226 source files (104 `.astro`, 122 `.scss`) were dropped at discovery; `filesScanned: 125` reads as "tool covered everything" when the tool actually ignored 64% of the repo. Landed: `discoverFilesWithDiagnostics` + `parseFilesWithDiagnostics` + warning code `extensions_skipped_no_parser` + `meta.analysisCoverage.skippedByExtension` wired through `runScanAndFormat`; `scan_project` threads the diagnostic. Follow-up: migrate remaining scan-surface tools (scan, scan_file, scan_diff) to `parseFilesWithDiagnostics` so they also emit the warning.
- [x] **V1-SHAPE-SNIPPET-EMPTY** `src/output/agent-response/build-finding.ts` + `src/output/agent-response/types.ts` — `buildSnippet` returned `{ before: [], highlighted: v.snippet ?? "", after: [] }`; `before` / `after` had zero writers anywhere in src/. Landed: `AgentFinding.snippet` is now `string | undefined`, omitted via conditional-spread when absent; `AgentSnippet` type removed; snapshot tests + CHANGELOG Breaking Changes entry updated.
- [x] **V1-SIZE-LABEL-ECHO-CAP** `truncateForEcho(text, max = 200)` helper added to `src/engine/ast-helpers.ts` (dda1414); single U+2026 ellipsis. Threaded through 13 rule files (227725b) — the three backlog-named offenders plus 10 additional sites discovered in the audit grep: `media/alt-text-missing` (4 sites), `semantics/button-name`, `document/iframe-title`, `tooltip/dismissable` (replaced bespoke truncator), `media/video-captions-missing`, `layout/reflow-hardcoded-width`, `parsing/duplicate-id`, `parsing/html-has-lang`, `document/lang-on-parts`, `document/meta-refresh`, `motion/pause-stop-hide`, `navigation/skip-link`. Read-only / controlled-vocabulary hits explicitly skipped (ARIA enums, table cell ≤40 cap, accesskey single chars). Helper unit tests + invariant assertions on label-in-name. `pointer/target-size` retains its own 40-char cap (intentional, tighter than default).
- [x] **V1-SIZE-RESPONSE-BUDGET** ADR 0021 (`docs/adr/0021-scan-response-size-budget.md`) accepted option (a): lower `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT` 200 → 25 in `src/mcp/tool-scan-project.ts` (c1945fa). Calibration from a 16-fixture measurement corpus (real-world + synthetic): bytes-per-file is stable ~2.9–6.5 KB across profiles; 25 keeps typical responses under 100 KB and caps worst-case label-heavy at ~163 KB with honest `truncated` + `nextOffset` signals. 200-file responses were 1.24 MB — factor-of-8 cut, not marginal tuning. New unit test asserts default-cap triggers truncation at 25 on a 30-file fixture. Token-aware budget (option b) rejected to preserve existing `limit`/`offset` contract semantics consumers already understand.
- [x] **V1-SHAPE-FIXCLASS-HEADLINE** `src/mcp/scan-assembly.ts` plan-summary builder — returned `plan.summary: "129 violations (129 guidance fixes). 2 notes… 4 actionable manual review items + 17 untargeted criteria"`. The parenthetical sums four categorically different `fixClass` sub-buckets (`mechanical` 31, `guidance` 30, `runtime-only` 24, `verify-in-source` 46) under one "guidance fixes" label. Directly violates `@docs/kb/architecture/ai-first-consumer.md` §"Composite headline counts are dishonest" — agents budget against the summary string and get four distinct work kinds conflated. Fix: either stop summing in the prose ("129 violations (31 mechanical, 30 guidance, 24 runtime-only, 46 verify-in-source)") or drop the parenthetical and rely on the per-finding `fixClass` discriminator (already shipped in Q2-FIXKIND). Effort: small. Landed `33a1978` (Option A — per-lane breakdown, zero-count lanes omitted). Follow-ups: (1) rename top-level `mechanicalEditsAvailable`/`guidanceFixesAvailable` fields to avoid the fixClass enum-name collision; (2) `src/output/agent-response/build-plan.ts` CLI `--format agent` summary uses the same composite "mechanical edits / guidance fixes" pattern and warrants the same split.
- [x] **V1-SHAPE-RULECOV-COUNT** `findingsEmitted: number` added to `PerRuleCoverage` (`src/types/violation.ts:261-268`) and stamped on every entry in `buildPerRuleCoverage` via a new `countFindingsByRule` linear pass over `allViolations` (f035908). Always present, zero-is-meaningful per the AI-first consumer doctrine. Scanner now passes `allViolations` (pre-severity-filter) to keep telemetry honest about what the engine observed. Unit + integration tests assert `findingsEmitted === violation-stream-count` invariant; existing test fixtures updated to populate the new required field. Counter was NOT pre-computed (backlog hint inaccurate); the linear pass adds negligible cost.
