Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest; labels must make kind explicit.
- [ ] **V1-CONFIG-NOTE-COLLAPSE-REDUNDANT-PROSE** `meta.configNote` ships a ~260-char prose string ("No ra11y.config found walking up from … — using built-in defaults …") on every scan when `configSource: null` — duplicating the `no_config_found` structured warning code. Fix: collapse to `configNote: true` when `warnings` contains `no_config_found`, rely on the structured warning for details; keep the prose field only when the detail differs from the code's documented meaning. Pairs with V1-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT-REGRESSION — same triple-readout concern, simpler axis. Low priority. Doctrine: verbose meta is signal, but redundant prose alongside a structured code is bloat.
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-HEADLINE-COMPOSITE-SPLIT** `checklist.summary.headline` renders `"12 actionable · 12 untargeted · 0 likely irrelevant"` on one line — while `meta.plan.actionableManualItems` + `untargetedCriteria` live separately as structured counters. The adjacent rendering invites agents to sum "12+12 = 24 things to review." Fix: drop the one-line composite headline; keep the structured per-kind counters; rename the headline string to explicitly name the split (`"12 actionable items; 12 untargeted criteria; 0 likely irrelevant criteria"`) OR emit as `headline: { actionable: 12, untargeted: 12, likelyIrrelevant: 0 }` object. Minor but canonical composite-headline-lie shape. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest.

Tool-orchestration / bootstrap / suppress / write-gate:

- [ ] **V1-BOOTSTRAP-SUGGESTED-CONFIG-EXCLUDE-LOUD** `bootstrap` tool emits a `suggestedConfig.exclude` mechanically derived from `scannedBuildArtifacts` entries — on one design-system scan the suggestion was `exclude: ["js/**", "scss/_functions.scss", "site/**"]`, which covers every source tree in the project (54 mis-classified artifact entries × a `.*/**` generalization). An agent pasting this into `ra11y.config.ts` would drop 2273 findings to near-zero. Fix: cap `suggestedConfig.exclude` at a conservative set (≤ 3 entries, require ≥N independent mis-classification signals per entry) OR route build-artifact paths through a commented-out `likely-build-paths` block the agent must opt into. Never emit blanket `js/**` / `site/**` excludes from artifact heuristics. Pairs with V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REGRESSION-AUDIT-MINIFIED downstream — but even with the classifier fixed, the generator should not amplify single heuristic signals into tree-sized excludes. Doctrine: surface-don't-suppress, applied at the generator step (an agent can't un-exclude a tree that was never parsed).
- [ ] **V1-SUPPRESS-MARKDOWN-FILE-UNSUPPORTED** `suppress({file: "…/docs.md", ruleId: "aria/role-from-class-only", reason: …})` rejects with `{code: "file-unsupported", error: "suppress handles .tsx/.jsx, .ts/.js, .html/.htm, and .css only."}` — yet the scanner flags findings on `.md` / `.markdown` files (hundreds of findings across `aria/role-from-class-only`, `semantics/heading-hierarchy`, `semantics/table-*` on SSG projects), and the scan response echoes a `suppressWith: "// ra11y-disable-next-line aria/role-from-class-only"` shape that the suppress tool itself refuses to apply. The MCP workflow doctrine ("dismiss by reading, suppress at source only when genuinely N/A") is unimplementable for the majority of SSG findings. Fix: extend `suppress` to accept `.md` / `.markdown` and emit the correct pragma shape — `<!-- ra11y-disable-next-line ruleId: reason -->` for embedded-HTML findings, OR a dedicated kramdown IAL / `{::comment}` form. Update the echoed `suppressWith` field to match the comment-kind of the target file extension. Sub-item of the `ra11y-disable` pragma family (pairs with V1-PRAGMA-LIQUID-COMMENT-SYNTAX, which is the reader half; this is the writer half). Doctrine: the deterministic dismissal escape hatch must be usable on every file the scanner flags.
- [ ] **V1-PLAN-SAFE-EDITS-STALE-MCP-SERVER** Commit `fce189c8 refactor(mcp): drop plan.safeEditsAvailable composite counter` removed `plan.safeEditsAvailable` per the composite-headline-lies doctrine — but the live MCP server still emits it on both `scan_project` and `bootstrap` responses. Either (a) the MCP subprocess is serving a pre-drop bundle, (b) the MCP server's build artifact isn't being picked up by `npm link` / the smoke harness, or (c) the drop landed in one code path but not the other. Fix: audit the MCP server build / release pipeline — add a CI smoke test that scans a fixture and asserts `plan.safeEditsAvailable` is absent from the response; confirm the dropped-field refactor reaches every invocation path. Doctrine: doctrine changes must propagate to the shipped artifact, not just the source.
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-LIMIT-NEGATIVE-VALIDATION** `checklist({limit: -1})` silently clamps to 1 per the tool's documented clamp range [1, 2000]; the caller passed a clearly-invalid value and received paginated-to-one-entry results rather than a validation error. Fix: reject any `limit < 1` with `{code: "invalid-argument", field: "limit", remediation: "limit must be in [1, 2000]"}`. Same treatment for the `maxCandidatesPerCriterion` bound. Mechanical. Doctrine: silent coercion on invalid input masks caller bugs.

Checklist / review-candidate shape:

- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-MDX-BACKTICKS** The iframe-review finder's source-text substrate does not strip MDX inline-code spans (backticks) or fenced code blocks before scanning — on `site/src/content/docs/helpers/ratio.mdx:14,19` it flags prose `` "**Pro-Tip!** You don't need `frameborder="0"` on your `<iframe>`s" `` as three 1.2.x candidates with `confidence: "high"`, while the real `<iframe>` inside `<Example code={…}>` on line 22 goes un-flagged. Mirrors Q6-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-FINDER-JS-STRING-LITERALS (JS string literals) for MDX. Fix: the iframe finder's source-text path must strip inline-code spans (`` `…` ``) and fenced code blocks (```` ``` ```` ... ```` ``` ````) in `.md` / `.mdx` before the iframe-token match. Downgrade `confidence` to at most `"medium"` when the matching token sits inside what the source looks like a prose container. Pair fixtures: `tests/fixtures/real-world/mdx-iframe-in-backticks/`. Doctrine: confidence `"high"` on a backtick-escaped prose match is dishonest.
- [ ] **V1-REVIEW-CANDIDATES-SIBLING-REASON-DEDUP** `reviewCandidates[*].reason` duplicates prose across sibling findings for the same criterion — on `sound-board/index.html` six `<audio>` elements emit 6 identical candidates per criterion for `wcag22:1.2.1`, `1.2.3`, `1.2.5` (18 rows with 3 unique reason strings). The `reviewCandidates.prompts[criterionId].text` dedup already exists for top-level prompts but per-finding `reason` strings are not deduped by `(ruleId, criterionId, reason)` tuple. Fix: hoist the shared reason to `prompts[criterionId].genericReason` alongside existing `.text`, keep per-location `snippet`; OR cap sibling candidates of the same shape at `N=3` + emit `additionalSiblingCount: <rest>`. Doctrine-consistent precedent: the prompt-text dedup is the same move one level up.
- [ ] **V1-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MARKDOWN-FIRES-DESPITE-HINT** `semantics/heading-hierarchy` fires "Document has no `<h1>`, first heading is `<h5>`" on every SSG Markdown file (29 findings, all admonition `<h5>`s being the only embedded-HTML headings) — even though `meta.analysisCoverage.hints` explicitly says "heading hierarchy [is] not checked" on Markdown-as-HTML-residue scans. One of the two surfaces is lying. Fix: actually skip the rule on Markdown-residue files (or any file where the parser saw embedded-HTML headings but the file's ATX `#`-headings were invisible), OR reason-enrich each finding with "partial-document-view: Markdown `#`-headings were not parsed; heading outline may be well-formed in the rendered output." Distinct from V1-HEADING-HIERARCHY-MARKDOWN-SELF-CONTRADICTION (the design-system README manifestation: ATX top-level heading); this item's manifestation is admonition-only headings in a file that has well-structured ATX headings upstream. Doctrine: self-reported coverage that contradicts what actually runs is dishonest.
- [ ] **V1-CONFORMANCE-SCOPE-FILES-MINIFIED-LEAK** `conformance_statement.scope.files[]` lists files that `scan_project` labeled `reason: "minified"` in `scannedBuildArtifacts` — claiming coverage the scan did not produce. On the `scope.files` enumeration includes `rotating-nav-animation/index.html` and `sticky-navigation/index.html`, both in `meta.scannedBuildArtifacts` and both evaluated by zero rules. A procurement reviewer reads "156 files in scope" but two of them contributed nothing. Fix: either (a) omit build-artifact-labeled files from `scope.files[]` and surface them in `scope.skippedFiles: [{path, reason}]`, or (b) annotate each entry in `scope.files[]` with a per-file `evaluated: boolean` flag. Pairs with V1-CONFORMANCE-SCOPE-FILES-CAP. Doctrine: composite headline counts are dishonest — a file count that sums "actually evaluated" with "skipped as build artifact" under one "scope.filesCount" is the same bug at the procurement-surface layer.

Minification / build-artifact classification:

- [ ] **V1-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-ENVELOPE-DRIFT** `meta.scannedBuildArtifacts` is documented (`src/mcp/build-artifacts.ts:593-666`) and typed as `BuildArtifactsGrouped { grouped, ungrouped, ungroupedTruncated? }` with `capMetaArray` applied to the ungrouped tail, but the on-wire response for `scan_project` on a template catalog ships **1168 flat `{path, reason}` entries** (~148 KB) — zero occurrences of `grouped`, `ungrouped`, `basename`, `suggestedGlob`, or `ungroupedTruncated` reach the wire. The grouper is called at `src/mcp/tool-scan-project.ts:1108-1113` but the serialization path between `groupBuildArtifactsByBasename` and the wire drops the envelope. This is the single largest source of response-bloat (responsible for `effectiveLimit: 1` truncations on every `scan_project` call at scale — body bodies are dropped so the flat SBA tail fits) and directly contradicts the Q-SHARED-META-ARRAY-BUDGET-CAP work. `metaMode: "delta"` cannot help either, because the delta baseline contains the same 148 KB payload. Fix: audit the serialization path between `groupBuildArtifactsByBasename` and the response root; post-fix grep the wire payload for `grouped` / `ungrouped` / `ungroupedTruncated` (must be present). Distinct from V1-MINIFIED-FILE-SCAN-KIND-SPLIT (per-kind counter) — this is the pre-existing grouped-envelope that isn't reaching the wire. Doctrine: the shape on the wire must match the type; advertising an envelope that flattens on emission is dishonest.
- [ ] **V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-CLASSIFIER-BYPASS-HTML-FP** `classifyBuildArtifact` called directly on a template catalog' mis-classified authored HTML files (e.g. `above-educational-template/contact.html` — 213 lines, one line > 500 chars, median 27, ratio 0.5 %) returns `null` — so the classifier itself did NOT classify the file as `"minified"`. Yet the file reaches the wire labeled `reason: "minified"` (459 non-`.min.*` files on this corpus; top filenames `jquery.fancybox.pack.js ×64`, `jquery.js ×49`, `index.html ×40`, `html5shiv.js ×38`, `bootstrap.css ×33`, `contact.html ×18`, `custom-fonts.css ×18`). The label enters via a path that bypasses the per-file classifier. Fix: trace the path from `collectBuildArtifacts` / the `buildArtifactsFields` assembly down to every site that can stamp `reason: "minified"` on an entry, and confirm every stamp site routes through `classifyBuildArtifact`. Pairs with V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-REGRESSION-AUDIT-MINIFIED, but distinct — that item investigates classifier predicate ordering; this item traces the non-classifier bypass. Doctrine: labels must be provable from the code; a label that says `minified` on a 213-line authored HTML file is dishonest.
- [ ] **V1-OPAQUE-COMPONENT-NAMES-MINIFIED-TOKEN-LEAK** `opaqueCustomComponentNames` at scale contains `["AG.y", "B", "H.length", "J", "J.length", "Math.abs"]` — not React components but minified JS symbol fragments the tsx parser captured from minified bundles. An agent reading the list might add `Math.abs` to their `nativeWrappers` config. Fix: before emitting, filter the candidate list by `(a) contains "." (member-access path)` → drop; `(b) matches a well-known global prototype (Math, Object, Array, JSON, Reflect, Symbol, Proxy)` → drop; `(c) single-uppercase-letter with numeric suffix (A1, B2, etc.)` → drop. Same file set that triggers this is the `.js`-parsed-as-TSX shape covered by V1-JS-PARSE-ERROR-REASON-MISLEADING. Doctrine: labeled buckets are only honest when provable from the code; "PascalCase-ish token seen in a parsed file" is not provably a React component.

Catalog-scale perf / scoping:

- [ ] **V1-PATTERN-ID-DEAD-INFRASTRUCTURE** Commit `feba3df8` added `patternId` (cross-template fingerprint) to `Violation` with the engine stamping at `src/engine/rule-runner.ts:182` and `src/engine/stamp-project-emission.ts:47` — both gate on `emitted.snippet` being a non-empty string. Grep of `src/rules/` for `snippet:` returns **zero** hits — no in-tree rule populates the `snippet` field that the stamp predicate requires. Verified on a 40,132-finding at-scale scan: `patternId` occurrences in the response = 0. Particularly fatal at this scale: the three biggest-volume rules (`motion/pause-stop-hide`, `contrast/minimum`, `contrast/non-text` — ~43 % of findings combined) are CSS-declaration rules with no natural snippet source, so `patternId` can never reach them under the current design. Fix: decide — either (a) populate `emitted.snippet` on at least one snippet-emitting rule (HTML/JSX shape rules are the realistic candidates: `navigation/href-javascript-void`, `semantics/button-name`, `aria/expanded-on-disclosure`, `media/alt-text-missing`) so `patternId` is non-empty on at least one finding family; OR (b) remove the type field and delete the stamp sites — current state advertises an affordance that no finding carries. Pairs with Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open — proposes the cross-template fingerprint for CSS findings via a different hash path that does not depend on `snippet`). Doctrine: advertising an affordance that never reaches the wire is dishonest.
- [ ] **V1-CSS-CROSS-TEMPLATE-FINGERPRINT** CSS-declaration rules (`motion/pause-stop-hide` 8956, `contrast/minimum` 5458, `contrast/non-text` 2805) fire tens of thousands of times on a template catalog where 117 near-identical copies of `bootstrap.css` ship across 172 template dirs. `patternId` cannot fingerprint these (see V1-PATTERN-ID-DEAD-INFRASTRUCTURE — it gates on `snippet`) and `groupKey` operates per-file, so 117 identical `.img-thumbnail` transitions collapse to 117 distinct finding groups. Fix: add a CSS-rule-specific fingerprint — `cssPatternId = hash(ruleId + selectorFamily + propertyFamily + valueShape)` — that collapses "same selector + same declaration shape" across files. Likely reduces the 17k CSS-rule findings on this corpus to ~40 canonical patterns. Pairs with Q6-CONTRAST-VENDOR-CSS-CROSS-FILE-DEDUPE (open, same-basename) and Q6-PATTERN-FINGERPRINT-CROSS-TEMPLATE (open, HTML/JSX element-level). This item is the CSS-declaration sibling that neither covers. Doctrine: the agent has no honest way to say "this is one canonical a canonical CSS transition, 117 copies" without such an affordance.
- [ ] **V1-PROJECT-DIRECTORY-ROLLUP** is a mono-repo of 50 independent mini-projects under one root; `scan_project` treats it as one repo and merges findings. `meta.perRuleCoverage.findingsEmitted` tells per-rule but not per-project / per-directory. An agent triaging a mono-repo-of-demos or a website-template catalog benefits from `plan.topDirectories: [{path, violationCount, topRule}]` — rank-ordered to expose uneven distribution and let the agent pick the sub-tree that matters first. Additive extension of V1-CROSS-FILE-ROLLUP-PRIMITIVE (which proposes `plan.topRules`). Doctrine: one tool call should answer "what next?" — on a 50-project mono-repo, per-project scoping is the first "what next" question.
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-MAX-CANDIDATES-DEFAULT-LOWER** `checklist({limit: 5})` on a template catalog returns 84,879 chars — one criterion (`wcag22:1.3.2 Meaningful Sequence`) alone carries enough candidates to blow the MCP host token ceiling even with `perCriterionClipped: true`. The per-criterion clip fires but the default `maxCandidatesPerCriterion` is too high for bulk catalogs. Fix: lower the default `maxCandidatesPerCriterion` to ≤ 20 (from current default) AND emit a `maxCandidatesPerCriterionHint: N` that the caller can raise via the input param if they want a deeper cut. Pairs with V1-CHECKLIST-PERCRITERION-CURSOR — that item adds the cursor; this item tightens the default to match realistic host budgets. Doctrine: response-size-first defaults are load-bearing for usability on catalogs.

Rule correctness / detection (false positives + missing scope):

- [x] **V1-RULE-ICON-FONT-HIDDEN-TITLE-NOT-NAME** `aria/icon-font-hidden` accepts `title` as a confirmed accessible name (verified at `src/rules/aria/icon-font-hidden.ts:265-266` for HTML, `:291-292` for JSX). On a social-icon row `<a href="#" title="Facebook"><i class="fa fa-facebook"></i></a>` the rule emits "the parent `<a>` 'Facebook' already carries the accessible name" and `suggest_fix` recommends `aria-hidden="true"` on the `<i>` — applying that guidance regresses the link to no reliable accessible name (VoiceOver iOS does not announce `title`; WAI-ARIA APG warns against title-as-only-name). Pattern reproduces 5×/file across templates, hundreds of times at scale. Fix: drop `title` from `accessibleNameSource()`; promote the case to a sibling `link-name-relies-on-title` finding instead. Doctrine: surface-don't-suppress, but a fix-suggestion that teaches an anti-pattern is worse than silent miss because it propagates the regression.
- [x] **V1-RULE-HREF-EMPTY-NOT-FLAGGED** `navigation/href-javascript-void` short-circuits on `href=""` returning `null` (verified at `src/rules/navigation/href-javascript-void.ts:72`) with the comment "link-no-href's domain, not ours" — but `link-no-href` only fires on absent attribute, not on present-empty-string. Result: `<a href="">Forgot password?</a>` (atlanta signin.html:95 + 6 social-row anchors at :141-146) escapes both rules. WCAG 4.1.2: empty href reloads the page — same broken affordance as `href="#"`. Fix: extend the predicate to treat `href=""` like `href="#"`, OR widen `link-no-href` to include the empty-string case.
- [x] **V1-RULE-LINK-NO-HREF-MISSED-ON-ATTRIBUTE-ABSENT** `<a class="prev"><i class="fa fa-chevron-circle-left"></i></a>` on agile-agency `index.html:159` has NO `href` attribute (carousel control wired at runtime). `navigation/link-no-href` does not fire; `navigation/link-descriptive-text` also stays silent. The control is not keyboard-focusable AND has no accessible name. Fix: ensure `link-no-href` fires on absent-href anchors that have an interactive class signal (`btn`, `nav-link`, `prev`, `next`) OR a JS click handler in a sibling `<script>`. Pairs with V1-RULE-HREF-EMPTY-NOT-FLAGGED.
- [x] **V1-RULE-FORM-LABEL-ADJACENT-INPUT-WITHOUT-ID** `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` fires on adjacent textarea+label pairs but skips `<label>Name</label><input type="text">` siblings where neither has `id`/`for`. On aerosky `contact.html:82-110` (5 form rows) it emits the textarea finding (line 106) and skips the 4 `<input>` rows above. Predicate likely requires the input to have an `id` so it can confirm the label *could* associate. Fix: also fire when both label has no `for` AND input has no `id` — the canonical "you forgot the for/id pair" template mistake. Highest-volume FN on this input set.
- [x] **V1-RULE-CONTRAST-MINIMUM-SCSS-ELIGIBILITY** `contrast/minimum` is gated on `.css`/`.html`/`.htm` only (verified at `src/rules/contrast/minimum.ts:91-93`). On an SSG docs-site scan, 18 `.scss` files exist with documented sub-AA color choices (`.shell .output { color: #888 }` on `#171717` ≈ 4.46:1; `.c { color: #818181 }` on `#212121` ≈ 4.5:1) — `contrast/minimum` perRuleCoverage shows `filesEvaluated:0`. Highest-leverage semantic miss across SSG ecosystems (Jekyll, Hugo, Middleman, Eleventy all ship SCSS source). Fix: add `.scss`, `.sass`, `.less` to `appliesTo.fileExtensions`. CSS variables and Sass interpolation can be deferred (`crossFileCapable: false` already does the honest-confidence signal). Same fix applies to `contrast/non-text`.
- [x] **V1-RULE-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-HIDE-IGNORES-ITERATION-COUNT** `motion/pause-stop-hide` and the 2.3.1 cycles/s heuristic fire on every CSS `animation:` and `transition:` declaration regardless of `animation-iteration-count` or fill mode. On `animated-countdown/style.css:30` (`animation: hide 0.2s ease-out;` — default iteration-count 1, runs once) the rule fires AND the 2.3.1 candidate reads "~5.0 cycles/s" (mathematically wrong; one-shot animation cannot cycle). Same shape on `good-cheap-fast/style.css:54` (`forwards` fill mode). 55 of 222 corpus violations (25%) attributable. Fix: require `animation-iteration-count: infinite|N>3` OR `animation-duration > 5s` OR `transition-duration > 5s` before firing; encode iteration-count + duration as additive `reason` text.
- [x] **V1-MOTION-2.3.1-CYCLES-PER-SECOND-MATH** The flashing-content finder converts `animation-duration: 0.15s` to "~6.7 cycles/s" arithmetically without consulting `animation-iteration-count`. On `_docsearch.scss:133-137` (single 150ms entrance animation, no iteration-count) the candidate's reason text claims a 6.7Hz flash. Fix: when iteration-count is absent or `1`, drop the cycles/s number and switch reason to "single 150ms transition; flashing only if `iteration-count: infinite`." Distinct from V1-RULE-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-HIDE-IGNORES-ITERATION-COUNT (firing-gate axis); this is the reason-text accuracy axis.
- [x] **V1-RULE-LANDMARK-MAIN-AND-HEADING-HIERARCHY-DEFER-ON-EMPTY-PAGE** Pages with NO headings AND no landmark elements silently pass `semantics/landmark-main` and `semantics/heading-hierarchy`. On `theme-clock/index.html` (no `<h1>`, no `<main>`) `scan_file` returns 0 findings; same for `kinetic-loader/`, `hoverboard/`, `random-image-generator/`. The empty-body case is a *stronger* 1.3.1 signal but lands as `findingsEmitted:0, coverageConfidence:high`. Fix: when a file has `<body>` but no headings AND no landmarks, fire both rules at the body root.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-3.3.4-SCOPE-NEEDS-FINANCIAL-LEGAL-GATE** The 3.3.4 review finder fires on `<form class="form subscribe-form">` newsletter signups across templates — but WCAG 3.3.4's normative scope is "legal commitments, financial transactions, modify/delete user data, test responses." Newsletter / contact / signin forms are explicitly outside scope. Fix: gate on specific class/id tokens (`payment`, `checkout`, `legal`, `terms`, `delete-account`, `transfer`) OR `action=` URL containing `/payment`/`/checkout`/`/legal`. Reason-text enrichment alone is insufficient — a finder that fires when the criterion's spec scope provably doesn't apply duplicates capability the agent has.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-1.3.2-ORDER-CSS-SELECTOR-DEFINITIONS** The 1.3.2 `order` CSS finder fires on selector *definitions* in vendor stylesheets (`bootstrap.css:734` `.order-first { order: -1; }` — vendor utility classes). Across templates corpus that's hundreds of identical fires per criterion. Fix: track whether the rule emitted on a vendor CSS file (path matches a vendor library banner; pairs with V1-VENDOR-LIBRARY-BANNER-DETECTION) — when so, downgrade or drop. The rule should fire on *applications* (HTML element with `class="order-N"`), not on selector definitions in vendor CSS.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-1.4.1-COLOR-EMPTY-BODY-FALSE-POSITIVE** The 1.4.1 status-color finder fires on `<p class="help-block text-danger"></p>` (empty body — validation-message placeholder). Color cannot be the sole signal of *nothing*. On templates corpus 10/10 sampled candidates were empty-body. Fix: gate the finder on the element having at least some non-whitespace text content OR non-presentational child element. Pairs with V1-FINDER-1.4.1-COLOR-READ-ELEMENT-BODY (next item).
- [x] **V1-FINDER-1.4.1-COLOR-READ-ELEMENT-BODY** The 1.4.1 status-color finder reads the `class` attribute but does not recurse into element body to check for visible text. Multiple cases where visible text IS inside the colored element (`<span class="badge bg-success">New</span>` cheatsheet:681; `<p class="text-warning">No grid classes…</p>` grid:85; `<small class="...text-success...">Added in v{version}</small>` AddedIn.astro:13) — reason claims "no visible text" while the visible text is the element's text node child. Selector logic must extend to element body before composing reason text. Distinct from V1-FINDER-1.4.1-COLOR-EMPTY-BODY-FALSE-POSITIVE.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-2.3.1-RAF-NEEDS-LUMINANCE-EVIDENCE** The 2.3.1 flashing finder fires on bare `requestAnimationFrame` calls in vendor scroll/animate libraries (`wow.js`, `wow.min.js`, `headroom.min.js`, `scrolltofixed.js`). 8/10 candidates on templates corpus were these. WCAG 2.3.1 is about color/luminance cycling >3 flashes/s; bare `requestAnimationFrame` is not evidence of that. Fix: require evidence of color/luminance cycling (animated `background-color`, `opacity`, `filter`) before firing.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-2.2.1-SETTIMEOUT-VENDOR-FILE-GATE** The 2.2.1 timing finder fires on every `setTimeout`/`setInterval` regardless of whether the call site is in a vendor file. On templates 10/10 sampled candidates cite `bootstrap.js`/`jquery-1.10.2.js` library internals. The finder also fires when the duration is a member-access (`self.options.interval`) — by definition not a static literal an agent can verify. Fix: when the finding sits in a file matching a vendor banner, downgrade to `info` and tag `inVendorFile: true`; when the duration is a member-access reference, encode that as additive reason text rather than firing high-confidence.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-1.4.5-IMAGES-OF-TEXT-MARKDOWN-ADJACENT-LINK-FP** The 1.4.5 "images-of-text" finder's "img short alt repeated in immediate sibling text node" predicate matches the *next markdown line* with a different sponsor link. On `README.markdown:58-67,78` 11 candidates fire on `[![Sponsor N]]` rows where the "sibling text" is the next sponsor's distinct markdown link, not duplicated alt. Fix: in markdown contexts, the "sibling text node" must be plain prose, not another link/image; or rewrite reason text to "next adjacent markdown link has similar alt." Pairs with V1-FINDER-1.4.5-PHOTO-WITH-LABEL.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-1.4.5-PHOTO-WITH-LABEL** The 1.4.5 finder fires when `<img alt="X">` is paired with adjacent `<p>X</p>` (`insect-catch-game/index.html:21,27,36,45` — 4 candidates each `<img alt="fly"><p>Fly</p>` inside `<button class="choose-insect-btn">`). 1.4.5 is about TEXT RENDERED AS PIXELS, not a photo with an HTML label. The duplicate-alt+label is at most a 1.1.1 decorative-vs-informative question. Fix: drop these candidates OR rephrase reason as "alt could be `''` if the image is decorative; otherwise verify the alt isn't pixel-text."
- [x] **V1-FINDER-1.3.3-SENSORY-FIXTURE-PROSE** The 1.3.3 sensory-characteristics finder substring-matches on common English words ("fields below", "right side", "button below", "see below") in test fixtures and demo prose. On `alert.html:13` flags `Holy guacamole! You should check in on some of those fields below.` — demo prose in an alert test fixture; no actual form fields below. On an SSG `pagination.md:134` flags "See below" in admonition prose. Fix: enrich reason with surrounding 1-2 sentences so the dismiss is mechanical; downgrade confidence in documented prose containers (`.md`/`.mdx` body). Also fix the modal.mdx:78 off-by-5-line citation.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-2.4.5-MULTIPLE-WAYS-REQUIRE-BODY** The 2.4.5 "Multiple Ways" finder treats every HTML root as a candidate site root. On `_includes/top.html:2` (a 19-line `<head>`-only partial) it fires "Likely root layout has no search/sitemap/breadcrumb/3-link nav" — irrelevant; the composed page has 2 navs + search + meta-links. On a vanilla-JS `3d-boxes-background/index.html` it fires on a single-page CSS-trick demo. On a design-system `js/tests/integration/index.html:2` it fires on a 67-line single-page test fixture. Fix: require evidence of `<body>` AND ≥1 anchor/nav before firing; for files in `_includes/`/`_partials/`/`_components/`, downgrade reason to "likely a fragment composed into a parent layout — verify multi-way nav lives in the parent."
- [x] **V1-RULE-BUTTON-NAME-FA-ICON-ONLY-STATIC** `semantics/button-name` does not fire on static `<button>` whose only children are `<i class="fa-…">` glyphs without `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`. `faq-collapse/index.html:22-25` has 5 `<button class="faq-toggle"><i class="fas fa-chevron-down"></i><i class="fas fa-times"></i></button>` instances — `scan_file` returns 0 button-name findings. Fix: extend the selector to consider element's static children — when every child is presentational (`<i>`/`<span aria-hidden>` with no text), fire.
- [x] **V1-RULE-EXPANDED-ON-DISCLOSURE-FIRES-ABSENT-ATTRIBUTE** `aria/expanded-on-disclosure` requires the attribute to be PRESENT (often with a wrong value) before flagging — does not fire on disclosure-shaped widgets that LACK the attribute entirely. `theme-clock/index.html:12` (`<button class="toggle">Dark mode</button>`), `faq-collapse` (5 disclosure buttons), `mobile-tab-navigation` (active tab `<li>`s) all silently pass. Fix: when an element looks like a disclosure widget (toggles a `.collapse`/`.active`/`.show` class via JS click handler), fire even when `aria-expanded` is absent.
- [x] **V1-RULE-LANG-ZXX-WITH-PROSE** `parsing/html-has-lang` only checks attribute *presence*, not validity-against-content. On `js/tests/visual/floating-label.html:2` a file declares `<html lang="zxx">` (BCP 47 "no linguistic content") but the page has lots of English UI ("Email address", "Open this select menu", "Lorem ipsum"). Fix: warn when `lang` is `zxx`/`und`/`mul`/`mis` AND the document has visible text content.
- [x] **V1-RULE-INVALID-ID-NON-ASCII** `parsing/invalid-id-shape` rule scope appears limited to empty/whitespace IDs only — does not flag non-ASCII / accented characters. On `js/tests/visual/scrollspy.html:32, 86` there's `id="présentation"` and `href="#présentation"` — `e` vs `é` mismatches break URL fragment routing in older browsers and many AT shortcuts. Fix: extend to flag ids containing characters outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`.
- [x] **V1-RULE-LINK-DESCRIPTIVE-TEXT-MISSES-GENERIC-PRONOUNS** `navigation/link-descriptive-text` treats text nodes "This link" / "that link" as long enough to pass. WCAG 2.4.4's anti-pattern is the *semantic* generic-pronoun pattern, not literal length. On `js/tests/visual/modal.html:57` two `<a href="#" data-bs-toggle="tooltip">` inline links labelled "This link" and "that link" stay silent. Fix: extend the rule to flag text matching `/^(this|that|the|here|click here|read more|more|learn more|info|details)$/i`. Pairs with templates pass 2 FN-T6 / FN-T14 (button text "Go!"/"Submit"/"OK"/"Click here").
- [x] **V1-RULE-REDUNDANT-ROLE-ON-HOST-ELEMENT** No rule flags `role="form"` on `<form>`, `role="navigation"` on `<nav>`, `role="main"` on `<main>`, `role="button"` on `<button>`, `role="img"` on `<img>` — all canonical "redundant role" antipatterns. On templates corpus `<form role="form">` appears in 3+ files. Pattern is short, stable, ubiquitous. Add as `aria/redundant-role-on-host-element`. WCAG 4.1.2.
- [x] **V1-RULE-FORM-SUBMIT-AS-ANCHOR** No rule flags `<form>` containing an `<a class="btn">` as the apparent submit control with no `<button type="submit">`/`<input type="submit">`. Templates `sb-admin-2/login.html:59` literally has the HTML comment "Change this to a button or input when using this as a form" above a `<a href="index.html" class="btn btn-lg btn-success btn-block">Login</a>`. Add as `forms/submit-not-button-or-input`. WCAG 4.1.2 + 3.3.2.
- [x] **V1-RULE-REQUIRED-MARKER-WITHOUT-REQUIRED-ATTR** `<label>Username/Email <span class="text-danger">*</span></label>` paired with `<input type="text" class="form-control">` (no `required`, no `aria-required`). The color-only `*` IS flagged (`color/meaning-by-color-only`), but the input itself is not flagged. Even an SR user hearing "Username/Email star" cannot programmatically discover the field is required. Add as `forms/required-marker-without-required-attr`. WCAG 1.3.1 + 3.3.2.
- [x] **V1-FINDER-DECORATIVE-IMG-ADJACENT-MEANING-WIDEN** `decorative-img-with-adjacent-meaning` finder exists but does not catch the most common a template hero-slider pattern: `<img src="img/slides/N.jpg" alt="" />` immediately wrapped by visible caption blocks (`<strong>Online Education</strong><p>The best educational template</p>`). The image is the *content* of the slide; marking it decorative is wrong. Templates an educational template `index.html:56-78`, agile-agency `index.html:67-83,102-123,148,192-213`. Fix: lower the gate — fire when `<img alt="">` sits in a `<li>`/`<figure>`/`<div class*="slide">`/`<div class*="card">` whose siblings include `<h1..h6>` or `<p>` with non-trivial text.
- [x] **V1-RULE-ALT-TEXT-PLACEHOLDER-ROLE-AS-ALT** `media/alt-text-placeholder` (per V1-SUGGEST-FIX-ALT-DIMENSIONS-ANTIPATTERN) detects dimension-as-alt (`alt="700x400"`). Extend to detect role-as-alt antipattern: `alt="logo"|"image"|"picture"|"photo"|"icon"|"image of …"`. Templates above-educational `index.html:35` `<img src="img/logo.png" alt="logo"/>`; aerosky `contact.html:37` `<img alt="logo">`.

Review-finder noise / dedup:

- [x] **V1-CHECKLIST-PRIORITY-AXIS-DEGENERATE** `summary.byPriority` ships `{high: N, medium: 0, low: 0}` on every corpus tested — every actionable criterion lands in `high` because `priorityFor()` (verified at `src/mcp/tool-checklist.ts:144-148`) returns `high` for every A/AA criterion that has candidates. Per-candidate `confidence` IS active (varies high/medium/low). Fix: derive criterion-level priority from the *distribution* of underlying candidate confidences (using `mode` or `median`, not max), OR drop the field. The signal-bearing axis is `confidence`; the criterion-level `priority` field is redundant. **Fourth-pass repeat**. Doctrine: labeled buckets that don't differentiate are buckets not earning their keep.
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-CRITERION-CANDIDATE-DEDUPE-ACROSS-IDS** Same `(file, line, reason)` candidate fires under multiple criterion IDs with byte-identical reason text. On `ratio.mdx:14` cited 3× under `wcag22:1.2.1`/`1.2.3`/`1.2.5`; sound-board `<audio>` rows fire 6 elements × 3 criteria = 18 candidates with 3 unique reason strings; template-catalog `fancybox.pack.js:4` cited 74× × 3 criteria = 222 likelyIrrelevant rows. Fix: when a candidate's `(ruleId, snippet, reason)` tuple is identical across N criterion IDs, fold to one candidate with `criterionIds: [...]` array; `verdict_candidate` then applies one verdict to all listed criteria. Pairs with V1-REVIEW-CANDIDATES-SIBLING-REASON-DEDUP (per-criterion sibling axis); this is the cross-criterion same-candidate axis.
- [x] **V1-CHECKLIST-MAX-CANDIDATES-PER-CRITERION-CLAMP** `checklist({maxCandidatesPerCriterion: 20})` returned max 10 per criterion on templates corpus — caller-supplied value silently clamped to 10 with no narration. Either honor downstream and narrate the clamp, or document/remove. Pairs with V1-CHECKLIST-LIMIT-NEGATIVE-VALIDATION. Doctrine: silent coercion on input masks caller bugs.
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-VENDOR-FILE-FINGERPRINT-COLLAPSE** Three different copies of `jquery.flexslider.js` in three template directories all emit identical 2.5.1 candidates with identical reason text. Same library = same SHA = same finding × 3. Recent commit `feba3df8` added cross-template fingerprinting for CSS via `vendorOccurrences`; JS vendor dupes still ride the wire raw. Fix: extend the pattern to JS vendor files (`jquery.*`, `bootstrap.js`, `wow.*`, `headroom.*`, `fancybox*`, `flexslider*`) — collapse to one candidate with `occurrences: [{path, line}]`. Pairs with Q6-CONTRAST-VENDOR-CSS-CROSS-FILE-DEDUPE (closed for CSS) and V1-CSS-CROSS-TEMPLATE-FINGERPRINT — the JS-vendor sibling.

Response shape / cross-surface drift / honesty:

- [ ] **V1-PERRULE-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-PARSE-ERROR-CORPUS** At corpus scope on templates (4043 files, 501 parse-error files, 12.4% parse-error rate), `perRuleCoverage` reports `coverageConfidence: "high"` for **71/71 rules** — zero rules report `low` or `medium` despite 12% of files crashing the tsx parser. The single-file `scan_file` response *does* report `low` honestly (8/71 rules). The aggregation step turns "no .css scanned" into "0 .css files" but still flips `coverageConfidence` to `high` because the project-wide directory contained 8 .css files. Fix: a rule whose `filesEligible` includes ≥10% parse-errored or partial-parsed files should flip to `medium`; ≥25% to `low`. Distinct from V1-PERRULE-COVERAGE-HONESTY-ON-PARSE-ERRORS (per-file axis); this is the corpus-aggregation axis.
- [ ] **V1-FIX-DESCRIPTION-INLINE-VS-REF-PER-FINDING-SHAPE-DRIFT** Within a single `scan_project` response, `fix` shape varies per-finding: rule A ships `fix: {safety, description, fixDescriptionRef}`; rule B ships `fix: {safety}` + sibling `fixDescriptionRef`; rule C ships `fix: {safety, description}` (inline only). On `news_item.html` line-3 vs line-17 of the same `navigation/link-descriptive-text` rule: the description is inlined when the rule fires once, ref-only when it fires multiple times (dedup optimization). Cross-tool: the same `findingId: "49f541ade48b"` (alert.html:9 semantics/landmark-main) ships `fix.description` inline on `scan_file` and ref-only on `scan_project` — agent toolchain pivoting between surfaces hits `undefined` reads. Fix: declare and enforce ONE shape — either always inline, OR always wrap as `fix.descriptionRef?: {hash}` *inside* `fix` (never as a sibling). Source-verified at `src/output/agent-response/types.ts:163` — types document both forms but the response-builder doesn't normalize.
- [ ] **V1-CONFORMANCE-SCOPE-FILES-ARRAY-UNCAPPED** `conformance_statement.scope.files: readonly string[]` ships every scanned file path inline (verified at `src/reports/conformance.ts:175 + :834`). On templates corpus that's 4043 paths × ~90 chars = 360 KB; total response 421,628 chars (16.8× over MCP token ceiling). Most agent-impactful failure since this is the *publishable* artifact. Fix: ship `scope.fileCount: 4043, scope.root: "/tmp/..."` plus an opt-in `verboseMeta: true` knob that re-includes the file list. Conformance-statement is unusable on any project >400 files today. Distinct from V1-CONFORMANCE-SCOPE-FILES-MINIFIED-LEAK; this is the unbounded-array axis.
- [ ] **V1-COVERAGE-PARSE-ERROR-FILES-UNCAPPED** `coverage` ships `analysisCoverage.parseErrorFiles` with all entries inline (501 entries × ~250 chars = ~125 KB) — single largest source of bulk in `coverage` responses. Fix: emit `parseErrorFileCount: 501` plus `topReasons: [{reason, count}]` rollup; behind `verboseMeta: true` re-include the full path list. Same shape applies to `partialParseFiles` (201 entries on templates). Pairs with V1-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-ENVELOPE-DRIFT (sibling array-uncapped axis on `scan_project`). Doctrine: any internal array > N entries must ship as `{topN, totalCount, truncated: true}`.
- [ ] **V1-COVERAGE-META-BLOCK-MISSING** `coverage` returns no `meta` block at all — agents cannot verify it scanned the same file set as a prior `scan_project` call (no `filesScanned` echo, no `configSource`, no `rootSource`, no `warnings`, no `rulesEvaluated`). For a tool whose entire job is "did the scan really cover this?" this is structurally inverted. Fix: every tool that runs the scanner should ship the same `meta` shape. Pairs with V1-WARNINGS-DETAILS-CROSS-SURFACE-REGRESSION.
- [ ] **V1-CROSS-SURFACE-WARNINGS-CODE-SET-DRIFT** Same scan state surfaces different warning codes per tool: `scan_project.warnings = [no_config_found, scanned_build_artifacts_present, extensions_skipped_no_parser, parse_errors_present, response_token_budget_truncated]` (5 codes); `coverage.warnings = [extensions_skipped_no_parser, parse_errors_present]` (2 codes); `scan_file.warnings = [no_config_found]` (1 code). `coverage` is missing `no_config_found` and `scanned_build_artifacts_present`; `scan_file` is missing `parse_errors_present` even when its own `analysisCoverage.parseErrorFileCount > 0`. Each tool emits a different subset; agents that wire warnings into routing logic miss the prompt unless they call `scan_project` first. Fix: shared meta-block populated by every tool that invokes the scanner.
- [ ] **V1-FINDINGS-SNIPPET-FIELD-OMITTED-ON-SCAN-SURFACES** `scan_project` and `scan_file` `findings[*]` lack `snippet` field; `checklist.items[*].candidates[*]` includes it. The asymmetry forces extra Read calls per finding and inflates `fix.description` prose. Source check: `src/output/agent-response/types.ts:127` declares `snippet?: string` (present-when-meaningful); the rule layer doesn't populate it for scan-side findings. Adding 1-3 lines per finding would (a) collapse the dishonest "is this fix mechanical?" question for cases like motion/pause-stop-hide, and (b) reduce per-finding `fix.description` prose. Pairs with V1-PATTERN-ID-DEAD-INFRASTRUCTURE which gates `patternId` on `emitted.snippet` being non-empty — populating snippets unblocks both.
- [ ] **V1-SUGGEST-FIX-SCHEMA-PRIMARY-ALTERNATIVES** The `suggest_fix` tool description promises "guidance with a ranked primary fix and alternatives" but `kind: "guidance"` ships a single `explanation` string with alternatives concatenated as prose ("`<label for>` ... or `aria-label` ... or `aria-labelledby`"). Schema-vs-reality drift. Fix: structure `kind: "guidance"` as `{primary: {description}, alternatives: [{description, rationale?}]}` so the agent can rank fixes without prose parsing. Also add missing fields: `nextStep`, `findingId` echo.
- [ ] **V1-SUGGEST-FIX-KIND-SUPPRESS-RECOMMENDED** `suggest_fix` on `semantics/heading-hierarchy` for a `_includes/` partial returns `kind: "guidance"` with explanation that primarily describes a *suppression* (pragma) rather than a fix. The guidance/edit/none discriminator is doing double duty as "structural recommendation" and "I give up — please add a pragma." Splitting `kind: "suppress-recommended"` from `kind: "guidance"` would make the agent's branching honest.
- [ ] **V1-SESSION-CONFIGURE-ECHO-STATE** `sessionConfigure({rules: {"motion/pause-stop-hide": "warning"}})` returns `{active: {ruleCount: 74, allowWrite: true}}` — does not echo what was set. No `rules: {[ruleId]: severity}` map, no `nativeWrappers` array, no `cwd` echo. Agent typing a wrong rule ID gets silent no-op. `sessionConfigure.allowWrite: true` is the active default contradicting the schema doc ("Defaults to false") — needs investigation. Fix: echo the merged session config back; surface `allowWrite: true` as a warning when it flips on.
- [ ] **V1-VPAT-NO-PASSING-CRITERIA-WARNING** `vpat` on a corpus with `summary.supports: 0` (every criterion fails or is "Partially Supports" or "Not Evaluated") ships as a publishable artifact with no warning. An empty `productName` triggers `product_metadata_placeholders_in_use`; an all-fail VPAT silently ships. Fix: add structured warning code `vpat_no_passing_criteria` when `summary.supports === 0`.
- [ ] **V1-VPAT-PARTIALLY-SUPPORTS-VS-NOT-EVALUATED** `vpat` reports `Partially Supports` for SCs with **0 findings** AND no manual-review evidence (1.3.4 Orientation, 1.4.4 Resize Text, 1.4.6, 2.1.4, 2.3.3, 2.5.2, 4.1.3) — that's prose for "we don't know" but reads to a procurement officer as "we tested and some parts work." Fix: when `(automatedFindings === 0 && manualEvidence === 0 && attestations === 0)`, emit `Not Evaluated`, not `Partially Supports`. Pairs with V1-CONFORMANCE-LIMITATIONS-FIELD-MISSING.)
- [ ] **V1-LIST-RULES-SATISFIES-INCONSISTENT-ACROSS-FAMILY** Adjacent same-family rules declare different `satisfies` arrays: `semantics/table-caption-missing` lists all 4 standards (`wcag22:1.3.1`, `wcag21:1.3.1`, `section508:1.3.1`, `en301549:9.1.3.1`); `semantics/table-headers` lists only 2 (wcag22 + wcag21); `semantics/table-th-scope-missing` lists all 4. Same family, three different shapes. Either equivalence resolution is broken in the registry, or `satisfies` is hand-curated and inconsistent. Fix: `list_rules` should display the *resolved* equivalentTo set. Audit every rule's `satisfies` against the equivalentTo index.
- [ ] **V1-EXPLAIN-RULE-KNOWN-FALSE-POSITIVES-FIELD** `explain_rule` does not surface known FP/FN patterns. `motion/pause-stop-hide` describes itself as discerning between `infinite` and one-shot animations — but the implementation fires on every CSS `animation:` regardless. `aria/icon-font-hidden` describes itself as covering both labeled-button and unlabeled-button cases; the implementation defers when ancestor has no name. `keyboard/handler-missing` describes scope as "the data-bs-* idiom data-bs-toggle…" but fires on container `data-bs-toggle="buttons"`. Add `knownFalsePositives: string[]` / `knownLimitations: string[]` arrays so agents can triage faster. Doctrine: `explain_rule` lying about behavior mis-calibrates the agent's dismissal logic.
- [ ] **V1-DETECTED-FRAMEWORK-CONFIDENCE-AND-INHERITANCE** `meta.detectedFramework: {name: "jekyll", buildOutput: "_site/", buildCommand: "bundle exec jekyll build"}` fires on a single sentinel `_config.yml` at the corpus root with no `Gemfile`/`_layouts/`/`_includes/` corroboration. On templates (174 independent un-built static-template snapshots) the advice is wrong — `_site/` would be near-empty. Worse: scanning a sub-template directory inherits `detectedFramework: jekyll` even when the sub-tree has no `_config.yml` (the detector walked up to an ancestor). Fix: add `detectedFramework.confidence: "high" | "medium" | "low"` derived from corroborating evidence count; on sub-scope scans, only inherit the parent's framework label when the sub-tree has its own corroborating evidence.
- [ ] **V1-OPAQUE-CUSTOM-COMPONENT-NAMES-FILTER-MEMBER-ACCESS** `opaqueCustomComponentNames` ships `["AG.y", "B", "H.length", "J", "J.length", "Math.abs"]` on templates — minified-JS symbol fragments captured by tsx parser, not React components. `detect_native_wrappers.emptyReason: "no-jsx-onclick-candidates-found-but-opaque-components-present"` cites the same hallucinated names — `propose_config` does too. **Three downstream tools amplify the same upstream bug.** Fix: before emitting, filter the candidate list by (a) contains `.` (member-access path) → drop; (b) matches a well-known global prototype (`Math`, `Object`, `Array`, `JSON`, `Reflect`, `Symbol`, `Proxy`, `Promise`) → drop; (c) single-uppercase-letter with numeric suffix (`A1`, `B2`) → drop; (d) basename matches a vendor library banner → drop. Pairs with V1-OPAQUE-COMPONENT-NAMES-MINIFIED-TOKEN-LEAK — this names the deterministic filters.
- [ ] **V1-PROPOSE-CONFIG-EXCLUDES-DOWNSTREAM-AMPLIFICATION** `propose_config` writes blanket `exclude: ["docs/**"]` on (the only directory with content), `exclude: ["js/**", "site/**"]` on (the source trees), and `exclude` lists 167 template directories at scale (95% of repo). Pasting any of these silences ~99% of real signal. Root cause: the heuristic gates on directory-name patterns or on `scannedBuildArtifacts` mis-classifications and propagates them into config. Fix: `propose_config` should never exclude a directory that contains files with active findings — gate on "does this dir have grounded findings? if yes, do not exclude." Pairs with V1-BUILD-ARTIFACT-CLASSIFIER-BYPASS-HTML-FP and V1-BOOTSTRAP-SUGGESTED-CONFIG-EXCLUDE-LOUD — both upstream amplifiers. Doctrine: surface-don't-suppress, applied at the generator step.
- [ ] **V1-PROPOSE-CONFIG-EXCLUDES-RATIONALE** `propose_config` ships an exclude list with no per-entry rationale. Agent cannot tell which entries came from `scannedBuildArtifacts` mis-classification vs. `node_modules`-style definitional vs. ad-hoc heuristic. Add `excludesReason: [{glob, reason: "labelled minified by scan_project" | "definitional" | "heuristic"}]` per entry so the agent can audit before pasting. Pairs with V1-PROPOSE-CONFIG-EXCLUDES-DOWNSTREAM-AMPLIFICATION (the gate).
- [ ] **V1-DEFAULT-EXCLUDED-PATTERNS-TEST-STORY-DROP** `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_PATTERNS` (verified at `src/input/discover.ts:60-70`) hard-codes `**/*.test.*`, `**/*.spec.*`, `**/*.stories.*`, `**/*.story.*`, `**/__tests__/**`, `**/stories/**`, `**/dev-tools/**`, `**/devtools/**` — the header comment literally says "scanning them produces noise (onChange on filter bars, render assertions, sample copy that happens to contain 'click below')." That's verbatim "human-attention suppression," which the AI-first doctrine rules out. A test file that uses `<button>` without an accessible name CAN be a real a11y bug — fixture code gets copy-pasted into production routinely. Drop: `**/*.test.*`, `**/*.spec.*`, `**/__tests__/**`, `**/*.stories.*`, `**/*.story.*`, `**/stories/**`, `**/dev-tools/**`, `**/devtools/**`. Keep: `**/__mocks__/**`. The `includeTests` opt-out escape hatch is the right shape; the default is wrong per doctrine.
- [ ] **V1-VERDICT-CANDIDATE-CANDIDATE-ID-PASSTHROUGH** `verdict_candidate` requires the caller to hand-build `{candidate: {criterionId, location, reason, snippet, confidence}, reviewPrompt}` from prior `checklist`/`review_candidates` responses (6 keys re-stitched). No `candidateId` link, no batch entry. At scale (5003 candidates on templates) one round-trip per candidate is unfeasible. Fix: add `candidateId` shortcut (`verdict_candidate({candidateId, reviewPrompt})`) that back-loads the snippet from the original call. Pairs with V1-CHECKLIST-ITEM-REVIEW-PROMPT-FIELD.
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-ITEM-REVIEW-PROMPT-FIELD** `checklist.items[*]` does not carry `reviewPrompt` — but `verdict_candidate` requires it. The natural workflow is `checklist → verdict_candidate`, but the prompt-text lives only on `review_candidates.prompts[criterionId].text`. An agent has to call `review_candidates` separately or hand-author the prompt. Fix: include `reviewPrompt` per criterion in `checklist.items[]` so the workflow is one tool call shorter.
- [ ] **V1-FINDING-CATEGORY-VS-FIXCLASS-CONTRADICTION** Same finding ships `fixClass: "runtime-only"` AND `category: "auto-fix"` AND `effort: "trivial"`. On `theme-clock/style.css:13` motion/pause-stop-hide finding — `runtime-only` per `fixesByClass` means "the scanner can't ground a deterministic fix here" but `category: auto-fix` reads as "apply_fix can do this." Same finding has `fix.safety: "safe"` and a `fix.description` that DOES specify the exact CSS rewrite. Fix: one of the two labels must be retracted. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest.
- [ ] **V1-PARTIAL-PARSE-FILES-WARNING-CODE** `analysisCoverage.partialParseFileCount` (13 on baseline, 201 on templates) does not trigger a warning code. `parse_errors_present` fires on total-failure files; partial parses get no warning. Fix: add `partial_parse_files_present` warning code (or fold into `parse_errors_present` with a `partialCount` payload in `warningsDetails`).
- [ ] **V1-MISSING-WARNING-CWD-APPEARS-MISROOTED** When `scan_project` returns `filesScanned === 0` AND `warnings: ["scanned_zero_files"]`, no companion warning suggests "did you mean a parent dir?" or echoes a `nearestNonEmptyParent` path. Add `cwd_appears_misrooted` warning emitted when `filesScanned === 0` AND a sibling/parent dir would have produced findings (cheap walk-up check). Also detect when `subdir_scan && parent_has_config && walked_up: false`.
- [ ] **V1-MISSING-WARNING-DIST-ONLY-SCAN** When `scannedBuildArtifacts.length === filesScanned` AND every file is dist-tagged (the entire scan is build-artifact-only), no headline signal escalates. A design-system dist-dir scan returned `1244 violations` against pure build artifacts with no warning. The existing `scanned_build_artifacts_present` is a boolean. Fix: add `dist_only_scan_detected` warning when `scannedBuildArtifacts.length === filesScanned && filesScanned > 0`. Doctrine analogue of `scanned_zero_files`.
- [ ] **V1-MISSING-WARNING-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-INCONSISTENT** When `parseErrorFileCount > 0` AND every rule reports `coverageConfidence: "high"`, the meta is internally inconsistent. Add structured warning `coverage_confidence_uniformly_high_with_parse_errors`. Tied to V1-PERRULE-COVERAGE-CONFIDENCE-PARSE-ERROR-CORPUS.

Tool-orchestration / new tool surfaces:

- [ ] **V1-TOOL-VERBOSE-META-INVERTED-DEFAULT** Bulk-shipping tools (`coverage`, `conformance_statement`, `scan_project`, `scan_file` on dense HTML) all blow the MCP token ceiling at scale. Root cause shared: per-call telemetry blocks (`scope.files`, `parseErrorFiles`, `scannedBuildArtifacts`, `referenceGuide`, full `perRuleCoverage`) ship inline by default. `scan_diff` already has a `verboseMeta: true` knob. Fix: invert the default — `verboseMeta: false` strips per-rule coverage, file lists, reference-guide tables; adopt across all bulk-shippers. The lean default fits comfortably under 30 KB; the verbose mode is opt-in for triage. Pairs with V1-CONFORMANCE-SCOPE-FILES-ARRAY-UNCAPPED + V1-COVERAGE-PARSE-ERROR-FILES-UNCAPPED + V1-SCANNED-BUILD-ARTIFACTS-ENVELOPE-DRIFT — those items fix specific arrays; this is the structural default-inversion.
- [ ] **V1-TOOL-SCAN-PROJECT-SUMMARY-MODE** Add `scan_project({summaryOnly: true})` that returns `plan + meta + topRules: [{ruleId, count}] + topFiles: [{path, count}] + filesByExtension + plan.summary` and *omits* per-file `files` array entirely. Fits under 20 KB. First-call ergonomics on bulk catalogs (templates 4043 files / 40k findings). Pairs with V1-RESPONSE-SIZE-PRE-ESTIMATOR.
- [ ] **V1-TOOL-FINDING-BY-ID** No tool retrieves a finding by `findingId` after the scan. `suggest_fix` requires `ruleId + file + line` — but `scan_project` returns `findingId: "b0d1b34c01d1"` opaque IDs. Add `get_finding({findingId})`.
- [ ] **V1-TOOL-FINDINGS-BY-RULE** `scan_project` paginates by file; an agent triaging "all 169 `aria/expanded-on-disclosure` findings" must page 110 files. Add `findings_by_rule({ruleId, cwd})` that returns just the findings for one rule across the project. Replaces 22 paginated calls with one. Pairs with V1-CROSS-FILE-ROLLUP-PRIMITIVE.
- [ ] **V1-TOOL-SESSION-INSPECT** No read-only tool to retrieve current session state. After `sessionConfigure`, agents have no way to verify rule overrides, native wrappers, or excludes without making a no-op `scan` call. Add `sessionInspect()` returning the same shape `sessionConfigure.active` returns plus `rules`, `nativeWrappers`, `cwd`. Pairs with V1-SESSION-CONFIGURE-ECHO-STATE.
- [ ] **V1-TOOL-LIST-FINDERS** Manual-review finders (`review/multiple-ways`, `review/pointer-input`, etc. — visible in `review_candidates.prompts[*].finderId`) are NOT enumerated by any tool. Agents discover them only by accident through `review_candidates` results. Per the rules-silently-defer / finders-silently-fire pattern, having a `list_finders` tool would expose the manual-review surface area for audit.
- [ ] **V1-TOOL-AUDIT-RULE-COVERAGE** No tool surfaces "rule X did not fire on file Y, but heuristic predicates suggest it should have." The only way to discover an FN is to manually inspect `perRuleCoverage` and reason about it. Add `audit_rule_coverage({ruleId, file})` returning `{fired: false, eligibleByExtension: true, predicateMissed: true, hint: "trigger requires static-text child; file contains Liquid expression"}`.
- [ ] **V1-MCP-DIST-STALE-CI-GATE** The deployed MCP `dist/cli.js` (verified Apr 21 19:03) is 2 days stale relative to current `main` HEAD (Apr 23 16:32). Multiple field-test "bugs" reproduce in the deployed binary but have already-merged fixes in source — concretely: pointer-input `panels`/`panel` substring FP (commit `10ab7391`, `src/review/finders/pointer-input.ts:97-122 + :312-313`), `configSearchedFrom` echo of cwd (Q6-CONFIG-CONTEXT-TRIPLE-READOUT closure at `src/mcp/tool-scan-project.ts:337-344`), `configNote` boilerplate (same closure), `plan.safeEditsAvailable` composite-headline drop (commit `fce189c8`). The 4-pass field-test repeatedly catches the same regressions because no test asserts `dist/cli.js` content hash matches HEAD before the MCP server starts. Fix: CI check that compares `dist/cli.js` content hash to a git-tracked manifest, OR a startup-time `console.warn` if `dist/` is older than the most recent `src/` commit, OR a `bun run mcp:check` that diffs `dist` against `bun build src/cli.ts` output. Pairs with V1-PLAN-SAFE-EDITS-STALE-MCP-SERVER (the symptom) — this is the systemic root cause.

Catalog-scale perf / scoping:

- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-VENDOR-IFRAME-OCCURRENCES-COLLAPSE** `checklist.likelyIrrelevant` ships 74 candidates per criterion × 4 criteria = 296 rows for templates corpus, all pointing at identical `<iframe id="fancybox-frame{rnd}"...` template literals in 74 copies of `fancybox.pack.js:4`. The bucket label is doctrine-correct (deterministic "no `<video>`/`<audio>` parsed") but the *quantity* is not — every fancybox install ships an identical line. Fix: collapse to one entry with `occurrences: 74`. Pairs with V1-CSS-CROSS-TEMPLATE-FINGERPRINT and V1-CHECKLIST-VENDOR-FILE-FINGERPRINT-COLLAPSE.
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-IFRAME-EMBEDDED-MEDIA-NOT-IRRELEVANT** `checklist.likelyIrrelevant: 4` on groups SCs 1.2.1/1.2.3/1.2.5 under "no `<video>` or `<audio>` detected" — bucket label is provable from code (deterministic fact), but the candidates inside include `docs/_tutorials/video-walkthroughs.md:10` (`<iframe src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/T1itpPvFWHI">`) and `docs/pages/jekyllconf.md:16` (parameterized YouTube embed via Liquid). YouTube iframes ARE WCAG 1.2.x in scope. The bucket sweeps real candidates into "likely irrelevant" because the heuristic confuses "no native `<video>` tag" with "no media." Fix: either (a) detect `<iframe src*=youtube|vimeo|twitch|brightcove|wistia>` and demote those candidates from `likelyIrrelevant` back to `actionable`, or (b) keep them in main `items[]` with reason "iframe embed of likely media (`youtube.com/embed/...`) — no native `<video>` tag but media accessibility still applies." Doctrine: a bucket is honest only when label-AND-implication are both 100% from evidence; here the implication is heuristic.
- [ ] **V1-PLAN-FINDINGS-BY-FILE-AND-RULE-HISTOGRAM** `scan_project` exposes `totalFilesWithFindings` but no per-file or per-rule distribution at the response root. On an SSG docs tree (71 files-with-findings) the agent can't budget pagination cost without iterating all 71. On templates (1793 files) paging 1793× costs hours of round-trips. Fix: add `plan.findingsByFile: [{path, count}]` (top-N rank-ordered) AND `plan.findingsByRule: {ruleId: count}`. Pairs with V1-CROSS-FILE-ROLLUP-PRIMITIVE.
- [ ] **V1-AGENT-RESPONSE-REFERENCE-GUIDE-OPT-IN** `scan_file` on dense HTML (>100 LOC) blows the MCP token ceiling because `referenceGuide.suppressPlacement` + `referenceGuide.fixDescriptions` ship on every per-file response (~30-40 KB each call). Fix: ship `referenceGuide` only on the first call per session, OR behind `includeReferenceGuide: true` opt-in. Pairs with V1-TOOL-VERBOSE-META-INVERTED-DEFAULT.
- [ ] **V1-FILES-BY-EXTENSION-GROUND-TRUTH-UNDERCOUNT** `meta.filesByExtension` undercounts actual file counts on the a design-system docs corpus: reports `.css:31, .js:56, .scss:114, .md:13` while ground-truth `find -type f -name '*.css'` returns 47, `.js` 116, `.scss` 122, `.md` 17 — deltas of -16 / -60 / -8 / -4 with no obvious default-exclusion explaining them. Deltas are not consistent with `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_PATTERNS` paths; silent drops somewhere between discovery and the bucket-by-ext counter. Fix: add an invariant test that `sum(filesByExtension.values()) + sum(skippedByExtension.values()) + excludedByPattern` equals the raw `find` count on a known fixture corpus; instrument the discovery pipeline to emit `meta.filesByExtension.excludedByPatternPerExt: {ext: count}` so the drop axis is visible. Doctrine: verbose meta is scan-confidence signal — a count that silently misses 60 JS files trains the agent to trust a dishonest denominator.
- [x] **V1-ALT-TEXT-PLACEHOLDER-SEQUENTIAL-SLIDE** `media/alt-text-placeholder`'s token list covers `image|screenshot|placeholder|TODO|photo|picture` but misses the canonical carousel-slide shape: `alt="First slide"`, `alt="Second slide"`, `alt="Third slide"` (design-system carousel docs, template kits, countless starter themes). Sequential positional labels are textbook placeholder — they carry the author's intent ("I'll describe this later") but ship as production alt text. Fix: extend token list to `/^(first|second|third|fourth|fifth|next|previous|prev|last)\s+slide$/i`, `/^slide\s+\d+$/i`, `/^(image|photo|picture)\s+\d+$/i`. Pairs with V1-RULE-ALT-TEXT-PLACEHOLDER-ROLE-AS-ALT (same rule, different antipattern axis).
- [x] **V1-TOOLTIP-DISMISSABLE-VISIBLE-TEXT-EQ-TITLE** `tooltip/dismissable` fix description offers three alternatives ("replace with visible text, aria-label, or custom tooltip library") but when visible text already equals `title`, the first two are redundant — the button at `js/tests/integration/index.html:18` (`<button title="Tooltip on top">Tooltip on top</button>`) already has the accessible name the alternatives recommend. Fix: when the flagged element's trimmed `textContent` equals the `title` attribute value (case-insensitive), emit only the tooltip-library upgrade hint and drop the redundant visible-text / aria-label alternatives. Reason-enrichment only — rule still fires (native title has no keyboard dismiss path). Doctrine: the tool must not suggest alternatives that re-state the existing state of the DOM.
- [x] **V1-MOTION-PAUSE-STOP-CAROUSEL-HAS-VISIBLE-CONTROLS-REASON** `motion/pause-stop-hide` fires on `data-bs-ride="carousel"` regardless of whether the carousel has user-operable `.carousel-control-prev` / `.carousel-control-next` controls in the same subtree. WCAG 2.2.2 is satisfied when a pause / stop / hide mechanism exists — visible prev/next buttons with keyboard focusability count as "stop" (user can halt auto-advance by interacting with controls). On `js/tests/visual/carousel.html:20` the rule fires despite controls at :37-44. Surface-don't-suppress per doctrine: keep the finding (controls being present does not prove they are keyboard-operable; AT pause flow is still weaker than an explicit pause button), but enrich reason with "note: descendant controls `.carousel-control-prev|next` detected at :37,:42 — verify keyboard focus + announcement carry pause semantics before dismissing." Reason-text only; no detection change.
- [x] **V1-ARIA-CONFLICTING-ROLE-DATA-BS-SLIDE-ENRICH** `aria/conflicting-role` on `<a role="button" data-bs-slide="prev">` (carousel anchor-as-button idiom) emits the generic "element has role that conflicts with its tag" reason without naming the data-bs-* context that pins down the fix. The canonical remediation is `<button type="button" data-bs-slide="prev">`, not "remove role." Fix: when `data-bs-slide` / `data-bs-dismiss` / `data-bs-toggle` / `data-bs-target` is present on the flagged element, enrich reason with "Bootstrap action trigger — use `<button type=\"button\">` (data-bs-* attributes work on buttons identically)." Reason-text enrichment; no detection change. Pairs with V1-FP-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-DATA-BS-CONTAINER (same attribute family, different rule).
- [x] **V1-TEMPLATE-CLASSIFIER-LIQUID-PIPE-FILTER-EVIDENCE** Classifier tags SSG includes / layouts as `handlebars-or-mustache` because the `{{ ... }}` tokens are ambiguous — but Liquid filter pipes (`{{ x | default: "en" }}`, `{{ y | escape }}`) are Liquid-exclusive syntax. Current per-file majority-vote doesn't credit the `|` pipe inside `{{ }}` as Liquid-only evidence; see `docs/_includes/top.html` tagged as handlebars. Fix: `{{ … | … }}` with a pipe separator inside the expression is high-confidence `jinja-or-liquid` — outweighs any `{{ plain }}` co-occurrence in the same file. Pairs with Q4-TEMPLATE-CLASSIFIER-LIQUID-AS-MUSTACHE-SINGLE-FILE (closed — covered the `{%- -%}` bracket axis; this item covers the filter-pipe axis). Doctrine: verbose meta integrity — wrong family tag mis-routes any downstream strip helper.
- [x] **V1-RULE-TABLE-TH-SCOPE-IMPLICIT-COL-SKIP** `semantics/table-th-scope-missing` fires on `<th>` that sits as a direct child of `<thead><tr>` — but HTML5 specifies implicit `scope="col"` for this shape, and AT (JAWS, NVDA, VoiceOver) all honor the implicit association. On `docs/_docs/configuration/options.md:16-17` a textbook `<thead><tr><th>Setting</th><th>Description</th></tr></thead>` is flagged as if scope were required. Fix: skip the rule when `<th>` is a direct child of `<thead>/<tr>` AND the table has a `<tbody>` sibling (the full standard pattern that browsers resolve implicitly); keep firing for `<th>` scattered in `<tbody>` rows (where scope IS required) and for tables without `<thead>`. Doctrine: the rule's spec is wrong — implicit scope is a determinate W3C/HTML5 rule, not a heuristic, so skipping here is spec-correctness not suppression.
- [ ] **V1-SCSS-RULES-COVERAGE-HOLE-JEKYLL** On an SSG source tree (18 `.scss` files, zero `.css`), `meta.analysisCoverage.rulesByExtension[".scss"]` lists only `focus/outline-visible` + `wrapper/drift` — the CSS-family rules (`contrast/minimum`, `contrast/non-text`, `contrast/enhanced`, `layout/*`, `focus/not-obscured`) never run despite SCSS containing plain CSS declarations the alias pipeline should route. Q4-SCSS-DISCOVERY-WIRE (closed) verified SCSS parses cleanly and fires `contrast/minimum` on the initial audit; the current SSG tree does NOT show those rules running. Either (a) the extensionMatches alias regressed, (b) the rule selectors themselves decline on SCSS substrate, or (c) the MCP subprocess is serving a stale bundle (V1-MCP-DIST-STALE-CI-GATE umbrella). Fix: add a cross-surface invariant test asserting every `.scss`-scanned repo fires at least one `contrast/*` rule when the source contains a low-contrast declaration; if the alias path is intact but selectors decline on `.scss`, widen the selector gate so CSS-family rules run on `.scss` after SCSS-only syntax is stripped. Pairs with Q6-SCSS-PARSING-DISCLOSURE (closed, telemetry axis) — this item is the detection axis.
- [ ] **V1-SCAN-FILE-META-CROSS-FILE-STATE-LEAK** `scan_file` on `docs/_includes/top.html` returns `meta.analysisCoverage.partialParseFiles` containing `error.html` (a different file in the same project). Per-file scans should report per-file diagnostics only; leaking project-scan state into a single-file response forces the agent to re-read and disambiguate whether `error.html` was actually involved in evaluating `top.html`. Fix: `scan_file` must filter `parseErrorFiles` / `partialParseFiles` / `scannedBuildArtifacts` / `opaqueCustomComponentNames` to only entries whose path equals the scanned file. Doctrine: cross-surface shape drift — `scan_file` and `scan_project` must not share the same unfiltered meta blob.
- [ ] **V1-CHECKLIST-AUTOMATED-PASS-RATE-SEMANTICS-UNCLEAR** `checklist.summary.automatedCoverage.automatedCriteriaPassRate: 58` on that ALSO carries 323 active violations and 71 files-with-findings reads to an agent as "most criteria pass" — but the denominator / numerator are undocumented in the response. Q-SHARED-PASS-RATE-COMPOSITE (closed) specified the split (`criteriaEvaluated / criteriaClean / criteriaWithFindings / criteriaUntestable`); the shipped field is still a bare scalar with no companion split on the checklist surface. Fix: either (a) ship the structured split alongside (or instead of) the scalar, (b) rename the field to `automatedCriteriaCleanRate` so the label names what it measures, or (c) drop the scalar entirely in favor of the per-bucket sibling counters. Distinct from V1-ZERO-SCAN-PASS-RATE-SENTINEL (zero-file edge case); this is the healthy-scan semantic-ambiguity axis. Doctrine: ambiguous field shapes are dishonest.
- [ ] **V1-FINDER-ACCESSIBLE-NAME-SR-ONLY-IMG-ALT-REDUNDANT** Canonical logo-link shape `<a href="/"><span class="sr-only">Brand</span><img src="logo.png" alt="Brand Logo"></a>` concatenates to accessible name "Brand Brand Logo" — duplicate token + redundant "Logo" suffix (AT announces the role automatically). On `docs/_includes/header.html:4-9` this shape ships unflagged; no rule or finder surfaces the sr-only + alt redundancy pattern. Fix: new review finder `review/accessible-name-redundant-composition` — compute accessible name for anchors / buttons whose children mix visually-hidden text (`.sr-only`, `.visually-hidden`, `[aria-hidden]`) with `<img alt="…">`; when the concatenated name contains case-insensitive duplicate tokens OR role-suffix redundancy (`logo|image|icon|button|link`), emit a 2.4.6 / 4.1.2 review candidate. Satisfies wcag22:2.4.6 + 4.1.2.
- [x] **V1-RULE-KEYBOARD-HANDLER-DRAGGABLE-NON-NATIVE** (already closed by 1510e5a6 — backlog stale) `keyboard/handler-missing` does not flag `<div draggable="true">` without `tabindex` + `onKeyDown`. On `drag-n-drop/index.html:11` the draggable div is only wired to mouse drag events in an external `.js`; keyboard users cannot initiate drag. `pointer/drag-alternative` fires (2.5.7 axis) but the 2.1.1 axis stays silent. Q5-DRAG-ALTERNATIVE-KEYBOARD-COMPOSE (closed) dealt with predicate overlap for already-detected draggable cases; on this shape the rule's own selector never matches because `<div draggable>` isn't in its native-interactive allowlist. Fix: extend `keyboard/handler-missing` selector to `[draggable="true"]` on non-native-interactive tags (`div`, `span`, `li`, `article`) — fire when neither `tabindex` nor `on[Kk]ey*` handler exists. Satisfies wcag22:2.1.1.
- [x] **V1-RULE-LABEL-FOR-ADJACENT-MISMATCH** Copy-paste bug where `<label for="email">Password:</label>` sits immediately above `<input id="password">` — label's `for` still matches a real id elsewhere in the document, so `forms/label-for-id-mismatch` declines; `forms/label-adjacent-unassociated` declines too (label DOES have a `for`). Net result: the password input escapes all label-family rules while AT double-announces the email label and leaves password unlabeled. `password-strength-background/index.html:31` is the canonical case. Fix: new sibling predicate in the label-family — fire when `<label for=X>` is immediately followed by an input whose `id !== X`, AND id `X` resolves to a different element in the same document. Distinct from V1-RULE-FORM-LABEL-ADJACENT-INPUT-WITHOUT-ID (no-id axis); this is the mismatched-ids-across-adjacency axis. Satisfies wcag22:1.3.1 + 3.3.2 + 4.1.2.
- [ ] **V1-FINDER-ALT-DUPLICATES-SIBLING-TEXT-INSIDE-BUTTON** Pattern `<button><p>Fly</p><img alt="fly"></button>` (`insect-catch-game/index.html:19-49`, 4 instances) — alt text duplicates a visible sibling text node inside the same interactive ancestor. 1.1.1 guidance says decorative/redundant images should use `alt=""`; the current duplication inflates accessible name ("Fly fly") and burdens AT. `media/alt-text-placeholder` misses this because the alt value is authored-looking ("fly" is not in the placeholder token list), yet in context it IS placeholder. Fix: extend `media/alt-text-placeholder` OR new review finder — when `<img alt="X">` is a descendant of `<button>` or `<a>` AND a sibling text node (direct or via `<p>`/`<span>`/`<strong>`) contains case-insensitive `X` (or X is a substring of the sibling), emit a 1.1.1 candidate recommending `alt=""`. Satisfies wcag22:1.1.1.
- [ ] **V1-RULE-TOGGLE-BUTTON-ARIA-PRESSED** `<button class="toggle">Dark mode</button>` on `theme-clock/index.html:12` flips `html.dark` — persistent on/off state, distinct from disclosure (which toggles visibility of a related region). Needs `aria-pressed="true|false"`, not `aria-expanded`. V1-RULE-EXPANDED-ON-DISCLOSURE-FIRES-ABSENT-ATTRIBUTE covers the disclosure-shape-without-attribute axis; this item is the toggle-button-without-pressed axis — different ARIA pattern. Fix: new review finder `aria/toggle-button-pressed-missing` — fire on `<button>` where class contains `/toggle|switch|mode/` OR visible text matches `/^(dark|light|night|day|on|off)\s*(mode)?$/i` AND element has no `aria-pressed`/`aria-checked`. Confidence `medium` (cross-file handler resolution lossy). Satisfies wcag22:4.1.2.
- [ ] **V1-FINDER-STATUS-MESSAGES-PLUS-MINUS-READOUT** 4.1.3 Status Messages finder does not fire on the plus/minus counter pattern: `<button>+</button><span id="size">10</span><button>-</button>` where the readout mutates on button click but has no `role="status"` / `aria-live`. `drawing-app/index.html:13` has the canonical shape. V1-LIVE-REGION-RUNTIME-MUTATION-CANDIDATE (open) covers the cross-file `elem.innerHTML = …` JS axis; this item is the static-DOM signal — provable from the HTML shape alone, higher-confidence than cross-file handler resolution. Fix: new review-candidate finder `review/status-message-plus-minus-readout` — fire on `<span>`/`<div>`/`<output>` sibling to two buttons whose trimmed text or class matches `/^[\+\-]$/` or `/\b(plus|minus|increase|decrease|up|down)\b/i`, where the target lacks `aria-live|role=status|role=alert|<output>`. Confidence `high` (fully static). Satisfies wcag22:4.1.3.
- [ ] **V1-TRUNCATED-RESPONSE-NEXTSTEP-PAGING-HINT** When `truncated: true` and `nextOffset` is present, `nextStep` prose still reads "call suggest_fix on the top finding" — ignoring that the agent has 80 files-with-findings and paged only 25. An agent following `nextStep` proceeds to single-finding triage and never pages the rest. Fix: when `truncated: true`, prepend `nextStep` with "Response truncated — {totalFilesWithFindings} files-with-findings, {filesReturned} returned. Call scan_project with offset:{nextOffset} to continue, OR triage the top returned finding first via: …" — AND ensure `nextStepStructured` offers a paging shape (`{tool: "scan_project", args: {offset: N}}`) alongside the finding-triage shape. Doctrine: one tool call should answer "what next?" — the current response points at the second-order question while hiding the first-order pagination cost. Pairs with Q-SHARED-LIMIT-REQUEST-VS-EFFECTIVE (closed, `effectiveLimit` surface axis).
- [ ] **V1-2.4.5-SINGLE-PAGE-REASON-ENRICH** Per P1-I + doctrine, 2.4.5 Multiple Ways fires on every single-page demo (50/50 projects, hundreds of template kits) because static analysis cannot distinguish SPA shell from multi-page site. Keep surfacing (doctrine) but enrich reason-text with the dismissal signal: "document has 0 outbound `<a>` links, 0 internal-fragment anchors, and 0 breadcrumb/nav landmarks — likely single-page context; review before flagging." Pairs with V1-FP-MULTIPLE-WAYS-CITE-COUNTS (closed, cite-count addition) — that item added nav-signal counts; this item explicitly frames "0 of everything" as dismissal signal. Reason-text only — no suppression. Doctrine: enrich `reason` so agent dismisses faster without heuristic suppression.
- [ ] **V1-EMPTY-ROOT-DIV-SCRIPT-ONLY-WARNING** Canonical vanilla-JS demo shape: body contains `<div id="buttons"></div>` + `<script src="script.js"></script>` and nothing else; all interactive controls are created by the script at runtime. ra11y returns 0 findings for `sound-board`, `hoverboard`, `pokedex`, `toast-notification`; `plan.summary` reads "No automated findings." Zero-output success per doctrine — agent concludes "clean page" when the real answer is "static scan cannot evaluate runtime-generated DOM." Fix: emit structured warning `warnings: ["dynamic_content_container_detected"]` + sibling `warningsDetails.dynamic_content_container_detected: {bodyChildCount, emptyContainerIds, scriptSources}` when body has ≤3 non-script visible children AND contains an empty `<div id>` AND a sibling `<script src>`. Pairs with V1-LIVE-REGION-RUNTIME-MUTATION-CANDIDATE (runtime-mutation axis on populated DOM) — this is the never-populated-at-parse-time axis. Doctrine: zero-output success is ambiguous failure.
- [ ] **V1-PARSING-MALFORMED-TAG-SURFACE-FINDING** `</h33>` (invalid heading tag — digit-typo on `</h3>`) in a template catalog `blazer-responsive-html5-coming-soon-template/index.html:47` surfaces only as `partialParseFiles[].reason`: no rule emits a finding at the location. Structural typos that a browser's error-recovery silently absorbs can shift semantic boundaries (heading level inference, landmark scope). Fix: emit a `parsing/malformed-tag` finding at every position where the partial-parse recovery resolved a structurally-invalid element token (impossible tag names like `h33`, `h7`, `h0`; numeric-suffixed tag names outside the HTML5 inventory). Pairs with V1-HTML-PARSER-MARKDOWN-URL-AUTOLINK (closed, scheme-shaped token recovery) — that fixed the parser; this item surfaces the recovery as a structured finding so the agent can action it. Satisfies wcag21:4.1.1 (parsing failures).
- [ ] **V1-REVIEW-CANDIDATE-CROSS-FILE-REPETITION-COLLAPSE** Website-templates' 528 HTML files trigger the same 2.4.5 candidate with byte-identical reason "Likely root layout has no search/sitemap/breadcrumb" on each `index.html`; `uniquePerCriterion` doesn't help because every candidate anchors to a different file path. V1-CHECKLIST-CRITERION-CANDIDATE-DEDUPE-ACROSS-IDS covers the same-candidate-across-criteria axis; V1-CHECKLIST-VENDOR-IFRAME-OCCURRENCES-COLLAPSE covers the vendor-file same-path axis. This item is the cross-file cross-template pattern-repetition axis. Fix: when the same `(ruleId, reasonHash)` fires on N files with N > threshold (e.g. 20), collapse to one top-level entry with `{pattern, occurrences: N, sampleFiles: [top-5-paths], totalFiles: N}` and elide the rest; the agent gets the signal once + sample coverage + the total without 528 near-identical rows. Doctrine: labeled buckets honest when the label is provable — "same-reason pattern fires on N files" is a deterministic fact.


### v1.0.0 — orchestrator follow-ups

Items surfaced during the /continue orchestrator run that landed the tenth-pass batch. Not user-surfaced drift — engineering follow-ups.

- [ ] **V1-SILENT-DROP-TYPEOF-BOOLEAN-AUDIT** — the V1-SESSION-CONFIGURE-ALLOWWRITE-SILENT-DROP fix (commits 7fd25c08, ded4616e) revealed a `typeof x === "boolean"` guard that silently discarded non-boolean inputs. Audit other MCP tool-input parsers for the same pattern — any `typeof` guard that quietly drops mismatched inputs instead of emitting a structured `invalid-param` error. Candidates: `sessionConfigure.autoDetectWrappers`, `scan_project.additionalPaths` shape, `suggest_fix.maxCandidates`, `checklist.maxCandidatesPerCriterion`, any other input shape assertions in src/mcp/*.ts. Fix with the discriminated `{ok, opts} | {ok:false, error}` pattern established in src/mcp/configure-opts.ts. Per AI-first doctrine, silent drops are dishonest — the agent thinks it configured a setting that never took effect.
- [ ] **V1-ANALYSIS-COVERAGE-FILE-LIMIT-APPROACHING** — `src/mcp/analysis-coverage.ts` hit 503 effective lines during the V1-FRONTMATTER-AS-TEMPLATE-DIRECTIVE-TRIGGER + V1-TEMPLATE-CLASSIFIER-MARKDOWN-PROSE-FALSE-POSITIVE integration; integrator self-refactored to drop back under 500. The file is absorbing markdown + frontmatter + code-span + template-engine classification all at once. Split into `markdown-classifier.ts` + `frontmatter-classifier.ts` + a thinner `analysis-coverage.ts` orchestrator before the next markdown-classifier item lands, or the next addition will bounce off the limits guard again.

---



- **Implement the remaining manual-only criteria as finders.** The coverage matrix (V1-COVERAGE-MATRIX) will enumerate actual gaps — per finder review the finder list confirms most are already covered. If V1-COVERAGE-MATRIX surfaces genuine gaps, each becomes a Track R `R-<criterion>-<topic>` item, NOT a Track V item. Track V verifies; Track R implements detection.
- **Widen real-world fixtures beyond the five above.** SPA routing, internationalization, dark-mode contrast, reduced-motion — all worth fixtures, none are "find all violations" gates. Log as Track F `F-V1.x` items if the v1.0 bar rises.
- **OSSF Scorecard / npm audit workflows.** Nice-to-have for supply-chain posture; zero-dep invariant already covers most of the surface. Skip unless a real signal arrives.

## History — Phases 0–17 complete

Historical record; do not modify. The `/continue` skill does not walk this section.

- **Phase 0** — Autonomous Claude Code infrastructure (hooks, agents, skills, settings).
- **Phase 1** — Guard + generator scripts; `scripts/verify.ts` single entrypoint.
- **Phase 2** — Core types, registries, scanner skeleton, AST helpers, public API surface.
- **Phase 3** — WCAG 2.2 standard module; 86 active criteria + historical 4.1.1.
- **Phase 4** — In-house utilities (logger, assert, fs, path, glob, git, ansi, string-width, wrap, color, contrast, args, index).
- **Phase 5** — Parsers: tsx, html, css, tailwind + theme resolver + discover; unit + fuzz tests.
- **Phase 6** — Rule engine + first 5 rules (alt-text-missing, contrast/minimum, link-descriptive-text, focus/outline-visible, parsing/duplicate-id).
- **Phase 7** — Scanner + terminal/plain/json formatters + theme + snapshot tests.
- **Phase 8** — CLI: scan, list-rules, list-standards, explain, coverage, checklist, vpat, certification, init, doctor.
- **Phase 9** — WCAG 2.1 standard module (reuses rules via equivalentTo).
- **Phase 10** — Section 508 + EN 301 549 standard modules (thin equivalentTo wrappers).
- **Phase 11** — Remaining rules across contrast, focus, keyboard, aria, semantics, forms, pointer, navigation, layout, tooltip, document domains. 54 rules total.
- **Phase 12** — Alternative formatters: sarif, junit, markdown, html, agent.
- **Phase 13** — Reports: coverage, checklist, vpat, certification.
- **Phase 14** — Config, inline disables, baseline mode (create/check/update), monorepo `projects: []` support.
- **Phase 15** — Plugin API (`defineRule`, `defineStandard`, `defineFormatter`, `defineConfig`) + three example plugins + CI smoke job.
- **Phase 16** — Long-form docs: getting-started, CLI, configuration, architecture, KB (architecture + patterns + concepts + gotchas + glossary), certification guides, plugin authoring, MCP docs, ADRs 0001–0005.
- **Phase 17** — CI workflows (ci.yml, release.yml), issue + PR templates, dependabot.
- **Phase 18 partial** — README with real output snapshots + multi-standard-scan integration test + mcp-session integration test coverage audit. Remaining demo/tag/publish items live in Track D above.
- **Phase 19 partial** — In-house JSON-RPC 2.0 MCP server (~200 LoC), session state + AST cache by mtime, 10 live tools, session config for `nativeWrappers`, `.mcp.json` auto-attach, integration tests. Remaining hardening items live in Track M above.
- **Phase 22 partial** — Review finders shipped: use-of-color, error-identification, headings-and-labels, on-input-body (landed as reason-text tiers on on-input-change per CLAUDE.md §1), `tool-audit` meta-tool. Remaining finder + finder tests live in Track R.
